-q_{i}=-\mathbb{E}^{p}Q_{ip}=\tfrac{1}{2n}\rho_{j}=0
, and so from (
5.14
) there follows
\tilde{R}_{ij}=R_{[ij]}
. Since
\Pi_{ip}\,^{p}=-nq_{i}=0
, the connection
\tilde{\nabla}
induces on
|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|
the same connection as that induced by
\nabla
. This shows the existence claim of the theorem.

On the other hand, if
\tilde{\nabla}
induces on
\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M
the same connection as that induced by
\nabla
, then it must be that
-nq_{i}=\Pi_{ip}\,^{p}=0
. If moreover the symmetric part of the Ricci tensor of
\tilde{\nabla}
vanishes, then by (
5.14
) it must be that
(n-2)Q_{ij}=T_{ij}-R_{(ij)}
, and this shows the uniqueness of
\tilde{\nabla}
.

If a Lie group
G
acts on
M
by automorphisms of
\nabla
preserving
\mathbb{E}
, then
G
preserves
T_{ij}
and
R_{(ij)}
, so also preserves
Q_{ij}
and hence acts as automorphisms of
\tilde{\nabla}
.

From (
5.14
) it follows that the Ricci curvature of
\tilde{\nabla}
is
\tilde{R}_{ij}=R_{[ij]}
, so that if the initial
\nabla
has symmetric Ricci tensor, then
\tilde{\nabla}
is Ricci-flat.
∎

Remark 10
.

In the setting of Theorem
5.6
, but with the Ricci tensor not assumed symmetric, one might try to first solve the antisymmetrization of (
5.14
) to produce a conelike nonsingular radiant structure having the same planes and having symmetric Ricci tensor. The antisymmetrization of (
5.14
) gives the equation
\tilde{R}_{[ij]}=R_{[ij]}+\tfrac{n}{2}dq_{ij}
. Since the antisymmetric part of the Ricci tensor is always exact, the equation
0=R_{[ij]}+\tfrac{n}{2}dq_{ij}
by itself always admits a solution. However, Example
35
exhibits a conelike radiant structure for which there is no solution with
q_{i}
satisfying also
\mathbb{E}^{p}q_{p}=0
. On the other hand, in Example
35
the conelike radiant structure already has antisymmetric Ricci tensor, so it does not show the conclusion of Theorem
5.6
is unattainable when the Ricci tensor is not assumed symmetric.

Corollary 5.7
.

On a manifold of dimension
n>2
, if
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},\Psi)
is an equiaffine nonsingular radiant structure such that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is conelike, then there is a unique
\mathbb{E}
-invariant connection
\tilde{\nabla}
such that
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E},\Psi)
is an equiaffine nonsingular radiant structure and
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is conelike, Ricci-flat, and has the same planes as
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
.

Proof.

An equiaffine radiant structure has symmetric Ricci tensor, so the claim follows from Theorem
5.6
.
∎

Said another way, there is a unique Ricci-flat
\mathbb{E}
-invariant conelike equiaffine radiant structure having the same planes as a given conelike equiaffine radiant structure.

A smooth map between foliated manifolds is
foliated
if its differential maps the tangent bundle of the foliation of the domain manifold into the tangent bundle of the foliation of the target manifold. If
X
is a nonsingular vector field on
\mathcal{F}
a smooth submersion
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
is
foliated
if it is foliated as a map between
\mathcal{F}
with the foliation by the orbits of
X
and
M
with the trivial foliation.

Example 17
.

The projection,
\rho:\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{P}^{+}(\mathbb{V})
, to the oriented projectivization of
\mathbb{V}
and the corresponding induced projection
\rho:\mathscr{H}^{n}(\lambda)\to\mathbb{P}^{+}(\mathbb{V})
are both foliated submersions. The fibers of the former are copies of
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, while those the latter are copies of
S^{1}
.

Definition 5.8
.

(1)

A nonsingular radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
on an
(n+1)
-dimensional manifold
\mathcal{F}

fibers
over a projective structure
[\nabla]
on an
n
-manifold
M
if there is a surjective smooth submersion
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
such that the image in
M
of each geodesic of
\widehat{\nabla}
is contained in a projective geodesic of
[\nabla]
.

(2)

A nonsingular conelike radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
on an
(n+1)
-dimensional manifold
\mathcal{F}

fibers
over a projective structure
[\nabla]
on an
n
-manifold
M
if there is a surjective smooth submersion
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
such that the image in
M
of each planelike surface is contained in a projective geodesic of
[\nabla]
.

Example 18
.

The standard flat affine connection
\nabla
on
\mathbb{V}
gives a conelike equiaffine radiant structure
(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\},\nabla,\Psi,\mathbb{E})
fibering over the standard flat projective structure on
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{V})
.

Example 19
.

Let
(\mathbb{V},D,\mathbb{E},\Psi)
be the standard equiaffine radiant structure on the
(n+1)
-dimensional vector space
\mathbb{V}
defined in Example
9
.
This example exhibits two Ricci-flat conelike nonsingular radiant structures
(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\},D,\mathbb{E})
and
(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\},\nabla,\mathbb{E})
that have the same planes and both fiber over the standard projective structure on
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{V})
. This shows the
\mathbb{E}
-invariance hypothesis in Theorem
5.6
is necessary for the validity of the uniqueness claim.

Let
h_{ij}
be a
Euclidean metric
on
\mathbb{V}
, meaning a
D
-parallel Riemannian metric. Let
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}h_{ip}
and
\beta_{i}=|\mathbb{E}|^{-2}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=d\log|\mathbb{E}|_{i}
so that
\beta_{i}\mathbb{E}^{i}=1
. Using
D_{i}|\mathbb{E}|^{\alpha}=\alpha|\mathbb{E}|^{\alpha}\beta_{i}
with
\alpha=2
yields
D_{i}\beta_{j}=|\mathbb{E}|^{-2}h_{ij}-2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}
. Antisymmetrizing this relation yields
d\beta_{ij}=0
. Contracting it with
\mathbb{E}^{i}
yields
\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{p}\beta_{j}=-\beta_{j}
, so
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\beta)_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}d\beta_{pi}=\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{p}\beta_{i}-\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{i}\beta_{p}=-\beta_{i}+\beta_{i}=0
. In particular,
\beta_{i}
can be viewed as a flat principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-connection on the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
bundle
\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{V})
.

Let
\sigma_{i}
be a smooth one-form on
\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\sigma_{p}\mathbb{E}^{p}=0
.
Define
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}=Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{k}-2\sigma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
where

(5.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}Q_{ij}&=|\mathbb{E}|^{\alpha}(D_{i}\beta_{j}+\beta_{i}\beta_{j})+2\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}=|\mathbb{E}|^{\alpha-1}D_{i}D_{j}|\mathbb{E}|+2\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}=|\mathbb{E}|^{\alpha-4}\left(|\mathbb{E}|^{2}h_{ij}-\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}\right)+2\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)},\end{split}

for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
.
Since
Q_{[ij]}=0
, the affine connection
\nabla=D+\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}
is torsion-free.
As
\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{p}\beta_{i}=-\beta_{i}
, there holds
\mathbb{E}^{p}Q_{ip}=\sigma_{i}
, so
\Pi_{ip}\,^{k}\mathbb{E}^{p}=0
and
\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{j}=\delta_{i}\,^{j}
, and
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure.

Because
\Pi_{ip}\,^{p}=-n\sigma_{i}
,
\Psi
is
\nabla
-parallel if and only if
\sigma_{i}=0
. In this case
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},\Psi)
is an equiaffine radiant structure on
\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}
.
Let
R_{ijk}\,^{l}
be the curvature of
\nabla
. By construction,
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}Q)_{ij}=\alpha Q_{ij}-2\alpha\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}+2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\beta_{j)}
, so, by Lemma
5.5
,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}R_{ij}&=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}Q)_{ij}+(n-1)(Q_{ij}+\nabla_{i}\sigma_{j}+\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j})+d\sigma_{ij}\\
&=(n-1+\alpha)Q_{ij}-2\alpha\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}+2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\beta_{j)}+(n-1)(D_{i}\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j})+d\sigma_{ij},\\
\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pij}\,^{k}&=\left(\alpha Q_{ij}-2\alpha\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}+2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\beta_{j)}\right)\mathbb{E}^{k}-2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}.\end{split}

Because
\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{p}\sigma_{i}=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i}-\sigma_{i}
and
\mathbb{E}^{p}d\sigma_{pi}=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i}
,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\rho_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pi}=(n-1+\alpha)\sigma_{i}+(n-1)\mathbb{E}^{p}D_{p}\sigma_{i}-\alpha\sigma_{i}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i}+\mathbb{E}^{p}d\sigma_{pi}=(n+1)(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i},

as follows also from Lemma
5.5
.
The tensor called
Q_{ij}
in Lemma
5.2
corresponds with the tensor
\alpha Q_{ij}-2\alpha\sigma_{(i}\beta_{j)}+2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\beta_{j)}
in the present example, and to apply Lemma
5.2
to conclude that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is conelike it has to be checked that the contraction of this tensor with
\mathbb{E}^{p}
equals
\tfrac{1}{n+1}\rho_{i}
. This follows from (
5.17
), for

(5.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{p}\left(\alpha Q_{ip}-2\alpha\sigma_{(i}\beta_{p)}+2(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{(i}\beta_{p)}\right)=\alpha\sigma_{i}-\alpha\sigma_{i}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i}=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\sigma)_{i}=\tfrac{1}{n+1}\rho_{i}.

Hence, by Lemma
5.2
,
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is conelike.

By Lemma
5.4
, the planelike surfaces of the conelike radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
on
\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}
are the two-dimensional subspaces of
\mathbb{V}
. This can be seen directly as follows. Any two-dimensional subspace
\Sigma\subset\mathbb{V}
is spanned by
\mathbb{E}
and a constant vector field
v^{i}\in\mathbb{V}
. By definition of
\nabla
,
\nabla_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}
,
\nabla_{\mathbb{E}}v=0
,
\nabla_{v}\mathbb{E}=v
, and
\nabla_{v}v=Q(v,v)\mathbb{E}-2\sigma(v)v
, so that
\Sigma_{0}=\Sigma\cap(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\})
is
\nabla
-totally geodesic. Given
p\in\mathbb{V}
and a two-dimensional subspace
L\subset T_{p}\mathbb{V}
containing
\mathbb{E}_{p}
there is
v\in T_{p}\mathbb{V}
such that
L
is spanned by
\mathbb{E}_{p}
and
v
. The constant vector field obtained by
D
-parallel transporting
v
, also denoted
v
, spans with
\mathbb{E}
a two-dimensional subspace
\Sigma_{0}\subset\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}
tangent at
p
to
L
and, by the preceding, totally geodesic.

Suppose
\alpha=1-n
and
\sigma_{i}=0
.
In this case, by the preceding and (
5.16
),
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is Ricci-flat and conelike, but
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\nabla
does not vanish and
\nabla
is not
\mathbb{E}
-invariant. The Ricci-flat conelike nonsingular radiant structures
(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\},D,\mathbb{E})
and
(\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\},\nabla,\mathbb{E})
have the same planes and fiber over the standard projective structure on
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{V})
. This shows that the uniqueness statement in Theorem
5.6
is false without the assumption of the
\mathbb{E}
-invariance of the connection.

Although in this case
\nabla
and
D
have the same planes, their geodesics behave quite differently. This is described in more detail than is strictly necessary because it is interesting in its own right and because it suggests that
\nabla
is interesting rather than pathological.
In
D
-affine coordinates
x^{0},\dots,x^{n}
, the equations for a
\nabla
-geodesic are

(5.19)

\displaystyle 0=\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{x}=\ddot{x}+|x|^{-n-3}\left(|\dot{x}|^{2}|x|^{2}-\langle x,\dot{x}\rangle^{2}\right)x.

The analysis of (
5.19
) reduces to the classical problem of motion in a central force field with a power law potential.
Define
r(t)=|x(t)|
.
Differentiating
|\dot{x}|^{2}|x|^{2}-\langle x,\dot{x}\rangle^{2}
using (
5.19
) shows that there is
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|\dot{x}|^{2}|x|^{2}-\langle x,\dot{x}\rangle^{2}=c^{2}
, while differentiating and using (
5.19
) shows that the energy

(5.20)

\displaystyle E(x)=\tfrac{1}{2}|\dot{x}|^{2}-\tfrac{c^{2}}{n+1}|x|^{-n-1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\dot{r}^{2}+\tfrac{c^{2}}{2}r^{-2}-\tfrac{c^{2}}{n+1}r^{-n-1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\dot{r}^{2}+\tfrac{c^{2}}{2}r^{-2}+U(r)=\tfrac{1}{2}\dot{r}^{2}+V(r),

is constant along a solution, where
U(r)=-\tfrac{c^{2}}{n+1}r^{-n-1}
and
V(r)=U(r)+\tfrac{c^{2}}{2}r^{-2}
. The preceding shows that a solution of (
5.19
) can be interpreted as a motion in a central force field with the potential energy
U(r)
and effective potential energy
V(r)
. The latter is so named because differentiating (
5.20
) shows
\ddot{r}=c^{2}(r^{-3}-r^{-n-2})=-\tfrac{\partial V}{\partial r}
. A nonrigorous qualitative discussion of the behavior of solutions to this problem is given in standard mechanics textbooks such as
[
135
, Chapter
4
]
,
[
67
, Chapter
3
]
, or
[
94
, Section
14
]
.

If
c=0
then (
5.19
) shows
\ddot{x}=0
, so the radial lines
x(t)=ta
,
a\in\mathbb{V}\setminus\{0\}
, are
\nabla
-geodesics. If
c\neq 0
then, replacing
x(t)
by the reparametrization
x(|c|^{-1}t)
, it can be and is assumed hereforth that
c^{2}=1
. In this case
x(t)
and
\dot{x}(t)
are linearly independent for all
t
. From (
5.19
) it follows that the two-form
x\wedge\dot{x}
is constant in time, so there is an
h
-orthonormal basis
\{a_{1},a_{2}\}
of
\text{Span}\,\{x(0),\dot{x}(0)\}
such that
x(t)=f_{1}(t)a_{1}+f_{2}(t)a_{2}
for some functions
f_{1}(t)
and
f_{2}(t)
that, by (
5.19
), satisfy the differential equations
\ddot{f}_{i}+(f_{1}^{2}+f_{2}^{2})^{-(n+3)/2}f_{i}=0
,
i=1,2
. Because
x\wedge\dot{x}=(f_{1}\dot{f}_{2}-\dot{f}_{1}f_{2})a_{1}\wedge a_{2}
,
f_{1}\dot{f}_{2}-\dot{f}_{1}f_{2}
equals
\pm c=\pm 1
. Because the order of
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
is arbitrary, it can be and is assumed that
f_{1}\dot{f}_{2}-\dot{f}_{1}f_{2}=1
. (The same conclusion follows more conceptually from the observation that, by construction, two-dimensional subspaces are totally geodesic for
\nabla
.) Taking
f_{1}(t)=\cos{t}
and
f_{2}(t)=\sin{t}
shows that the unit radius circles centered on the origin,
x=\cos(t)a+\sin(t)b
, are
\nabla
-geodesics.
Writing
f_{1}=r\cos\theta
and
f_{2}=r\sin\theta
and differentiating shows that
\dot{\theta}r^{2}=f_{1}\dot{f}_{2}-\dot{f}_{1}f_{2}=1
, which shows that the constancy of
c^{2}
can be regarded as conservation of angular momentum.

On the domain
r>0
, the effective potential
V(r)=\tfrac{1}{2}r^{-2}-\tfrac{1}{n+1}r^{-n-1}
has a unique maximum at
r=1
, with value
V(1)=\tfrac{n-1}{2(n+1)}
. In qualitative terms, the behavior of a solution with energy
E_{0}
is determined by the relation of the initial value
r_{0}=r(0)
with the roots of the equation
E_{0}=V(r)
, for, because
\dot{r}^{2}=2(E_{0}-V(r))
, the latter correspond to turning points of
r(t)
.

Consider a solution with initial conditions
r(0)=r_{0}
and
\dot{r}(0)=\dot{r}_{0}
. These are related to the initial position
x(0)
and velocity
\dot{x}(0)
by
r_{0}=|x(0)|
and
\dot{r}_{0}^{2}+r_{0}^{-2}=|\dot{x}(0)|^{2}
. By (
5.20
), the energy
E_{0}
is given by
2E_{0}=\dot{r}_{0}^{2}+2V(r_{0})
. If
r_{0}=1
and
E_{0}
equals the maximal value
\tfrac{n-1}{2(n+1)}=V(1)
of
V(r)
, then
\dot{r}(0)^{2}=2(E_{0}-V(r_{0}))=0
. The unique solution of
\ddot{r}=r^{-3}-r^{-n-2}
with initial conditions
r(0)=1
and
\dot{r}(0)=0
is
r(t)=1
. This yields the circular solutions
x=\cos(t)a+\sin(t)b
already mentioned. With the same energy, so that
\dot{r}_{0}=0
, if
r_{0}<1
, then from
\ddot{r}=r^{-3}-r^{-n-2}
it follows that
\ddot{r}<0
, so
\dot{r}<0
as well for
t>0
, and both
\dot{r}
and
r
decrease for
t>0
. Hence
\dot{r}=-(2(E_{0}-V(r))^{1/2}
and so
r(t)\to 0
as
t\to T
for
T=-\int_{r_{0}}^{0}(2(E_{0}+\tfrac{1}{n+1}s^{-n-1}-s^{-2})^{-1/2}\,ds
. If instead
r_{0}>1
, then the same reasoning shows that
r
and
\dot{r}
are increasing for
t>0
, and
r(t)\to\infty
as
t\to\infty
. If
E_{0}>V(1)
, then
\dot{r}(t)\neq 0
for all valid
t
, and
r(t)
increases or decreases. If
E_{0}<V(1)
, then there are
r_{\min}<1<r_{\max}
such that
V(r_{\min})=E_{0}=V(r_{\max})
and the behavior of
r(t)
depends on the relation of
r_{0}
to the interval
[r_{\min},r_{\max}]
. If
r_{0}<r_{\min}
,
r(t)
tends to
0
in finite time, if
r_{0}>r_{\max}
, then
r(t)
tends to
\infty
as
t\to\infty
, while if
r_{0}\in[r_{\min},r_{\max}]
then
r(t)\in[r_{\min},r_{\max}]
for
t>0
, so the solution stays bounded, although it is not closed. More precise description of the phase curve can also be obtained by writing
u=r^{-1}
(as in
[
135
]
) and using
\tfrac{d}{dt}=r^{-2}\tfrac{d}{d\theta}=u^{2}\tfrac{d}{d\theta}
to rewrite
\ddot{r}=r^{-3}-r^{-n-2}
to obtain
\tfrac{d^{2}u}{d\theta^{2}}=u^{n}-u=u(u-1)(u^{n-2}+u^{n-1}+\dots+u+1)
, which describes the solution in polar coordinates. The corresponding expression for the energy is
(\tfrac{du}{d\theta})^{2}=\tfrac{2}{n+1}u^{n+1}-u^{2}+2E_{0}
.
More detailed discussion of the solutions is omitted, but the preceding suffices to show that the geodesics of
\nabla
behave quite differently than those of
D
(which are all unbounded, being lines).

Independent of the considerations of this section, it seems interesting that there is a family of affine connections with nice properties (e.g. Ricci-flat) and whose geodesics are motions in a central field with power law potential. The author has not found this in the literature.

6.
Extended projective structures and the associated cone connections

This section introduces the notion of
extended projective structure
, which is a projective structure coupled to a class of principal connections on a principal bundle
\rho:N\to M
with one-dimensional structure group, and establishes one of the main results of the paper, Theorem
6.2
, that shows that there is a canonical correspondence between extended projective structures on
M
and certain conelike radiant structures on
N
.

It is convenient to recall some definitions.
For a principal
G
-bundle
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
,
\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
denotes the space of principal
G
-connections on
\mathcal{F}
.
By a principal bundle
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
with one-dimensional structure group is meant either a principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle or a principal
S^{1}
-bundle. In either case the Lie algebra of the structure group is identified with
\mathbb{R}
. A principal
\mathbb{R}^{+}
-bundle is regarded as a principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle via the identity inclusion of the structure groups.
Because the structure group is abelian, a connection
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
is by definition a one-form
\beta\in\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!1}}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{F})
preserved by the principal action and such that
\beta(\mathbb{X})=1
, in which
\mathbb{X}
is the fundamental vector field generating the principal action
R_{e^{t}}
. Since the principal action preserves
\beta
there holds
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{X}}\beta=0
, and so also
i(\mathbb{X})d\beta=0
; that is
d\beta
is horizontal and there is a closed two-form
\omega\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
, the
curvature
of
\beta
, such that
d\beta=\rho^{\ast}(\omega)
.
A connection
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
determines and is determined by the homogeneous horizontal subbundle
H=\ker\beta\subset T\mathcal{F}
, where
horizontal
means that
\ker T\rho(u)\cap H_{u}=\{0\}
and
T\rho(u)(H_{u})=T_{\rho(u)}M
for all
u\in\mathcal{F}
, and
homogeneous
means that
H
is preserved by the differential
TR_{r}
of the principal action. The horizontal lift
X\in\Gamma(TM)\to\hat{X}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{F})
determined
\beta
is the unique section of
\Gamma(H)
covering
X
. The uniqueness implies that the map
X\to\hat{X}
is a
C^{\infty}(M)
-module map, where
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
acts on
\Gamma(T\mathcal{F})
by multiplication by
\rho^{\ast}(f)
. The difference of
\tilde{\beta}-\beta
of
\tilde{\beta},\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
is a homogeneity
0
one-form annihilating the vertical so has the form
\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
for some
\gamma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
. The
\tilde{\beta}
horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
is expressible in terms of its
\beta
-horizontal lift
\hat{X}
as
\hat{X}-\gamma(X)\mathbb{X}
.

By (
2.4
) the antisymmetric parts of the Ricci tensors of projectively equivalent connections are cohomologous two-forms. The resulting cohomology class is trivial because the antisymmetric part of the Ricci tensor of a torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
is exact, for if
\mu
is any volume density, then
\nabla_{i}\mu=\sigma_{i}\mu
for some one-form
\sigma
, and
2R_{[ij]}\mu=-R_{ijp}\,^{p}\mu=2\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}\mu=2\nabla_{[i}\sigma_{j]}\mu=d\sigma_{ij}\mu
, so
2R_{[ij]}=d\sigma_{ij}
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle. Since any two elements of
\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
differ by the pullback via
\rho
of a one-form on
M
, their curvatures determine a cohomology class
[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;\mathbb{R})
. (When the structure group is
S^{1}
, the cohomology class
\tfrac{1}{2\pi}[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
is integral, equal to the first Chern class of the bundle
[
38
,
86
]
.)

The idea behind the definition of extended projective structure is to link representatives
\nabla\in[\nabla]
and
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:M\to N)
in such a way that a two-form representing the cohomology class
[\omega]
can be associated with the linked pair.

A projective structure
[\nabla]
is a torsor for the abelian additive group
\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
with
\gamma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
acting on
\nabla\in[\nabla]
by
\gamma\cdot\nabla=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
. The space
\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
of principal connections on
\rho:N\to M
is also a torsor for
\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
, with
\gamma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
acting on
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
by
\gamma\cdot\beta=\beta-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
. It follows that
\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
acts on
[\nabla]\times\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
by
\gamma\cdot(\nabla,\beta)=(\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k},\beta-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma))
. The orbit of
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla]\times\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
under this action is denoted
[\nabla,\beta]
and is called an
extended projective structure
on
\rho:N\to M
. Pairs
(\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{\beta})
and
(\nabla,\beta)
are
projectively equivalent
if there is
\gamma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
such that
\gamma\cdot(\nabla,\beta)=(\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{\beta})
.

An extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\rho:N\to M
determines an underlying projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
and associates with each representative connection
\nabla\in[\nabla]
a principal connection
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
. The different possible extended projective structures
[\nabla,\beta]
determining a given projective structure
[\nabla]
can be parametrized by the choice of
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
, in the sense that if
[\nabla,\beta]
is an extended projective structure then so is
[\nabla,\beta+\rho^{\ast}(\sigma)]
for any
\sigma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
, but there is no canonical choice of
\beta
unless
\rho:N\to M
has some additional structure (in particular is somehow determined by the smooth structure on
M
, as is the case, for example, when
N
is taken to be
\mathcal{V}
). More succinctly, the space of extended projective structures on
\rho:N\to M
is a torsor for
\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle over the
n
-manifold
M
.
Given an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\rho:N\to M
, the two-form

(6.1)

\displaystyle\eta_{ij}=\omega_{ij}-\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}=\omega_{ij}+2P_{[ij]}.

associated with a representative
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla,\beta]
does not depend on the choice of
(\nabla,\beta)
, so determines a canonical representative of the cohomology class
[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;\mathbb{R})
determined by
\rho:N\to M
.

Proof.

By (
2.4
), the two-form
\eta
defined in (
6.1
) does not depend on the choice of
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla,\beta]
. Because the antisymmetric Ricci tensor
R_{[ij]}
of
\nabla
is exact,
\eta
represents the cohomology class
[\omega]
.
∎

The two-form
\eta
associated with the extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
by (
6.1
) is the the
associated two-form
of
[\nabla,\beta]
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
[\nabla,\beta]
be an extended projective structure on
\rho:N\to M
with associated two-form
\eta
.

(1)

There is a unique torsion-free affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
N
having antisymmetric Ricci tensor and inducing on
M
the given extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
and so that
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is a conelike radiant structure invariant under the principal action (and so
\mathbb{E}
-invariant) and fibering over
(M,[\nabla])
.

For any
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla,\beta]
, the connection
\widehat{\nabla}
is given by

(6.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}=\widehat{\nabla_{X}Y}+\rho^{\ast}(Q(X,Y))\mathbb{E},

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\mathbb{E}=\hat{X}=\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\hat{X},

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}.

where
\hat{X}\in\Gamma(TN)
is the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
,
\omega\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
is the curvature of
\beta
, and
Q\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
is defined by

(6.3)

\displaystyle Q_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{1-n}R_{(ij)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{ij}=P_{(ij)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{ij}=P_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{n+1}R_{[ij]}-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{ij}=P_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{ij},

where
R_{ij}
and
P_{ij}
are the Ricci tensor and projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
, and
\widehat{\nabla}
satisfies

(6.4)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}+\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=-\rho^{\ast}(Q)_{IJ},

\displaystyle 2Q_{[ij]}=-\omega_{ij}.

Moreover,
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
has the following properties:

(a)

The planelike surfaces of
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
are the
\rho
-preimages of the projective geodesics of
M
and the base curve
\gamma=\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}:I\to M
of a parametrized curve
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
is a projective parametrization of a projective geodesic of
[\nabla]
if and only if
\hat{\gamma}
is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic.

(b)

The Ricci curvature of
\widehat{\nabla}
equals
-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)
.

(c)

For any
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla,\beta]
, the curvature tensor
\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L}
of
\widehat{\nabla}
satisfies

(6.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{E}^{I}\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L}=0=\mathbb{E}^{K}\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L},\qquad\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{A}\beta_{A}=\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ},&\\
&\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}=B_{ijk}\,^{l}-\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{j]k}+\delta_{k}\,^{l}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{ij}=R_{ijk}\,^{l}+\delta_{i}\,^{l}P_{(jk)}-\delta_{j}\,^{l}P_{(ik)}-\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\omega_{j]k}+\delta_{k}\,^{l}\omega_{ij},&\end{aligned}

where
B_{ijk}\,^{l}
and
C_{ijk}
are the projective Weyl and projective Cotton tensors of
\nabla
and the splitting of
TN
determined by
\beta
is used in writing
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}
.

(2)

Any conelike radiant structure
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
on
N
invariant under the principal action on
\rho:N\to M
induces on
\rho:N\to M
an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
for which it fibers over the underlying projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
and has the same planelike surfaces as the associated Ricci antisymmetric, principal action invariant, conelike radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
associated with
[\nabla,\beta]
as in (
1
).

Definition 6.3
.

The connection
\widehat{\nabla}
associated with the extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
by Theorem
6.2
is the
cone connection determined by
[\nabla,\beta]
.

Remark 11
.

If
\omega_{ij}=\omega_{[ij]}
is an antisymmetric two-form on a two-dimensional vector space, then
2\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\omega_{j]k}=-\omega_{ij}\delta_{k}\,^{l}
.
To see this, let
X
and
Y
span the vector space and let
Z=aX+bY
. Then
2X^{i}Y^{j}Z^{k}\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\omega_{j]k}=\omega_{jk}Y^{j}Z^{k}X^{l}-\omega_{ik}X^{i}Z^{k}Y^{l}=a\omega_{jk}Y^{j}X^{k}X^{l}-b\omega_{ik}X^{i}Y^{k}Y^{l}=-\omega_{ij}X^{i}Y^{j}Z^{l}
.
Consequently, when
n=2
, the last identity of (
6.5
) simplifies to
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}=\tfrac{3}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{ij}\delta_{k}\,^{l}=-\hat{R}_{ij}\delta_{k}\,^{l}
.

The remainder of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
6.2
. This is organized as follows:

•

Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
shows that a radiant structure on
N
induces an extended projective structure on
M
and that conelike radiant structures with the same planelike surfaces induce the same extended projective structure.

•

Lemmas
6.6
and
6.8
define the cone connection of a pair
(\nabla,\beta)
and show that it depends only on the extended projective structure generated by
(\nabla,\beta)
.

•

Lemmas
6.11
and
6.12
show that the cone connection of an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
fibers over the underlying projective structure
[\nabla]
.

The section concludes with the proof of Theorem
6.2
and some remarks.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. A torsion-free affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
N
invariant under the principal action and constituting with
\mathbb{E}
a radiant structure determines on
M
an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
. More precisely:

(1)

For each
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
with curvature
\omega
there is a torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
on
M

satisfying (
6.2
), where
\hat{X}\in\Gamma(TN)
is the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, and
Q\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
satisfies (
6.4
).

(2)

The connections
\tilde{\nabla}
and
\nabla
determined by
\widehat{\nabla}
together with the principal connections
\tilde{\beta}=\beta-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
and
\beta
as in (
1
) satisfy
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
, so
(\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{\beta})
and
(\nabla,\beta)
are projectively equivalent.

Proof.

For
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
, let
\hat{X}
be the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
. Because
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is radiant and
[\mathbb{E},\hat{X}]=0
there hold
\hat{X}=\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\mathbb{E}=\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\hat{X}
and
\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}
. Because
\widehat{\nabla}
is invariant under the principal action, the vector field
\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}
is likewise invariant, so there are
\nabla_{X}Y\in\Gamma(TM)
and
Q(X,Y)\in C^{\infty}(M)
such that
\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}=\widehat{\nabla_{X}Y}+\rho^{\ast}(Q(X,Y))\mathbb{E}
. The expression
\nabla_{X}Y
is a
C^{\infty}(M)
-module map in the first argument, is linear in the second argument, and satisfies a Leibiz rule in the second argument, and the expression
Q(X,Y)
is a
C^{\infty}(M)
-module map in both arguments, so
\nabla
is an affine connection on
M
,
Q\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
, and there holds (
6.2
). Straightforward computation using (
6.2
) shows the first identity of (
6.4
). Antisymmetrizing the first identity of (
6.4
) shows the second identity of (
6.4
).
This shows (
1
).

Because the
\tilde{\beta}=\beta-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
horizontal lift of
X
is
\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}(\gamma(X))\mathbb{E}
, the connection
\tilde{\nabla}
determined by
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\tilde{\beta}
as in (
6.2
) is related to
\nabla
by
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
. This shows
\widehat{\nabla}
determines on
M
an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
. This shows (
2
).
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. If
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\hat{D}
are torsion-free affine connections on
N
that are invariant under the principal action and constitute with
\mathbb{E}
conelike radiant structures such that
\rho(\widehat{\nabla})=0
and
\rho(\hat{D})=0
, then
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\hat{D}
induce the same extended projective structure on
\rho:N\to M
.

Proof.

Because
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\hat{D}
induce on
|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}N|
the same connection, by Lemma
5.4
there is
\hat{T}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}N)
such that
\widehat{\nabla}-\hat{D}=\hat{T}_{IJ}\mathbb{E}\,^{K}
and
\mathbb{E}^{P}\hat{T}_{PI}=0
. By the invariance of
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\hat{D}
under the principal action there is
T\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M)
such that
\hat{T}=\rho^{\ast}(T)
, so that
\widehat{\nabla}-\hat{D}=\rho^{\ast}(T)_{IJ}\mathbb{E}\,^{K}
. For
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
let
\nabla
and
D
be the connection induced on
M
as in Lemma
6.4
. For
X,Y\in\Gamma(TM)
,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla_{X}Y}=\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}-\beta(\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y})\mathbb{E}=\hat{D}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}+T(X,Y)\mathbb{E}-\beta(\hat{D}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}+T(X,Y)\mathbb{E})=\widehat{D_{X}Y}.

which proves the claim.
∎

Given a principal bundle
\rho:N\to M
with one-dimensional structure group, a tensor
Q\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
is
compatible
with
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
if
2Q_{[ij]}=-\omega_{ij}
where
\omega
is the curvature of
\beta
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
be a principal connection having curvature
\omega_{ij}\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
and let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
.

(1)

For any
Q_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
compatible with
\beta
,
the affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
N
defined by (
6.2
), where
\hat{X}\in\Gamma(TN)
is the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, is torsion-free and forms with
\mathbb{E}
a conelike radiant structure invariant under the principal action (so
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{\nabla}=0
).

(2)

Replacing
Q_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
in (
6.2
) by any
\tilde{Q}_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
compatible with
\beta
determines a conelike radiant structure having the same planelike surfaces as has that determined by
Q_{ij}
.

(3)

There is a unique choice of
Q_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
compatible with
\beta
such that the resulting conelike radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
has antisymmetric Ricci tensor. It is given by (
6.3
), where
R_{ij}
and
P_{ij}
are the Ricci tensor and projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
. In this case the curvature tensor
\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L}
and Ricci tensor
\widehat{\mathsf{Ric}}_{IJ}
of
\widehat{\nabla}
satisfy

(6.7)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}=B_{ijk}\,^{l}-\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{j]k}+\delta_{k}\,^{l}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{ij},&\\
&\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{A}\beta_{A}=\rho^{\ast}(C)_{ijk}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{kij},&\widehat{\mathsf{Ric}}_{IJ}=-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{IJ},\end{aligned}

where
B_{ijk}\,^{l}
and
C_{ijk}
are the projective Weyl and projective Cotton tensors of
\nabla
,
\eta_{ij}=\omega_{ij}-\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}=\omega_{ij}+2P_{[ij]}
, and the splitting of
TN
determined by
\beta
is used in writing
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}
.

Proof.

Uppercase Latin indices indicate tensors on
N
, while lowercase Latin indices indicate tensors on
M
.
The principal connection
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
can be viewed as a one-form satisfying
\beta_{Q}\mathbb{E}^{Q}=1
and
\mathbb{E}^{Q}d\beta_{QI}=0
. Fixing
\beta
determines a splitting
TN\simeq\text{Span}\,\{\mathbb{E}\}\oplus\ker\beta
and so it makes sense to decorate sections of tensor powers of
\ker\beta
and its dual with lowercase Latin indices, and to indicate sections of tensor powers of
\text{Span}\,\{\mathbb{E}\}
its dual with the single index
\infty
. Let
\omega_{ij}
be the curvature two-form of
\beta
defined by
\rho^{\ast}(\omega)=d\beta
. For
X\in\Gamma(TM)
let
\hat{X}
denote the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X
.

Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free connection on
M
. Given an arbitrary
Q_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
, define an affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
N
associated with the triple
(\nabla,Q,\beta)
by requiring that it satisfy the identities (
6.2
). Because
[\hat{X},\hat{Y}]=\widehat{[X,Y]}-d\beta(X,Y)\mathbb{E}
, the connection
\widehat{\nabla}
is torsion-free if and only if
Q
is compatible with
\beta
, and this is assumed in what follows. In this case,
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure on
N
.

Let
R_{ijk}\,^{l}
and
\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L}
be the curvature tensors of
\nabla
and
\widehat{\nabla}
. Using the splitting of
TN
given by the span of
\mathbb{E}
and the kernel of
\beta
, it makes sense to write
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}
for
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{L}-\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{P}\beta_{P}\mathbb{E}^{L}
. It follows from (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) that
\mathbb{E}^{P}\hat{R}_{PIJ}\,^{K}=0
. Since
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\mathbb{E}^{J}=0
this implies
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{\nabla}=0
. From the construction (
6.2
) there is apparent the validity of the stronger statement that the principal action on
N
is by automorphisms of
\widehat{\nabla}
. By Lemma
5.2
, that
\mathbb{E}^{P}\hat{R}_{PIJ}\,^{K}=0
implies that
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is conelike. This shows (
1
).

By Lemma
5.4
changing the choice of
Q
, subject to the requirement of compatibility with
\beta
, determines a conelike radiant structure having the same planelike surfaces as has
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
. This shows (
2
).

Routine computations show that the nonvanishing components of the curvature of
\widehat{\nabla}
and its traces are

(6.8)

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}=R_{ijk}\,^{l}+2\delta_{[i}\,^{l}Q_{j]k}+\omega_{ij}\delta_{k}\,^{l},

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{P}\beta_{P}=2\nabla_{[i}Q_{j]k},

(6.9)

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{ij}=R_{ij}+(n-1)Q_{ij}-\omega_{ij},

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{[ij]}=R_{[ij]}-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\omega_{ij}.

in which the notations indicating pullback by
\rho
have been omitted for readability.
It is evident from (
6.9
) that in general the skew-part of the Ricci tensor of
\widehat{\nabla}
need not vanish. By (
6.9
), requiring that the symmetric part of the Ricci tensor of
\widehat{\nabla}
vanish determines
Q_{ij}
uniquely as in (
6.3
). In this case

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}&=B_{ijk}\,^{l}+\tfrac{1}{n+1}\left(\delta_{i}\,^{l}R_{[jk]}-\delta_{j}\,^{l}R_{[ik]}-2\delta_{k}\,^{l}R_{[ij]}\right)-\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\omega_{j]k}+\omega_{ij}\delta_{k}\,^{l}\\
&=B_{ijk}\,^{l}-\delta_{[i}\,^{l}\eta_{j]k}+\delta_{k}\,^{l}\eta_{ij},\\
\hat{R}_{ijk}\,^{P}\beta_{P}&=C_{ijk}-\tfrac{1}{n+1}\nabla_{k}R_{[ij]}+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{k}\omega_{ij}=C_{ijk}+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{k}\eta_{ij},\\
\hat{R}_{ij}&=R_{[ij]}-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\omega_{ij}=-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\eta_{ij}.\end{split}

This shows (
6.7
) and completes the proof of (
3
).
∎

Definition 6.7
.

The unique connection
\widehat{\nabla}
associated with the pair
(\nabla,\beta)
and having antisymmetric Ricci tensor as in (
3
) of Lemma
6.6
is the
cone connection
determined by
\nabla
and
\beta
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on the
n
-manifold
M
, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. The cone connections of any two pairs
(\nabla,\beta)
and
(\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{\beta})
representing the same extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\rho:N\to M
are the same, so it makes sense to speak of the
cone connection of
[\nabla,\beta]
.

Proof.

For
\gamma\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
, straightforward computations using the definition of the cone connection, (
6.3
), (
6.2
), the remark that the horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
with respect to
\beta+\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
is
\hat{X}-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma(X))\mathbb{E}
, and the remark that the projective Schouten tensors
\tilde{P}_{ij}
and
P_{ij}
of
\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
and
\nabla
are related by
\tilde{P}_{ij}=P_{ij}+\nabla_{i}\gamma_{j}-\gamma_{i}\gamma_{j}
show that the cone connections of
(\nabla,\beta+\rho^{\ast}(\gamma))
and
(\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k},\beta)
both equal

(6.11)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}+2\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{k}-2\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)_{(I}\beta_{J)}\mathbb{E}^{K}+\left(\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\gamma)_{(IJ)}-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)_{I}\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)_{J}\right)\mathbb{E}^{K},

where
\widehat{\nabla}
is the cone connection of
(\nabla,\beta)
. It follows that the cone connection associated with
(\nabla,\beta)
and the cone connection associated with
(\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k},\tilde{\beta}=\beta-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma))
are equal.
∎

Lemmas
6.9
and
6.10
are needed in the proof of Lemma
6.11
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
G
be a Lie group with identity element
e
, Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, and left-invariant Maurer-Cartan form
\omega_{G}
. Let
\sigma
be a smooth
\mathfrak{g}
-valued one-form on a nonempty open subinterval
I\subset\mathbb{R}
. For every
a_{0}\in I
there exists a unique smooth immersion
c:I\to G
such that
c(a_{0})=e
and
c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})=\sigma
.

Proof.

This is a special case of the more general statement with
I
replaced by an open domain in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\sigma
replaced by a smooth
G
-valued one-form satisfying the Maurer-Cartan identity
d\sigma+[\sigma,\sigma]=0
, whose proof can be found in
[
90
, section
3.5
]
or
[
123
, Theorem
6.1
]
.
∎

Let
G
be either
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
or
S^{1}
and in either case identify its Lie algebra with
\mathbb{R}
. Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
G
-bundle. Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an interval containing
0
. The
base curve
of a smoothly immersed curve
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
is the curve
\gamma=\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}:I\to M
in
M
. It is an immersion if
\hat{\gamma}
is everywhere transverse to the vertical. A
lift
to
N
of a smooth immersion
\gamma:I\to M
is a smooth immersion
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
such that
\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}=\gamma
. If
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
, the lift is
based
at
u
if
\hat{\gamma}(0)=u
.
If
\beta
is a principal
G
-connection on
N
, a
\beta
-horizontal lift
of
\gamma
is a lift
\hat{\gamma}
of
\gamma
such that
\hat{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)=0
.

Since
G
is abelian its left-invariant Maurer-Cartan form
\omega_{G}
is simply an invariant one-form invariant. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
the exponential map
\exp_{G}
takes the forms
\exp_{\mathbb{R}^{\ast}}(t)=e^{t}
and
\exp_{S^{1}}(t)=e^{\mathsf{i}t}
, and in either case
\omega_{G}=dt
. Let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vector field generating the principal
G
-action. If
\hat{\gamma}
is a lift of
\gamma
then so is
R_{c}(\hat{\gamma})
for any
c\in C^{\infty}(I,G)
. In this case

(6.12)

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}R_{c}(\hat{\gamma})=TR_{c}(\hat{\gamma})(\dot{\hat{\gamma}})+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{d}{dt})\mathbb{E}_{R_{c}(\hat{\gamma})},

in which, for example,
c^{\ast}(\omega_{\mathbb{R}^{\ast}})(\tfrac{d}{dt})=c^{-1}\dot{c}
.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
G
-bundle and let
\beta
be a principal
G
-connection on
N
, where
G
is
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
or
S^{1}
. Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be a nonempty open interval and let
\gamma:I\to M
be a smooth immersion. Given
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
there is a unique
\beta
-horizontal lift of
\gamma
based at
u
.

Proof.

Choose an open neighborhood
U
of
\gamma(0)
over which
N
trivializes, so that there is a smooth
G
-equivariant diffeomorphism
\phi:U\times G\to\rho^{-1}(U)
such that
\rho\circ\phi
equals the projection
U\times G\to U
onto the first factor. Let
I
be an open subinterval of
\mathbb{R}
containing
0
and such that
\gamma(I)\subset U
. Define a lift
\hat{\gamma}:I\to\rho^{-1}(U)
of
\gamma:I\to U
by
\hat{\gamma}(t)=\phi(\gamma(t),e)
.
By Lemma
6.9
there is a unique smooth immersion
c:I\to G
such that
c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})=-\hat{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)
and
c(0)=e
. Define a lift
\bar{\gamma}:I\to\rho^{-1}(U)
of
\gamma:I\to U
by
\bar{\gamma}(t)=R_{c(t)}\hat{\gamma}(t)
. Then
\rho\circ\bar{\gamma}(0)=\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}(0)=\gamma(0)
and, by (
6.12
) and the construction of
c
,

(6.13)

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)(\tfrac{d}{dt})=\beta(\tfrac{d}{dt}R_{c}(\hat{\gamma}))=R_{c}^{\ast}(\beta)(\dot{\hat{\gamma}})+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{d}{dt})=\hat{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)(\tfrac{d}{dt})+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{d}{dt})=0.

This shows there exists a
\beta
-horizontal lift of
\gamma
based at
u
. The computation (
6.13
) also shows the uniqueness, for if
\tilde{\gamma}:I\to\rho^{-1}(U)
is another
\beta
-horizontal lift based at
u
, then there is a smooth immersion
c:I\to G
such that
\bar{\gamma}=R_{c}\tilde{\gamma}
and, as in (
6.13
),
0=\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)=\tilde{\gamma}^{\ast}(\beta)+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})=c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})
, so that
c
is a constant map. Since
\tilde{\gamma}(0)=\bar{\gamma}(0)
,
c(0)=e
, so
c(I)=e
and
\tilde{\gamma}=\bar{\gamma}
.
∎

Lemma
6.11
is needed to prove that a radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
on the total space of
\rho:N\to M
fibers over the projective structure it induces on
M
(in the sense of Definition
5.8
). Although this is geometrically clear, a precise statement requires some care.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be a torsion-free affine connection on
N
invariant under the principal action and constituting with
\mathbb{E}
a radiant structure. Let the torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
and the tensor
Q\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
be associated with
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\rho:N\to M)
as in (
6.2
) of Lemma
6.4
. Let
[\nabla]
be the projective structure generated by
\nabla
.
Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be a nonempty open subinterval containing
0
.

(1)

Let
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
be a smooth immersion with base curve
\gamma:I\to M
. The image
\hat{\gamma}(I)
of
\hat{\gamma}
is a projective geodesic of
\widehat{\nabla}
if and only if there is
b\in C^{\infty}(I)
such that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}=(b-2A)\dot{\gamma},

\displaystyle Ab=\dot{A}+A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma}),

where
A=\beta(\dot{\hat{\gamma}})\in C^{\infty}(I)
.
In this case
\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\gamma}}}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}=b\dot{\hat{\gamma}}
and the image
\gamma(I)
is contained in a projective geodesic of
\nabla
.

(2)

Suppose that the path of the smooth immersion
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective geodesic of
\nabla
. For each
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
there is a subinterval
J\subset I
containing
0
in its interior and a lift
\hat{\gamma}:J\to N
of
\gamma
based at
u
and such that
\dot{\hat{\gamma}}\wedge\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\gamma}}}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}=0
on
J
(so that
\hat{\gamma}(J)
is a projective geodesic of
\widehat{\nabla}
).

(3)

(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is a conelike radiant structure fibering over
(M,[\nabla])
, where
[\nabla]
is the projective structure generated by
\nabla
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
be a smooth immersion with base curve
\gamma:I\to M
. Choose a smooth vector field
X
and smooth function
f
defined in a neighborhood of the image of
\gamma(I)
such that
\dot{\gamma}=X_{\gamma}
and
f\circ\gamma=A=\beta(\dot{\hat{\gamma}})\in C^{\infty}(I)
, so that there holds
\dot{\hat{\gamma}}=(\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}(f)\mathbb{E})_{\hat{\gamma}}
. By (
6.2
),

(6.15)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}(f)\mathbb{E}}(\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}(f)\mathbb{E})=\widehat{\nabla_{X}X}+2\rho^{\ast}(f)\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}\left(Q(X,X)+df(X)+f^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}.

Differentiating
f\circ\gamma=A
gives
df(X)_{\gamma}=\dot{A}
, and so (
6.15
) yields

(6.16)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{d/dt}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}}+2A\hat{\dot{\gamma}}+\left(\dot{A}+A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})\right)\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\gamma}},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\gamma}}\wedge\widehat{\nabla}_{d/dt}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\widehat{\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}}+\left(\dot{A}-A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})\right)\widehat{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\gamma}}-A\widehat{\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\gamma}}.

If (
6.17
) vanishes on
I
, then
\dot{\gamma}\wedge\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}=0
, so there is
g\in C^{\infty}(I)
such that
\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}=g\dot{\gamma}
, and
\dot{A}-A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=gA
on
I
, and, conversely, if there is such a
g
, then (
6.17
) vanishes. Writing
g=b-2A
with
b\in C^{\infty}(I)
, it follows that
\dot{\hat{\gamma}}\wedge\widehat{\nabla}_{d/dt}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}=0
on
I
if and only if there hold (
6.14
). Substituting (
6.14
) into (
6.16
) yields

(6.18)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{d/dt}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=b\widehat{\dot{\gamma}}+\left(\dot{A}+A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})\right)\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\gamma}}=b\left(\widehat{\dot{\gamma}}+A\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\gamma}}\right)=b\dot{\hat{\gamma}}.

This proves (
1
).

Now suppose that the path of the smooth immersion
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective geodesic of the projective structure
[\nabla]
generated by
\nabla
and fix
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
. By the assumption that
\gamma(I)
is a projective geodesic there is
g\in C^{\infty}(I)
such that
\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}=g\dot{\gamma}
.

Choose a vector field
X
such that
\dot{\gamma}=X_{\gamma}
. Then the integral curve
\hat{\gamma}
of the
\beta
-horizontal lift
\hat{X}
such that
\hat{\gamma}(0)=u
is a lift of
\gamma
based at
u
. Any other lift
\hat{\sigma}
of
\gamma
based at
u
has the form
\hat{\sigma}=R_{c}(\hat{\gamma})
for some
c\in C^{\infty}(I,G)
satisfying
c(0)=1
. By (
6.12
),

(6.19)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=TR_{c}(\hat{\gamma})(\dot{\hat{\gamma}})+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\sigma}}=TR_{c}(\hat{\gamma})(\hat{X}_{\hat{\gamma}})+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\sigma}}=\hat{X}_{\hat{\sigma}}+c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\sigma}},

so that
\hat{\sigma}
is an integral curve of
\hat{X}+f\mathbb{E}
where
f
is any smooth function defined on a neighborhood of
\gamma(I)
and satisfying
f\circ\gamma=c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})
. Let
A=\beta(\dot{\hat{\sigma}})
. By (
6.19
),
A
equals
c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})
.
There holds (
6.15
),
and differentiating
f\circ\gamma=A
gives
df(X)_{\gamma}=\dot{A}
, and so

(6.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\sigma}}}\dot{\hat{\sigma}}&=(g+2A)\dot{\hat{\sigma}}+\left(\dot{A}-A^{2}+Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})-gA\right)\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\sigma}}\\
&=b\dot{\hat{\sigma}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\dot{b}-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2}-\left(\dot{g}-\tfrac{1}{2}g^{2}-2Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})\right)\right)\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\sigma}},\end{split}

where
b=g+2A
. By (
6.20
), the image of
\hat{\sigma}(I)
is contained in a projective geodesic of
\widehat{\nabla}
if and only if

(6.21)

\displaystyle\dot{b}-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2}-\left(\dot{g}-\tfrac{1}{2}g^{2}-2Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})\right)=0.

There is an open interval
J\subset I
containing
0
on which (
6.21
) has a unique solution
b\in C^{\infty}(J)
such that
b(0)=0
.
A
\hat{\sigma}
which is a projective geodesic is found by solving
A=c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})
for
c
with
A=\tfrac{1}{2}(b-g)
. Precisely, once
b
has been obtained,
A
is determined from
b
and
g
by
A=(b-g)/2
, and, by Lemma
6.9
, there is a unique
c\in C^{\infty}(J,G)
such that
c(0)=e
and
c^{\ast}(\omega_{G})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t})=A
. (In the case
G=\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
, the solution is given explicitly as
c(t)=\exp{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}(b(v)-g(v))\,dv\right)}
.) The resulting lift
\hat{\sigma}
solves
\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\sigma}}}\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=(g+2A)\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=b\dot{\hat{\sigma}}
and is based at
u
. This proves (
2
).
By Corollary
5.3
,
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is conelike, so to show (
3
) it is needed only to show that it fibers over
(M,[\nabla])
.

For
p\in M
, let
\Sigma
be a planelike surface in
N
containing
u\in\rho^{-1}(p)
. Let
X\in T_{u}\Sigma
be transverse to
\mathbb{E}_{u}
. Let
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
be a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic such that
\hat{\gamma}(0)=u
and
\dot{\hat{\gamma}}(0)=X_{u}
. Because
\Sigma
is
\widehat{\nabla}
-totally geodesic,
\hat{\gamma}(I)\subset\Sigma
. By (
1
), the image
\gamma(I)
of the base curve
\gamma=\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}:I\to M
is a
[\nabla]
-projective geodesic and
\gamma:I\to M
is a parametrization of this projective geodesic. Given
Y=X+f\mathbb{E}_{u}\in T_{u}\Sigma
, let
\tilde{\gamma}:J\to M
be a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic such that
\tilde{\gamma}(0)=u
and
\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(0)=Y_{u}
. As before, the base curve
\rho\circ\tilde{\gamma}
is a projective parametrization of a
[\nabla]
-projective geodesic. As

(6.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}\rho(\tilde{\gamma}(t))&=T\rho(u)(\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(0))=T\rho(u)(Y_{u})=T\rho(u)(X_{u})=T\rho(u)(\dot{\hat{\gamma}}(0))=\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}\rho(\hat{\gamma}(t))\end{split}

the base curves
\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}
and
\rho\circ\tilde{\gamma}
equal
p
and have the same direction at
t=0
; since they parametrize
[\nabla]
-projective geodesics they must parametrize the same projective geodesic. If
Y
is replaced by its a multiple by a function not zero at
u
then the
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic
\tilde{\gamma}
is replaced by an appropriate linear reparametrization of itself, and the image of its base curve is unchanged. Since the intersection of
\Sigma
with a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesically convex neighborhood of
u
is foliated by
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesics, the preceding shows that
\rho(\Sigma)
is contained in a
[\nabla]
-projective geodesic.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\rho:N\to M
be a
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
or principal
S^{1}
bundle and let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection associated with a torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
on
M
and a principal connection
\beta
on
N
. Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be a nonempty open subinterval containing
0
.

(1)

If the immersion
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic, then its base curve
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective parametrization of the projective geodesic
\gamma(I)
.

(2)

Suppose that the smooth immersion
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective parametrization of a projective geodesic of
\nabla
. For each
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
there is a subinterval
J\subset I
containing
0
in its interior and a lift
\hat{\gamma}:J\to N
of
\gamma
based at
u
and such that
\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\gamma}}}\dot{\hat{\gamma}}=0
on
J
. In particular, there is a reparametrization of
\gamma
defined in an open neighborhood of
0
so that there is a lift of
\gamma
based at
u
which is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic, and this parametrization is determined up to precomposition with a linear fractional transformation.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in the proof of (
1
) of Lemma
6.11
. Because
\hat{\gamma}:I\to N
is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic, by (
1
) of Lemma
6.11
there holds (
6.14
) with
b=0
and, by (
6.3
),
Q(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=P(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})
, where
P
is the projective Schouten tensor of
[\nabla]
. In this case,
g=2A
and (
6.14
) yields
\dot{g}-\tfrac{1}{2}g^{2}-2P(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=0
, so that
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective parametrization of its image. This proves (
1
).

Now suppose the smooth immersion
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective parametrization of a projective geodesic of the projective structure
[\nabla]
generated by
\nabla
and fix
u\in\rho^{-1}(\gamma(0))
. By the assumption that
\gamma(I)
is a projective geodesic there is
g\in C^{\infty}(I)
such that
\nabla_{d/dt}\dot{\gamma}=g\dot{\gamma}
. Let the notations be as in the proof of (
2
) of Lemma
6.11
. By the proof of (
2
) of Lemma
6.11
, any other lift
\hat{\sigma}
of
\gamma
based at
u
has the form
\hat{\sigma}=R_{c}(\hat{\gamma})
for some
c\in C^{\infty}(I,G)
satisfying
c(0)=1
, and the function
A=\beta(\dot{\hat{\sigma}})
satisfies (
6.21
) where
b=g+2A
. Since
\gamma:I\to M
is a projective parametrization of
\gamma(I)
,
\dot{g}-\tfrac{1}{2}g^{2}-2P(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=0
, and the unique solution,
b(t)
, of (
6.21
) with initial condition
b(0)=0
is the function that is identically zero; that is
b(t)=0
for all
t
. The resulting lift
\hat{\sigma}
solves
\widehat{\nabla}_{\dot{\hat{\sigma}}}\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=(g+2A)\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=b\dot{\hat{\sigma}}=0
and is based at
u
.
Moreover, by Lemma
2.3
, if
\gamma:I\to M
is any parametrization of a projective geodesic of
\nabla
there is, after possibly passing to a subinterval
J\subset I
, a reparametrization, uniquely determined up to a linear fractional transformation, that is a projective parametrization. This proves (
2
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

By Lemmas
6.6
and
6.8
there is associated with the extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
a unique torsion-free affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
N
that with
\mathbb{E}
constitutes a conelike radiant structure invariant under the principal action, having antisymmetric Ricci tensor, and inducing
[\nabla,\beta]
. By (
3
) of Lemma
6.11
,
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
fibers over
(M,[\nabla])
, and the
\rho
-preimages of the projective geodesics of the projective structure
[\nabla]
generated by
\nabla
are the planelike surfaces in
N
. From Lemma
6.12
there follows the stronger statement that the base curve of a parametrized curve in
N
is a projective parametrization of a projective geodesic of
[\nabla]
if and only if the curve in
N
is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic. That the curvature of
\widehat{\nabla}
has the form (
6.5
) follows from (
6.7
) of Lemma
6.6
. This proves (
1
).

There remains to prove (
2
). Suppose given a conelike radiant structure
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
on
N
invariant under the principal action. Since it is invariant under the principal action, by Lemma
6.4
,
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
induces an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\rho:N\to M
, and, by (
3
) of Lemma
6.11
,
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
fibers over the underlying projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
. Because
\hat{D}
is
\mathbb{E}
-invariant,
\rho(\hat{D})_{i}=0
by (
3.17
), so by Theorem
5.6
, there is a conelike
\mathbb{E}
-invariant radiant structure
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
having antisymmetric Ricci tensor, having the same planelike surfaces as
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
, and inducing on
|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}N|
the same connection as
\hat{D}
. By (
2
) of Theorem
5.6
,
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
is invariant under the principal action.
Since it is invariant under the principal action, by Lemma
6.4
,
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
induces an extended projective structure on
M
, and, by (
3
) of Lemma
6.11
,
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
fibers over its underlying projective structure on
M
. Since
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
fibers over
(M,[\nabla])
and
(\tilde{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
has the same planelike surfaces as
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
, this underlying projective structure must be
[\nabla]
. This shows that
\hat{D}
and
\tilde{\nabla}
induce on
M
extended projective structures having the same underlying projective structure
[\nabla]
. By Lemma
6.5
, the induced extended projective structures are the same.
∎

Remark 12
.

By (
1b
) of Theorem
6.2
(which follows from the last equality of (
6.10
)), the cone connection of
(\nabla,\beta)
is Ricci-flat if and only if
2R_{[ij]}=(n+1)\omega_{ij}
, that is the curvature of the connection induced by
\nabla
on the density bundle
|\operatorname{Det}M|^{1/(n+1)}
equals the curvature of
\beta
. Since the antisymmetric part of the Ricci tensor is always exact, every torsion-free affine connection is projectively equivalent to one with symmetric Ricci tensor. It follows that if the bundle
\rho:N\to M
admits a flat connection then there are pairs
(\nabla,\beta)
with vanishing associated two-form, so with Ricci-flat cone connection.

Remark 13
.

The geometric content of the vanishing of the symmetric part of the Ricci tensor in Theorem
6.2
is that this condition is what forces the linkage between the parametrization of the geodesics of
\widehat{\nabla}
and the projective parametrization of the projective geodesics of
[\nabla]
in (
1a
) of Theorem
6.2
.

7.
Classical Thomas connection revisited

Theorem
6.2
can be viewed as generalizing to
S^{1}
-bundles an essentially local result that goes back to T. Y. Thomas
[
128
,
129
,
133
]
, that associates with a projective structure on an
n
-manifold a Ricci-flat affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on the total space of a trivial
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
principal bundle over
M
. Here
\widehat{\nabla}
is called the
(classical) Thomas connection
of
[\nabla]
.
An appropriate specialization of Theorem
6.2
yields Theorem
7.3
, which gives a gauge equivariant, global, functorial formulation of the classical Thomas connection. For modern accounts of Thomas’s construction and the closely related tractor formalism for parabolic geometries, see, for example,
[
7
,
25
,
44
,
45
]
, and in particular
[
70
]
which gives a streamlined and careful account situated in the general context of parabolic geometries.

The Thomas construction is revisited here primarily to elucidate two points. The first is the functorial nature of the construction. As the construction is essentially local, this amounts to clarifying what characterizes the classical Thomas connection in the sense of what conditions on
\widehat{\nabla}
determine it uniquely. The usual accounts establish existence but are somewhat vague with respect to uniqueness. Clarifying this point is relevant for the problem of the recognition of a Thomas connection, that is, given a connection, deciding whether it is a Thomas connection. Theorem
10.3
gives an example where such a characterization is used to decide when the conjugate of a Thomas connection with respect to a metric is again a Thomas connection.

The second point is to clarify the global nature of the construction, in particular that the construction makes sense on real line bundles that need not be trivial or canonically trivialized (as is often assumed). This amounts to describing how to extend the construction to line bundles over
M
that transform like density bundles without being density bundles, in particular in the sense that they need not admit any natural action of
\diff(M)
. This amounts to working with a pseudo-hyperplane bundle as in Definition
4.7
instead of the necessarily topologically trivial density bundle
|\operatorname{Det}TM|^{1/(n+1)}
. In the setting of Theorem
6.2
, if
N=|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|^{1/(n+1)}\setminus\mathsf{0}(M)
and if
\beta
is taken to be the principal connection induced by a connection
\nabla
on
M
, then
(\nabla,\beta)
determines an extended projective structure and the cone connection of
[\nabla,\beta]
depends only on the projective equivalence class
[\nabla]
. This recovers the classical Thomas connection of the projective structure
[\nabla]
. The special feature of the bundle of nonvanishing densities is that an affine connection induces a principal connection on this bundle, so any projective structure determines in a canonical way an extended projective structure. Although this means that the original construction of Thomas works for any
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-principle bundle with the property that its sections transform locally like densities, it is what fails for an arbitrary principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle having no additional structure. The additional structure needed is formally analogous to a spin structure or square root of the canonical line bundle on a Kähler manifold, namely it is an identification of some power of the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, or some associated line bundle, with some density bundle, and this is what the notion of pseudo-hyperplane bundle as in Definition
4.7
formalizes. (This discussion can be situated in the general context of parabolic geometries as in
[
25
, Sections
3.5
and
4.15
]
, but the more concrete approach taken here, while less powerful, is useful in applications.)

A torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
on
M
induces a covariant derivative, also denoted
\nabla
, on a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
as follows. For a local section
e
of
\mathcal{E}
and a vector field
X
on
M
, define

(7.1)

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}e=\tfrac{1}{2n+2}e^{-2n-1}\otimes\nabla_{X}(e^{2n+2}),

where
\nabla_{X}(e^{2n+2})
denotes the covariant derivative of the section local section
e^{2n+2}
of
(\det T^{\ast}M)^{\otimes 2}
.
A tensor taking values in
\mathcal{E}^{k}
is said to have
p-weight

k
(where
p-weight
abbreviates
projective weight
). Locally a section of
\mathcal{E}^{k}
behaves like a
-k/(n+1)
-density.
In particular, if
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
and
e\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E}^{k})
, then

(7.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{\nabla}_{i}e=\nabla_{i}e+k\gamma_{i}e,

\displaystyle\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}e=\tfrac{k}{2(n+1)}R_{ijp}\,^{p}e=-\tfrac{k}{n+1}R_{[ij]}e=kP_{[ij]}e.

It is straightforward to check that the the right-hand side of (
7.1
) with the Lie derivative
\mathfrak{L}_{X}
in place of
\nabla_{X}
defines an action of the Lie algebra
\vect(M)
of vector fields on
\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
. This means that sections of
\mathcal{E}
locally behave as densities, so
\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
is a module over
\vect(M)
, even though in general
\diff(M)
need not act on
M
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold equipped with a pseudo-hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
be the frame bundle of the dual pseudo-tautological bundle.

(1)

A torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
on
M
induces a principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-connection
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
, and the curvature
\omega\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
of
\tau
satisfies
\omega_{ij}=-\tfrac{1}{n+1}R_{ijp}\,^{p}=\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}=-2P_{[ij]}
.

(2)

A projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
determines in a canonical way an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\tau]
on the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
for which the associated two-form
\eta_{ij}
vanishes identically.

(3)

A projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
determines a Lie algebra embedding
\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}:\Gamma(TM)\to\Gamma(T\mathcal{F})
satisfying
T\rho(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=X
and
R_{r}^{\ast}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=\mathscr{L}(X)
, and such that, for
\nabla\in[\nabla]
inducing
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
, there holds

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)=\hat{X}-\tfrac{1}{n+1}\rho^{\ast}(\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X))\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}},

where
X\in\Gamma(TM)\to\hat{X}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{F})
is the horizontal lift determined by
\tau
.
If
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a hyperplane line bundle, then
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=0
, so that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
, while if
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle, then
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
.

Proof.

Regarding
\mathcal{F}
as
\mathcal{E}^{-1}\setminus\{0\}
,
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
is defined by the requirement that if
\tilde{u}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})
is the homogeneity
1
function corresponding with a smooth section
u\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
, then the homogeneity
1
smooth function on
\mathcal{F}
corresponding with
\nabla_{X}u
is
d\tilde{u}(\hat{X})
, where
\hat{X}
is the
\tau
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
. By definition the curvature
\omega\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
satisfies
d\tau=\rho^{\ast}(\omega)
. On the one hand, by (
7.2
),
2\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}u=\tfrac{1}{n+1}R_{ijp}\,^{p}u=-\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}u
. On the other hand, for
X,Y\in\Gamma(TM)
, the homogeneity
1
function on
\mathcal{F}
corresponding with
\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}u-\nabla_{Y}\nabla_{X}u-\nabla_{[X,Y]}u
equals

(7.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\widetilde{\nabla_{Y}u}(\hat{X})&-d\widetilde{\nabla_{X}u}(\hat{Y})-d\tilde{u}(\widehat{[X,Y]})\\
&=\hat{X}d\tilde{u}(\hat{Y})-\hat{Y}d\tilde{u}(\hat{X})-d\tilde{u}\left([\hat{X},\hat{Y}]+d\tau(\hat{X},\hat{Y})\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}}\right)=-d\tau(\hat{X},\hat{Y})\tilde{u}=-\omega(X,Y)\tilde{u},\end{split}

where there has been used
d\tilde{u}(\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}})=\tilde{u}
. This shows (
1
).

Given a projective structure
[\nabla]
, each
\nabla\in[\nabla]
induces
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
. Let
u\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
.
If
\bar{\nabla}=\nabla+2\gamma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
, then, by (
7.2
),
\bar{\nabla}_{i}u=\nabla_{i}u+\gamma_{i}u
, and it follows that the horizontal lift of
X
with respect to the principal connection
\bar{\tau}\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
induced by
\bar{\nabla}
is
\hat{X}+\rho^{\ast}(\gamma(X))\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}}
and
\bar{\tau}=\tau-\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
. These identities imply
[\nabla,\tau]
is an extended projective structure on
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
and also that (
7.3
) does not depend on the choice of
\nabla\in[\nabla]
.
By (
1
),
\omega_{ij}=\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}
, where
d\tau=\rho^{\ast}(\omega)
, so the two-form
\eta_{ij}
associated with
[\nabla,\tau]
satisfies
\eta_{ij}=\omega_{ij}-\tfrac{2}{n+1}R_{[ij]}=0
. The properties
T\rho(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=X
and
R_{r}^{\ast}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=\mathscr{L}(X)
hold by the definition (
7.3
).
That (
7.3
) defines a Lie algebra embedding can be checked directly using the identities

(7.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{L}_{X}\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(Y)&-\mathfrak{L}_{Y}\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X)-\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}([X,Y])=-\mathsf{Ric}(X,Y)+\mathsf{Ric}(Y,X),\\
[\hat{X},\hat{Y}]&=\widehat{[X,Y]}-\tfrac{1}{n+1}(\mathsf{Ric}(X,Y)-\mathsf{Ric}(Y,X))\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}},\end{aligned}

the first holding for any torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
and the second following from Lemma
7.1
.

Suppose
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a hyperplane line bundle. From (
7.3
) it follows that
T\mathcal{Q}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=\mathscr{L}(X)
where
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{V}
is the map induced from the isomorphism
\mathcal{E}^{n+1}\simeq\operatorname{Det}TM
, and the property
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=0
follows. Taking the exterior derivative shows
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
. If
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle, then the preceding argument applies locally to show that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
.
∎

The conclusion of Lemma
7.2
requires argument because
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
is disconnected.

Lemma 7.2
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, let
\mathcal{E}\to M
be a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
be equipped with the volume density
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
.
Let
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}}
. A Ricci-flat,
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, equiaffine conelike radiant structure
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
on
(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
is invariant under the principal action on
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

Because
\widehat{\nabla}
is
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, it is invariant under the principal action
R_{r}
for
r>0
, so it suffices to prove that
R_{-1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})=\widehat{\nabla}
. Because
\mathbb{E}
and
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
are invariant under the principal action,
R_{-1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})
is a Ricci-flat,
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, equiaffine conelike radiant structure on
(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
. Because
R_{-1}
preserves the planes of
\widehat{\nabla}
,
R_{-1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})
has the same planes as
\widehat{\nabla}
, and because
R_{1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})
and
\widehat{\nabla}
preserve the same volume density
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
they induce on
|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}\mathcal{F}|
the same connection, so, by Theorem
5.6
,
R_{-1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})=\widehat{\nabla}
.
∎

Theorem 7.3
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, let
\mathcal{E}\to M
be a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
be the frame bundle of the dual pseudo-tautological line bundle equipped with the pseudo-Euler structure determined by the volume density
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
and radiant Euler vector field
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}}
.

There is a bijection between the set of projective structures on
M
and the set of Ricci-flat,
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, conelike equiaffine radiant structures on
(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
associating with a projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
a unique torsion-free affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
\mathcal{F}
such that
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
is a Ricci-flat, conelike equiaffine radiant structure invariant under the principal action on
\mathcal{F}
and fibering over
(M,[\nabla])
.

(1)

The curvature
\hat{R}
of
\widehat{\nabla}
satisfies

(7.6)

\displaystyle\hat{R}(\hat{X},\hat{Y})\hat{Z}=\widehat{B(X,Y)Z}+C(X,Y,Z)\mathbb{E},

\displaystyle\hat{R}(\hat{X},\hat{Y})\mathbb{E}=\hat{R}(\mathbb{E},\hat{X})\hat{Y}=\hat{R}(\hat{X},\mathbb{E})\mathbb{E}=0,

where
B
and
C
are the projective Weyl and Cotton tensors of
[\nabla]
and the horizontal lifts of
X,Y,Z\in\Gamma(TM)
are with respect to any principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-connection on
\mathcal{F}
.
In particular,
\widehat{\nabla}
is flat if and only if
[\nabla]
is projectively flat.

(2)

The planelike surfaces of
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
are the
\rho
-preimages of the projective geodesics of
M
and the base curve
\gamma=\rho\circ\hat{\gamma}:I\to M
of a parametrized curve
\hat{\gamma}:I\to\mathcal{F}
is a projective parametrization of a projective geodesic of
[\nabla]
if and only if
\hat{\gamma}
is a
\widehat{\nabla}
-geodesic.

(3)

If
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a hyperplane line bundle, then
\widehat{\nabla}
preserves the volume form
\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=d\mu^{\mathcal{F}}
.

(4)

For all
X\in\Gamma(TM)
,
\operatorname{div}_{\widehat{\nabla}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))=0
.

The connection
\widehat{\nabla}
of Theorem
7.3
is the (classical)
Thomas connection
associated with
[\nabla]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.1
,
[\nabla]
determines an extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\mathcal{F}
in such a way that for
(\nabla,\beta)\in[\nabla,\beta]
,
\beta
is induced by
\nabla
, and such that
\eta_{ij}=0
.
Because
\eta_{ij}=0
, by Theorem
6.2
there is a unique torsion-free affine connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
\mathcal{F}
that with
\mathbb{E}
constitutes a Ricci-flat, conelike radiant structure invariant under the principal action on
\mathcal{F}
and fibering over
(M,[\nabla])
. Because
\eta_{ij}=0
, by (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) of Theorem
6.2
,
\widehat{\nabla}
is defined by
\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y}=\widehat{\nabla_{X}Y}+P(X,Y)\mathbb{E}
and
\widehat{\nabla}\mathbb{E}=\delta
, where
\hat{X}
is the
\beta
-horizontal lift of
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, and satisfies (
1
) and (
2
), in particular the curvature of
\widehat{\nabla}
has the form (
7.6
).

Suppose
\mathcal{E}\to M
is a hyperplane line bundle.
Consider the Euler structure
(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{E},\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=d\mu^{\mathcal{F}})
defined in Lemma
4.9
and let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the Thomas connection of
[\nabla]
. By Lemma
3.9
,
\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}-(n+1)\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
, the last equality by the construction of
\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}
. Because
\iota(\mathbb{E})\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=0
and
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mu=(n+1)\mu
, for
X\in\Gamma(TM)
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\iota(\mathbb{E})\left(\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}\right)=\mathfrak{L}_{\hat{X}}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}-\rho^{\ast}(\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}X)\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=0,

the penultimate equality by (
7.3
) and the last equality by (
3
) of Lemma
7.1
. This shows that
\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
for all
X\in\Gamma(TM)
and with
\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
, this shows
\widehat{\nabla}\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=0
. For a general pseudo-hyperplane line bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
, essentially the same argument shows that
\widehat{\nabla}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
. This shows that
\widehat{\nabla}
is equiaffine and shows (
3
). Said differently, the claim that
\widehat{\nabla}
is equiaffine is essentially local so that its validity for a general pseudo-hyperplane line bundle follows from its validity for a hyperplane line bundle.

The preceding establishes that associated with a projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
there is a Ricci-flat, conelike equiaffine radiant structure on
\mathcal{F}
invariant under the principal action and fibering over
(M,[\nabla])

By Lemma
7.2
a Ricci-flat,
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, conelike equiaffine radiant structure
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
on
\mathcal{F}
is necessarily invariant under the principal action.
By (
3
) of Lemma
6.11
,
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E})
fibers over the projective structure
[\nabla]
it induces on
M
, so
(\hat{D},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
and
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|)
are Ricci-flat, conelike equiaffine radiant structures on
(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{E},|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}})
invariant under the principal action and fibering over the same projective structure
[\nabla]
. By the uniqueness of
\widehat{\nabla}
,
\hat{D}=\widehat{\nabla}
.

For
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, by definition of the divergence and (
3
) of Lemma
7.1
,
\operatorname{div}_{\widehat{\nabla}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\Psi^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=d\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=0
. This proves (
4
) when
\mathcal{E}
is a hyperplane line bundle. For a general pseudo-hyperplane line bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
,
\widehat{\nabla}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
, so that
\operatorname{div}_{\widehat{\nabla}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X))|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}|\Psi^{F}|-\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathcal{F}}(X)}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
.
∎

Remark 14
.

The algebraic and differential Bianchi identities for the Thomas connection
\widehat{\nabla}
of
[\nabla]
yield (
2.3
) and
\nabla_{[i}C_{jk]l}+P_{[i|p|}B_{jk]l}\,^{p}=0
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold equipped with a pseudo-hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
. Let
[\nabla]
be a projective structure on
M
with Thomas connection
\widehat{\nabla}
. If
k\neq n+1
, for a trace-free
A_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}\,^{j}\in\Gamma(S^{k}T^{\ast}M\otimes TM)
there is a unique
[\nabla]
-invariant lift

\mathscr{L}(A)_{I_{1}\dots I_{k}}\,^{J}\in\Gamma(S^{k}T^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes T\mathcal{F})
satisfying:

(1)

\mathscr{L}(A)
is a
horizontal lift
of
A
, meaning
\iota(\mathbb{E})\mathscr{L}(A)=0
and for all
X_{1},\dots X_{k}\in\Gamma(TM)
and
\theta\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
there holds
\rho^{\ast}(\theta)(\mathscr{L}(A)(\mathscr{L}(X_{1}),\dots,\mathscr{L}(X_{k})))=A(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})
;

(2)

\mathscr{L}(A)
is
\widehat{\nabla}
-divergence free,
\widehat{\nabla}_{J}\mathscr{L}(A)_{I_{1}\dots I_{K}}\,^{J}=0
.

Proof.

Fix
\nabla\in[\nabla]
and consider the associated invariant lift of vector fields.
If
\mathscr{L}(A)
is an invariant lift of
A
then there is
B\in\Gamma(S^{k}T^{\ast}M)
such that, for all
X_{1},\dots,X_{k}\in\Gamma(TM)
,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(A)(\hat{X}_{1},\dots,\hat{X}_{k})=\widehat{A(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})}+\rho^{\ast}(B(X_{1},\dots,X_{k}))\mathbb{E}.

From the identities
\iota(\mathbb{E})(\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathscr{L}(A))=0
,
(\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathscr{L}(A))=(1-k)\mathscr{L}(A)
, and

(7.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\mathscr{L}(A))(\mathbb{E},\hat{X}_{1},\dots,\hat{X}_{k-1})&=-\mathscr{L}(A)(\hat{X},\hat{X}_{1},\dots,\hat{X}_{k-1}),\\
(\widehat{\nabla}_{\hat{X}}\mathscr{L}(A))(\hat{X}_{1},\dots,\hat{X}_{k})&=\widehat{(\nabla_{X}A)(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})}+B(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})\hat{X}\\
&+\left((\nabla_{X}B)(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})+P(X,A(X_{1},\dots,X_{k})\right)\mathbb{E},\end{aligned}

for
X,X_{1},\dots,X_{k}\in\Gamma(TM)
, it follows that
\widehat{\nabla}_{J}\mathscr{L}(A)_{I_{1}\dots I_{K}}\,^{J}=(n+1-k)B_{I_{1}\dots I_{K}}+\rho^{\ast}(\mathsf{tr}\,\nabla A)_{I_{1}\dots I_{k}}
, where
