(\mathsf{tr}\,\nabla A)_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}=\nabla_{p}A_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}\,^{p}
. Consequently, when
k\neq n+1
, the condition
\widehat{\nabla}_{J}\mathscr{L}(A)_{I_{1}\dots I_{K}}\,^{J}=0
determines
B
in (
7.8
) uniquely by
B_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}=-\tfrac{1}{n+1-k}\nabla_{p}A_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}\,^{p}
. Since the condition determining
B
does not depend on the choice of
\nabla\in[\nabla]
, the resulting tensor
\mathscr{L}(A)
also does not depend on this choice. Alternatively, it is straightforward to check directly that
\mathscr{L}(A)
is well defined independently of the choice of
\nabla
.
∎

Example 20
.

By (
2.3
) and (
7.6
), if
n>2
, the curvature of the Thomas connection
\widehat{\nabla}
of
[\nabla]
is the
[\nabla]
-invariant lift of the projective Weyl tensor
B_{ijk}\,^{l}
of
[\nabla]
.

Corollary 7.5

(
[
70
, Theorem
4.4
]
)
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold equipped with a pseudo-hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
. Let
[\nabla]
be a projective structure on
M
with Thomas connection
\widehat{\nabla}
.
For all
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, the invariant lift
\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{L}_{X}[\nabla])
of
\mathfrak{L}_{X}[\nabla]
is
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla}
. In particular,
X
is an infinitesimal automorphism of
[\nabla]
if and only if
\mathscr{L}(X)
is an infinitesimal automorphism of
\widehat{\nabla}
.

Proof.

By the uniqueness claim of Lemma
7.4
, it suffices to check that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla}
is a horizontal lift of
\mathfrak{L}_{X}[\nabla]
that is
\widehat{\nabla}
-divergence free. Although the details are omitted, a straightforward computation using (
7.6
) and (
7.5
) shows that for
X,Y,Z\in\Gamma(TM)
and
\theta\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
,
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla})(\mathbb{E},\hat{Z})=0
and
\rho^{\ast}(\theta)((\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla})(\hat{Y},\hat{Z})=(\mathfrak{L}_{X}[\nabla])(Y,Z)
, so that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla}
is a horizontal lift of
\mathfrak{L}_{X}[\nabla]
.
For any torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
and any
X\in\Gamma(TM)
there holds

(7.10)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{L}_{X}\nabla)_{ip}\,^{p}=\nabla_{i}\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X)-2X^{p}R_{[pi]}.

Applying (
7.10
) with
\mathscr{L}(X)
and
\widehat{\nabla}
in place of
X
and
\nabla
, using the Ricci-flatness of
\widehat{\nabla}
, and using that, by (
3
) of Theorem
7.3
,
\operatorname{div}_{\widehat{\nabla}}(\mathscr{L}(X))=0
, yields
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widehat{\nabla})_{IP}\,^{P}=0
.
∎

Remark 15
.

The essential local content of Lemma
7.4
and Corollary
7.5
goes back in some form to Thomas
[
129
]
. The invariant lift is a close relative of the notion of saturated cotractor used in
[
68
]
; for example in that language the application of Lemma
7.4
to the projective Weyl tensor as in Remark
20
is essentially
[
68
, Equation
33
]
. In
[
70
, Theorem
4.4
]
the result is proved essentially as stated here for
\mathcal{E}=|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|^{1/(n+1)}
.

Let
\Aut(\mathcal{F})
denote the group of principal bundle automorphisms of the principal bundle
\rho:\mathcal{F}\to M
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold and let
\mu
be the tautological
n
-form on the total space of the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle
\rho:\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{F}{\left(\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M\right)}\to M
.

(1)

The map
\mathsf{L}:\diff(M)\to\Aut(\mathcal{V})
associating with
\phi\in\diff(M)
its
tautological lift

\mathsf{L}(\phi)\in\Aut(\mathcal{V})
defined by

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(\phi)(s)(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})=s(T\phi^{-1}(\phi(\rho(s))(X_{1})\wedge\dots\wedge T\phi^{-1}(\rho(\phi(s))(X_{n})),

\displaystyle\text{for}\,\,X_{i}\in T_{\phi(\rho(s))}M,

is an injective homomorphism whose image

(7.12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(\diff(M))=\{\Phi\in\Aut(\mathcal{V}):\Phi^{\ast}(\mu)=\mu\}

acts as automorphisms of the associated Euler structure
(\mathcal{V},\Psi^{\mathcal{V}},\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{V}})
.

(2)

For any
s\in\Gamma(\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M)
and any
\phi\in\diff(M)
, the homogeneity
-1
functions
\tilde{s}
and
\widetilde{\phi^{\ast}s}
on
\mathcal{V}
corresponding with
s
and
\phi^{\ast}s
satisfy
\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}\tilde{s}=\tilde{s}\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi)=\widetilde{\phi^{\ast}s}
.

(3)

The
tautological lift

\mathscr{L}:\Gamma(TM)\to\Gamma(T\mathcal{V})
defined by setting
\mathscr{L}(X)
equal to the infinitesimal generator of the image under
\mathsf{L}
of the flow of
X
is an injective Lie algebra homomorphism whose image is

(7.13)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\Gamma(TM))=\{Z\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{V}):R_{r}^{\ast}(Z)=Z,\mathfrak{L}_{Z}\mu=0\}.

For a torsion-free affine connection
\nabla
on
M
inducing
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{V})
and
X\in\Gamma(TM)
,

(7.14)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(X)=\hat{X}-\rho^{\ast}(\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X))\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{V}},

where
\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X)=\mathsf{tr}\,\nabla X
.

Proof.

Using local trivializations of
\mathcal{D}
it is straightforward to check that
\mathsf{L}(\phi)
is a diffeomorphism of
\mathcal{D}
.
That
\rho\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi)=\phi\circ\rho
,
\mathsf{L}(\phi)\circ R_{r}=R_{r}\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi)
, and
\mathsf{L}(\phi_{1}\circ\phi_{2})=\mathsf{L}(\phi_{1})\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi_{2})
follow directly from (
7.11
), while
\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}(\mu)=\mu
follows from (
7.11
) together with (
4.2
).

If
\Phi\in\Aut(\mathcal{V})
covers the identity then there is a homogeneity
0
function
\tilde{\phi}
on
\mathcal{V}
such that
\Phi(s)=R_{\tilde{\phi}(s)}s
, and it follows from (
4.2
) that
\Phi^{\ast}(\mu)_{s}=\tilde{\phi}(s)\mu_{s}
. Consequently,
\Phi^{\ast}(\mu)=\mu
if and only if
\tilde{\phi}
is identically
1
, or, what is the same,
\Phi
is the identity.
If
\Phi\in\Aut(\mathcal{V})
covers
\phi\in\diff(M)
then
\mathsf{L}(\phi^{-1})\circ\Phi
covers the identity, and it follows that
\Phi^{\ast}(\mu)=\mu
if and only if
\Phi=\mathsf{L}(\phi)
. This shows (
7.12
). Since
\mathsf{L}(\phi)
preserves
\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{V}}
, this means that
\mathsf{L}(\diff(M))
acts as automorphisms of the associated Euler structure
(\mathcal{V},\Psi^{\mathcal{V}},\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{V}})
.

By definition of
\mu
, for any section
s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{D})
, the homogeneity
-1
function
\tilde{s}
on
\mathcal{V}
corresponding with
s
satisfies
\rho^{\ast}(s)=\tilde{s}\mu
. Hence, for
\phi\in\diff(M)
,

(7.15)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\phi^{\ast}s})\mu=\rho^{\ast}\phi^{\ast}s=(\phi\circ\rho)^{\ast}s=(\rho\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi))^{\ast}s=\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}\rho^{\ast}s=\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}(\tilde{s}\mu)=(\tilde{s}\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi))\mu,

which shows that
\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}\tilde{s}=\tilde{s}\circ\mathsf{L}(\phi)=\widetilde{\phi^{\ast}s}
.

That for
X\in\Gamma(TM)
there hold
T\rho(\mathscr{L}(X))=X
,
R_{r}^{\ast}(\mathscr{L}(X))=\mathscr{L}(X)
, and
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\mu=0
is immediate from the definition. If
Z\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{V})
is as in (
7.13
), then it generates a local flow by principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle automorphisms of
\mathcal{V}
preserving
\mu
, which by (
7.12
) are in the image of
\mathsf{L}
, and so differentiating along the flow shows that
Z
is in the image of
\mathscr{L}
.

For
X\in\Gamma(TM)
, differentiating
\mathsf{L}(\phi_{t})^{\ast}\tilde{s}=\widetilde{\phi_{t}^{\ast}s}
along the flow
\phi_{t}
of
X
and using (
7.15
) shows that
d\tilde{s}(\mathscr{L}(X))=\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}_{X}s}
.
Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
inducing
\tau\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{V})
. For a local section
s
of
\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M
,
\nabla_{X}s=\mathfrak{L}_{X}s-\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X)s
. Because
\tilde{s}
has homogeneity
-1
,

(7.16)

\displaystyle d\tilde{s}(\hat{X})-d\tilde{s}(\mathscr{L}(X))=\widetilde{\nabla_{X}s}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}_{X}s}=-\rho^{\ast}(\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X))\widetilde{s}=d\tilde{s}\left(\rho^{\ast}(\operatorname{div}_{\nabla}(X))\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{V}}\right),

where
\hat{X}
is the
\tau
-horizontal lift of
X
. This shows (
7.14
).
∎

Remark 16
.

By its definition (
7.11
),
\mathsf{L}(\phi)
extends to an element of
\diff(\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M)
that fixes the image of the zero section. For
s\in\Gamma(\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M)
and
\phi\in\diff(M)
,
\mathsf{L}(\phi^{-1})\circ s\circ\phi
is a section of
\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M
, and

(7.17)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(\phi^{-1})\circ s\circ\phi=(\mathsf{L}(\phi^{-1})\circ s\circ\phi)^{\ast}\mu=\phi^{\ast}s^{\ast}\mathsf{L}(\phi^{-1})^{\ast}\mu=\phi^{\ast}s^{\ast}\mu=\phi^{\ast}s,

by the properties characterizing
\mu
.

For any representation
\sigma:\mathbb{R}^{\ast}\to\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
, the principal bundle automorphism
\mathsf{L}(\phi)\in\Aut(\mathcal{V})
induces a line bundle automorphism of the associated bundle
\mathcal{V}\times_{\sigma}\mathbb{R}
also denoted
\mathsf{L}(\phi)
. In Corollary
7.7
this is applied to the bundle
|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|^{-1/(n+1)}
corresponding with
\sigma(t)=|t|^{1/(n+1)}
.

Corollary 7.7
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, let
\mathcal{E}=|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|^{-1/(n+1)}\to M
and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
.
The assignment
[\nabla]\to\widehat{\nabla}
is equivariant with respect to the action of
\diff(M)
in the sense that the tautological lift of
\phi\in\diff(M)
satisfies
\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})=\widehat{\phi^{\ast}([\nabla])}
, and
\mathsf{L}(\phi)\in\Aut(\widehat{\nabla},\Psi)
if and only if
\phi\in\Aut([\nabla])
.

Proof.

When
\mathcal{E}=|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|^{-1/(n+1)}
, if
\phi\in\diff(M)
, then
\mathsf{L}(\phi)^{\ast}(\widehat{\nabla})
constitutes with
\mathbb{E}
a Ricci-flat
\mathbb{E}
-invariant conelike radiant structure fibering over
(M,\phi^{\ast}([\nabla]))
, so, by the uniqueness part of Theorem
7.3
, equals the Thomas connection of
\phi^{\ast}([\nabla])
.
∎

Example 21
.

This example generalizes Example
4
. Let
M
be an
n
-manifold equipped with a pseudo-hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
and let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
. Let
[\nabla]
be a projective structure on
M
with Thomas connection
\widehat{\nabla}
. Fix
1<\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and let
\bar{M}
be the quotient of
\mathcal{F}
by the action of
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
on
\mathcal{F}
given via the principal action
R_{r}
of
r\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
on
\mathcal{F}
by
(k,\epsilon)\to R_{\epsilon\lambda^{k}}
. Because the principal action is by automorphisms of
\widehat{\nabla}
, there are a torsion-free affine connection
\bar{\nabla}
and a vector field
\bar{\mathbb{E}}
on
\bar{M}
such that
(\mathcal{F},\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})\to(\bar{M},\bar{\nabla},\bar{\mathbb{E}})
is a submersion of radiant structures. In general,
(\bar{M},\bar{\nabla},\bar{\mathbb{E}})
is a Ricci-flat conelike radiant structure. Its planelike surface are the images in
\bar{M}
of the planelike surfaces of
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
. If
[\nabla]
is flat, then
(\bar{M},\bar{\nabla},\bar{\mathbb{E}})
is flat; if moreover
\mathcal{E}=|\operatorname{Det}TM|^{-1/(n+1)}
, then it is affinely isomorphic to the radiant flat affine manifold constructed in Example
4
.

Note that the parallel volume density on
\mathcal{F}
does not descend to
\bar{M}
and
(\bar{M},\nabla,\bar{\mathbb{E}})
admits no parallel volume by Lemma
3.10
. This is consistent with the main theorem of
[
29
]
, which shows that a three-manifold fibered by circles over a compact surface of genus at least two admits no flat unimodular affine structure.

8.
Metrics compatible with radiant structures and radiant statistical structures

A connection
\nabla
on
TM
determines a dual connection, customarily also denoted by
\nabla
, on
T^{\ast}M
, and defined by
(\nabla_{X}\mu)(Y)=\mathfrak{L}_{X}(\mu(Y))-\mu(\nabla_{X}Y)
for
X,Y\in\Gamma(TM)
and
\mu\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
. Using a metric
h
on
TM
the dual connection can be transported back to
TM
, and the result is the
h
-conjugate connection

\bar{\nabla}
defined for
X\in\Gamma(TM)
and
\mu\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
by
\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mu=(\nabla_{X}\mu^{\flat})^{\sharp}
. Expanding this shows that
\bar{\nabla}
is defined by
\mathfrak{L}_{X}h(A,B)=h(\nabla_{X}A,B)+h(A,\bar{\nabla}_{X}B)
for
X,A,B\in\Gamma(TM)
. Alternatively,

(8.1)

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}-\nabla=h^{kp}\nabla_{i}h_{jp},

and from (
8.1
) it follows that
h
-conjugacy is an involution on the space of affine connections. By (
8.1
),
\bar{\nabla}_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
. In particular, a connection
\nabla
is
h
-self-conjugate, meaning
\bar{\nabla}=\nabla
, if and only if
\nabla
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
.
However, as follows from (
8.1
), the torsion
\bar{\tau}_{ij}\,^{k}
of
\bar{\nabla}
is expressed in terms of the torsion
\tau_{ij}\,^{k}
of
\nabla
by

(8.2)

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}_{ij}\,^{k}=\tau_{ij}\,^{k}+2h^{kp}\nabla_{[i}h_{j]p},

so that
h
-conjugacy does not preserve the affine subspace of torsion-free affine connections. For this reason it is convenient to work with the
h
-opposite connection

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
of
\nabla
defined by
h(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{X}Y,Z)=-h(\nabla_{X}Y,Z)+h([X,Y],Z)-h([X,Z],Y)-h([Y,Z],X)+\mathfrak{L}_{X}h(Y,Z)+\mathfrak{L}_{Y}h(X,Z)-\mathfrak{L}_{Z}h(X,Y)
for
X,Y,Z\in\Gamma(TM)
.
Alternatively,

(8.3)

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla-\nabla=h^{kp}\left(\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+\nabla_{j}h_{ip}-\nabla_{p}h_{ij}\right)-\tau_{ij}\,^{k}+2h_{q(i}\tau_{j)p}\,^{q}h^{pk},

The interpretation of (
8.3
) is that the opposite connection of a torsion-free
\nabla
is the reflection of
\nabla
through the Levi-Civita connection of
h
. It follows from (
8.3
) that opposition is an involution in the sense that
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla)=\nabla
. Note that
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
.
By (
8.3
), the torsion
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\tau_{ij}\,^{k}
is given by
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\tau_{ij}\,^{k}=-\tau_{ij}\,^{k}
. In particular, the
h
-opposite connection of a torsion-free connection is again torsion-free.

Lemma 8.1
.

For an affine connection
\nabla
and a pseudo-Riemannian metric
h
on
M
any two of the following implies the other two:

(1)

\nabla
is torsion-free;

(2)

\bar{\nabla}
is torsion-free;

(3)

\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=0
;

(4)

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla=\bar{\nabla}
.

Proof.

Combining (
8.1
), (
8.3
), and (
8.2
) shows

(8.4)

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla-\bar{\nabla}=h^{kq}\left(2\nabla_{[j}h_{q]i}-\tau_{ijq}+\tau_{jqi}+\tau_{iqj}\right)=h^{kq}\left(\bar{\tau}_{jqi}-\tau_{ijq}-\tau_{qij}\right),

where
\tau_{ijk}=\tau_{ij}\,^{p}h_{pk}
and
\bar{\tau}_{ijk}=\bar{\tau}_{ij}\,^{p}h_{pk}
. That any two of (
1
)-(
3
) implies the third is immediate from (
8.2
). That in these cases there holds (
4
) follows from (
8.4
). That (
1
) and (
4
) imply (
3
) is immediate from the first equality of (
8.2
) and, in this case, the second equality of (
4
) implies that
\bar{\nabla}
is torsion-free so there holds (
2
).
If there holds (
2
) then
2\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=-\tau_{ijk}
, so
\tau_{[ijk]}=0
. In (
8.4
) this yields
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla-\bar{\nabla}=h^{kp}\tau_{jpi}=-2h^{kp}\nabla_{[j}h_{p]i}
from which it follows that (
2
) and (
4
) imply (
1
) and (
3
).
If there hold (
3
) and (
4
) then by (
8.4
) there holds
0=-\tau_{jki}+\tau_{ijk}+\tau_{kij}
. Antisymmetrizing this shows
\tau_{[jki]}=0
which yields
\tau_{jki}=\tau_{ijk}+\tau_{kij}=-\tau_{jki}
so that
\nabla
is torsion-free. By (
8.2
),
\bar{\nabla}
is torsion-free as well. This shows (
3
) and (
4
) imply (
1
) and (
2
).
∎

A
statistical structure
is a pair
(\nabla,h)
comprising a torsion-free affine connection,
\nabla
, and a pseudo-Riemannian metric,
h_{ij}
, such that
\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=0
. Statistical structures are also called
Codazzi structures
, for example in
[
126
]
. For background, in addition to
[
126
]
see
[
1
,
6
,
91
,
107
]
.

By Lemma
8.1
, if
(\nabla,h)
is a statistical structure, then
(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla,h)=(\bar{\nabla},h)
is also a statistical structure, the
conjugate statistical structure
. Conjugation is an involution on the space of statistical structures with a fixed underlying metric.

A statistical structure is
special
if
0=h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq}=\nabla_{i}|\det h|
.
Since
\bar{\nabla}_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
, a statistical structure is special if and only if the conjugate statistical structure is special.
Because
|\det h|^{-1}\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}|\det h|=-R_{ijp}\,^{p}=2R_{[ij]}
, a special statistical structure has symmetric Ricci tensor.

Next there are examined conditions of compatibility between a radiant structure and a pseudo-Riemannian metric. These conditions extend notions of compatibility for a
flat
affine connection and a metric usually called Hessian metrics or affine Kähler metrics as in
[
60
]
. The related literature is large. A recent survey of some aspects is
[
28
]
.

The homogeneity condition
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h=2h
, or the stronger condition that the metric
h
has homogeneity
2
with respect to the flow of
\mathbb{E}
, plays a role in the treatment of the ambient metric in
[
52
]
and
[
120
]
. Definition
8.2
adapts terminology introduced in
[
120
]
.

Definition 8.2
.

A pair
(h,\mathbb{E})
comprising a pseudo-Riemannian metric
h
and a vector field
\mathbb{E}
is a
self-similar metric structure
if
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h=2h
.

By definition, the vector field of a self-similar metric structure
(h,\mathbb{E})
is conformally Killing for
h
. A special case of Lemma
3.9
yields

(8.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{p}h_{ij}

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij},

from which it follows that a pair
(h,\mathbb{E})
is self-similar if and only if
\nabla_{\mathbb{E}}h=0
.

A radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
and a metric
h_{ij}
determine the one-form
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}h_{pi}
and the function
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
. Note that if
h
is of indefinite signature, then it can be that
v
vanishes even though
\mathbb{E}
is nonsingular, for it could be that
\mathbb{E}
is
h
-null.
Direct computations yield

(8.6)

\displaystyle dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}+\mathbb{E}^{a}\mathbb{E}^{b}\nabla_{i}h_{ab},

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=2\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}+2\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{j}h_{ip}+\mathbb{E}^{a}\mathbb{E}^{b}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}h_{ab},

which suggest investigating conditions implying
dv=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
and
\nabla dv=2\nabla\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, let
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
be a radiant structure, let
h_{ij}
be a pseudo-Riemannian metric, and let
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
be the opposite connection of
\nabla
. Define
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}h_{pi}
. There hold:

(8.7)

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-h_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{i}h_{jp}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{[i}h_{p]j}+\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{p}h_{ij}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{[i}h_{p]j}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij},

(8.8)

\displaystyle d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}

\displaystyle=2\nabla_{[i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j]}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{[i}h_{j]p},

(8.9)

\displaystyle h_{jp}(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{p}-\delta_{i}\,^{p})

\displaystyle=d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij},

(8.10)

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}-h_{ij}

\displaystyle=-(\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}-h_{ij})+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij}.

In particular:

(1)

\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is closed if
\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=0
.

(2)

Any two of the following imply the third:

(a)

(h,\mathbb{E})
is self-similar;

(b)

\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is closed;

(c)

(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure.

If these hold,
2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=dv_{i}
where
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
.

(3)

Any two of the following imply the third:

(a)

(h,\mathbb{E})
is self-similar;

(b)

\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}=h_{ij}
;

(c)

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}=h_{ij}
.

Proof.

Direct computation and (
8.5
) yield (
8.7
). Antisymmetrizing the first equality of (
8.7
) gives (
8.8
).
Claim (
1
) follows from (
8.8
).
Computations using
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
show

(8.11)

\displaystyle h_{jp}(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{p}-\delta_{i}\,^{p})

\displaystyle=h_{jp}\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{p}-h_{ij}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{[i}h_{j]p}+\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{p}h_{ij}=d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij},

which is (
8.9
). From (
8.9
) it is apparent that any two of (
2a
)-(
2c
) imply the third. In the case there hold (
2a
)-(
2c
), then, since
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is closed, by (
8.8
) and (
8.6
),
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}+\mathbb{E}^{a}\mathbb{E}^{b}\nabla_{a}h_{ib}=\mathbb{E}^{p}(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ip}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
.
Computations using (
8.7
), (
8.9
), and
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
yield

(8.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-h_{ij}&=\mathbb{E}^{p}\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+h_{jp}\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{p}-h_{ij}\\
&=-\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij}=d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}-(\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-h_{ij})+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij}.\end{split}

Symmetrizing (
8.12
) yields (
8.10
).
From (
8.10
) it is apparent that any two of (
3a
)-(
3c
) imply the third.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

If a compact manifold
M
admits a nonsingular radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
and a metric
h_{ij}
such that
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}h_{pi}
is closed, then
M
is a fiber bundle over
S^{1}
. Moreover,
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is the pullback of the generator of
H^{1}(S^{1};\mathbb{R})
if and only if
\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

By
[
130
, Theorem
1
]
a compact manifold admitting a nowhere vanishing closed one-form is a fiber bundle over
S^{1}
. The second claim is
[
51
, Lemma
2.1
]
.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

A manifold admitting a radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
and a metric
h_{ij}
such that the function
v=\mathbb{E}^{a}\mathbb{E}^{b}h_{ab}
satisfies
\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}
is not compact.

Proof.

By the nondegeneracy of
h
,
v
is a Morse function and all its critical points have the same index, but if
M
were compact, then
v
would have both a maximum and a minimum.
∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
be a radiant structure on
M
. Let
h
be a metric and define
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
. If there holds
\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}=h_{ij}
, then
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
if and only if
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
, in which case
\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}
.

Proof.

From (
8.6
) and (
8.8
) there follows

(8.13)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}^{\flat})_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{a}d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ai}+dv_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{a}\mathbb{E}^{b}\nabla_{a}h_{bi}+2\mathbb{E}_{i}^{\flat}=\mathbb{E}^{p}(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{pi}.

From (
8.13
) it follows that the condition
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h=2h
implies
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
.
It follows from the definition of
v
,
\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{j}=\delta_{i}\,^{j}
, and (
8.13
) that

(8.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}2\mathbb{E}^{p}(\nabla_{(i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j)}-h_{ip})&=\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}+\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}-2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\\
&=dv_{i}-\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{p}+(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}^{\flat})_{i}-3\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=(dv_{i}-2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i})+((\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}^{\flat})_{i}-2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}).\end{split}

from which the claimed equivalence is evident.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

On a smooth manifold,
M
, let
(h,\mathbb{E})
be a self-similar metric structure such that
d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}=0
.
For a torsion-free affine connection,
\nabla
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure and
\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
.

(2)

(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure and
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
.

When these conditions hold:

(1)

The function
v=h(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
satisfies
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
and so also
2h_{ij}=2\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}dv_{j}
.

(2)

The curvatures
R_{ijk}\,^{l}
of
\nabla
and
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}R_{ijk}\,^{l}
of
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
satisfy

(8.15)

\displaystyle R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=-2\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k},

\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=-2\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=2\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}.

(3)

On
\hat{M}=\{p\in M:v(p)\neq 0\}
, the closed one-form
\beta_{i}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}dv_{i}=v^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
satisfies
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\beta)_{i}=0
and
\beta(\mathbb{E})=1
. The restriction to
\hat{M}
of the metric
h_{ij}
has the form

(8.16)

\displaystyle h_{ij}=v\left(\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\right)=v\left(\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\right).

Proof.

The equivalence of (
1
) and (
2
) follows from (
3
) of Lemma
8.3
, (
8.12
), and the observation that antisymmetrizing
\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
implies
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is closed.
If there hold (
1
) and (
2
) then
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{a}\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{a}=2\mathbb{E}^{a}h_{ia}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
. By the Ricci identity,
R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=-2\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}=-2\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}
and similarly with
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
in place of
\nabla
. The first equality of (
8.16
) follows from

(8.17)

\displaystyle v\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}=\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-dv_{i}\beta_{j}.

Since
\beta_{i}\mathbb{E}^{i}=v^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=1
and
d\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{ij}=0
, skewing (
8.17
) shows
v(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\beta)_{i}=v\mathbb{E}^{p}d\beta_{pi}=0
.
The claims for
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
follow by taking
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
in place of
\nabla
.
∎

Definition 8.8
.

A
radiant Hessian structure
is a triple
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
such that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure,
(h,\mathbb{E})
is a self-similar metric structure, and
\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
.

The definition of a radiant Hessian structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
implies that
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
is closed. By Lemma
8.5
, a manifold admitting a radiant Hessian structure is not compact.
This conclusion is analogous to the statement that a complex Hopf surface admits no Kähler structure.

By Lemma
8.7
, if
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant Hessian structure, then
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
satisfies
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
and
\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}
, and
(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is also a radiant Hessian structure, the
opposite
radiant Hessian structure.

Lemma 8.9
.

On a smooth manifold,
M
, let
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
be a radiant Hessian structure with opposite radiant Hessian structure
(\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
. Let
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
and let
\beta_{i}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}dv_{i}
where
v\neq 0
.
For
t\in\mathbb{R}
the connection

(8.18)

\displaystyle\nabla(t)=(1-t)\nabla+t\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla=\nabla+th^{kp}\left(\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+2\nabla_{[j}h_{p]i}\right)

satisfies
\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla(t)_{i}dv_{j}=\nabla(t)_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
.
In particular, the Levi-Civita connection,
D=\nabla(1/2)
of
h_{ij}
satisfies
D_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=h_{ij}
. In this case, around any point
p\in M
at which
v\neq 0
there is an open neighborhood
U
such that the pseudo-Riemannian manifold
(U,h)
is isometric to a warped product metric of the form

(8.19)

\displaystyle\epsilon dr\otimes dr+r^{2}\pi^{\ast}(g)

on
(a,b)\times\Gamma
where
\Gamma=\{q\in U:v(q)=v(p)\}
,
\epsilon=\operatorname{sgn}(v(p))
,
\pi:(a,b)\times\Gamma\to\Gamma
is the projection on the second factor,
r=\operatorname{sgn}(v)|v|^{1/2}
, and the metric
\epsilon g_{ij}
is the restriction to
\Gamma
of the tensor

(8.20)

\displaystyle\nabla(t)_{i}\beta_{j}+\beta_{i}\beta_{j}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}\nabla(t)_{i}dv_{j}-\tfrac{1}{4}v^{-1}dv_{i}dv_{j}=\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla(t)_{i}d\log{|v|}_{j},

which does not depend on
t
.

Proof.

By (
8.3
),
\nabla(t)=\nabla+th^{kp}\left(\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+2\nabla_{[j}h_{p]i}\right)
, and so,

(8.21)

\displaystyle\nabla(t)_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=\nabla_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-t\mathbb{E}^{p}\left(\nabla_{i}h_{jp}+2\nabla_{[j}h_{p]i}\right)=h_{ij},

the final equality by (
8.5
) and (
8.7
) of Lemma
8.3
. Hence
(\nabla(t),\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant Hessian structure for all
t
. In particular,
\nabla(t)_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}
and
\nabla(t)_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}=vh_{ij}
for all
t
.

That
D_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}
implies that
(M,h)
is locally isometric to a warped product is well-known. See
[
30
, pages
192-194
]
for the proof of a more general statement in the Riemannian setting. As in the applications considered here the signature of
h_{ij}
is indefinite, this is briefly sketched so as to get straight the signs. Also it is useful to know the relations between
\beta
,
v
,
r
, and
h
.

Let
U
be an open neighborhood of
p
on which the flow
\phi_{t}
of
\mathbb{E}
is defined for all
t
in some interval
(a,b)
containing
0
and
v
does not vanish and set
\epsilon=\operatorname{sgn}(v(p))
. Define
r=\epsilon|v|^{1/2}
on
U
.
Define
\tilde{g}_{ij}=\epsilon(\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+\beta_{i}\beta_{j})
where
\epsilon=\operatorname{sgn}(v(p))
. Because
\nabla(t)_{i}dv_{j}
and
\nabla(t)_{i}\beta_{j}
do not depend on
t
,
\tilde{g}_{ij}
equals the expressions in (
8.20
). By Lemma
8.7
,

(8.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}h_{ij}&=v(\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j})=\epsilon v\tilde{g}_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{4}v^{-1}dv_{i}dv_{j}=r^{2}\tilde{g}_{ij}+\epsilon dr_{i}dr_{j}.\end{split}

Note that
dv_{i}=2rdr_{i}
and
dr(\mathbb{E})=r
, so that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}r=r
. From (
8.22
) there follows
0=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h-2h=r^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{g}
, so that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{g}=0
. Define
g_{ij}
to be the restriction to
\Gamma=\{q\in U:v(q)=v(p)\}
of
\tilde{g}_{ij}
. Restricting (
8.22
) to
\Gamma
shows that along
\Gamma
there holds (
8.19
), and the preceding shows that
\phi_{t}^{\ast}(\tilde{g})_{q}=g_{q}
for all
q\in\Gamma
, or, what is the same
\pi^{\ast}(g)=\tilde{g}
on
U
. Thus the map
\Psi:(a,b)\times\Gamma\to M
defined by
\Psi(t,q)=\phi_{t}(q)
satisfies

(8.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi^{\ast}(h)&=\Psi^{\ast}(\epsilon v\tilde{g}+\tfrac{1}{4}v^{-1}dv\otimes dv)=\epsilon v_{0}e^{2t}\phi_{t}^{\ast}(\tilde{g})+v_{0}e^{2t}dt\otimes dt\\
&=\epsilon v_{0}e^{2t}\left(\pi^{\ast}(g)+\epsilon dt\otimes dt\right)=r^{2}\pi^{\ast}(g)+\epsilon dr\otimes dr,\end{split}

where
v(\phi_{t}(q))=v_{0}e^{2t}
and
r(\phi_{t}(q))=\epsilon\sqrt{|v_{0}|}e^{t}
.
∎

Lemma 8.10
.

For a triple
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
such that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure and
(\nabla,h)
is a statistical structure, the
h
-conjugate connection
\bar{\nabla}
forms with
\mathbb{E}
a radiant structure if and only if
(h,\mathbb{E})
is a self-similar metric structure.
In this case,
(\bar{\nabla},h)
is a statistical structure.

Proof.

If
(\nabla,h)
is statistical, then, by (
2
) of Lemma
8.3
,
\bar{\nabla}=\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
, and by (
3
) of Lemma
8.3
,
\,\!{}^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla=\bar{\nabla}
forms with
\mathbb{E}
a radiant structure if and only if
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h=2h
. In this case, because
\bar{\nabla}_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
,
(\bar{\nabla},h)
is statistical.
∎

Definition 8.11
.

A
radiant statistical structure
is a triple
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
such that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is a radiant structure,
(\nabla,h)
is a statistical structure, and
(h,\mathbb{E})
is a self-similar metric structure.

If
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant statistical structure and
\bar{\nabla}
is the
h
-conjugate connection of
\nabla
, then by Lemma
8.10
,
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
is also a radiant statistical structure, called the
conjugate
radiant statistical structure.

A radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant Hessian structure, and in this case
\bar{\nabla}=\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla
, so the opposite radiant Hessian structure is the conjugate radiant statistical structure.

A radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is
special
if the underlying statistical structure
(\nabla,h)
is special; it is
conelike
if the underlying radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
is conelike.

On an oriented manifold
M
, a radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
determines a radiant Euler structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},\mathsf{vol}_{h})
, where
\mathsf{vol}_{h}
is the volume form determined by
h
and the given orientation of
M
. That
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h=2h
implies
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\det h=2n\det h
, and it follows that
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{vol}_{h}=n\mathsf{vol}_{n}
.

Similarly, if on an oriented manifold
M
, the radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is special, then
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},\mathsf{vol}_{h})
is an equiaffine Euler structure.

Example 22
.

If
D
is the Levi-Civita connection of a pseudo-Riemannian metric
g_{ij}
and
X^{i}
is a concurrent vector field, meaning
D_{i}X_{j}^{\flat}=g_{ij}
where
X_{j}^{\flat}=X^{p}g_{pj}
, then
(\mathfrak{L}_{X}g)_{ij}=2D_{(i}X_{j)}^{\flat}=2g_{ij}
, so
(D,X,g)
is a radiant statistical structure.

The self-conjugate radiant statistical structures, those for which
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)=(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
, are exactly those for which
\nabla
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
and
\mathbb{E}
is a concurrent vector field.

Corollary 8.12

(Corollary of Lemma
8.7
)
.

On a smooth manifold,
M
, for a radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
there hold:

(1)

The function
v=h(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
satisfies
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
and
\nabla_{i}dv_{j}=2h_{ij}=\,\!^{\mathsf{op}}\nabla_{i}dv_{j}
.

(2)

The curvature
R_{ijk}\,^{l}
of
\nabla
satisfies
R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=0
.

(3)

On
\hat{M}=\{p\in M:v(p)\neq 0\}
, the closed one-form
\beta_{i}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}dv_{i}=v^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
satisfies
(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\beta)_{i}=0
and
\beta(\mathbb{E})=1
. The restriction to
\hat{M}
of the metric
h_{ij}
has the form
h_{ij}=v\left(\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\right)
.

All the same statements hold for the conjugate radiant statistical structure
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
.

Example 23
.

By Propositions
2.4
and
3.4
of Fefferman and Graham’s
[
52
]
, a pre-ambient space that is
straight
constitutes a radiant statistical structure for which
dv_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
. Here
\mathbb{E}^{i}
is the vector field called
T
in
[
52
]
, that generates dilations by
s^{2}
in the fibers of the bundle of metrics.

A statistical structure
(\nabla,h)
is
equiaffine
if there is a nonvanishing volume density
\Psi\in\Gamma(|\operatorname{Det}T^{\ast}M|)
such that
\nabla\Psi=0
. In this definition, no relation is supposed between
\Psi
and
h
. A radiant statistical structure is equiaffine if its underlying radiant structure is equiaffine.

For a statistical structure
(\nabla,h)
, define
R_{ijkl}=R_{ijk}\,^{p}h_{pl}
. The curvature tensor and those derived from it associated with the conjugate statistical structure
(\bar{\nabla},h)
are decorated with
\bar{\,\cdot\,\,}
.

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
(\nabla,h)
be a statistical structure with conjugate statistical structure
(\bar{\nabla},h)
.

(1)

The curvature and Ricci tensors
R_{ijkl}=R_{ijk}\,^{p}h_{pl}
,
\bar{R}_{ijkl}=\bar{R}_{ijk}\,^{p}h_{pl}
,
R_{ij}
, and
\bar{R}_{ij}
satisfy

(8.24)

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{ijkl}=-R_{ijlk},

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{ij}=R_{ipqj}h^{pq},

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{[ij]}=-R_{[ij]},

\displaystyle h^{ij}\bar{R}_{ij}=h^{ij}R_{ij}.

(2)

(\bar{\nabla},h)
is Ricci-flat if and only if
h^{pq}R_{ipq}\,^{j}=0
.

(3)

(\bar{\nabla},h)
has symmetric Ricci tensor if and only if
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
has symmetric Ricci tensor.

(4)

(\nabla,h)
is equiaffine for the volume density
\Psi
if and only if
(\bar{\nabla},h)
is equiaffine for the volume density
A\Psi
where
\det h=A\Psi^{\otimes 2}
(so
h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq}=A^{-1}dA_{i}
).

(5)

The following are equivalent:

(a)

(\bar{\nabla},h)
is special;

(b)

(\nabla,h)
is special;

(c)

(\bar{\nabla},h)
and
(\nabla,h)
are simultaneously equiaffine for the same volume density
\Psi
.

If these hold,
\det h=c\Psi^{\otimes 2}
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
.

(6)

If
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant statistical structure with conjugate radiant statistical structure
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
, then

(8.25)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{i}=-\rho_{i},

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}_{p}^{\flat}=0,

\displaystyle R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}_{p}^{\flat}=0.

Proof.

Since
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}=\bar{\nabla}-\nabla=h^{kp}\nabla_{i}h_{jp}=h^{kp}\nabla_{p}h_{ij}
, the curvature tensors are related by

(8.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{R}_{ijkl}&=R_{ijkl}+2h_{lp}\nabla_{[i}\Pi_{j]k}\,^{p}-2h_{lq}\Pi_{p[i}\,^{q}\Pi_{j]k}\,^{p}\\
&=R_{ijkl}+2\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}h_{kl}+2h^{pq}\left(\nabla_{[j}h_{q]l}\nabla_{i}h_{kp}-\nabla_{[i}h_{q]l}\nabla_{j}h_{kp}\right)=-R_{ijlk}.\end{split}

the last equality by the Ricci identity and the statistical property. Tracing (
8.26
) shows
\bar{R}_{ij}=R_{ipqj}h^{pq}
.
By the algebraic Bianchi identity and (
8.26
),
-2\bar{R}_{[ij]}=\bar{R}_{ijp}\,^{p}=-R_{ijp}\,^{p}=2R_{[ij]}
. Claim (
2
) is immediate from (
8.24
). By (
8.24
),
\bar{\nabla}
is Ricci symmetric if and only if
\nabla
is Ricci symmetric. This shows (
3
).

Given a volume density,
\Psi
, there is a nonvanishing
A\in C^{\infty}(M)
such that
\det h=A\Psi^{\otimes 2}
. If
\nabla\Psi=0
, then

(8.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq})A\Psi^{\otimes 2}&=(h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq})\det h=\nabla_{i}\det h=dA_{i}\Psi^{\otimes 2},\end{split}

so
h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq}=A^{-1}dA_{i}
. By (
8.1
),

(8.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\nabla}_{i}(A\Psi)=\nabla_{i}(A\Psi)-(h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq})A\Psi=(dA_{i}-Ah^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq})\Psi=0.\end{split}

This shows (
4
). It follows that
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
are both equiaffine for the same volume density
\Psi
if and only if
A
is constant, which holds if and only if
h^{pq}\nabla_{i}h_{pq}=A^{-1}dA_{i}=0
, that is,
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is special. Because
\bar{\nabla}_{i}h_{jk}=-\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
,
h^{jk}\bar{\nabla}_{i}h_{jk}=0
if and only if
h^{jk}\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=0
. This shows (
5
).

If
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant statistical structure with conjugate
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
, contracting (
8.24
) with
\mathbb{E}^{j}
yields
\bar{\rho}_{i}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{R}_{[pi]}=2\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{[ip]}=-\rho_{i}
. From (
8.26
) there follows
\bar{R}_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=-R_{ijpk}\mathbb{E}^{p}=0
, the last equality by (
3.16
). By duality,
R_{ijk}\,^{p}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{p}=0
too. This shows (
8.25
).
∎

A radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is
nonsingular
if
\mathbb{E}^{i}
is nonsingular.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
be a nonsingular radiant statistical structure with conjugate radiant statistical structure
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
.
Then
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
are both conelike if and only if
\nabla
and
\bar{\nabla}
are both
\mathbb{E}
-invariant (equivalently,
\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{R}_{pijk}=0
and
\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pijk}=0
).
In this case,
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
have the same planelike surfaces if and only if
\nabla=\bar{\nabla}
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
(in which case
\mathbb{E}
is a concurrent vector field).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2
,
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
are both conelike if and only if there are
Q_{ij},\bar{Q}_{ij}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M)
such that
\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}Q_{pq}=0
,
\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}\bar{Q}_{pq}=0
, and

(8.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pijk}=Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}-2q_{(i}h_{j)k},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{R}_{pijk}=\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}-2\bar{q}_{(i}h_{j)k}

where
q_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}Q_{ip}
and
\bar{q}_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{Q}_{ip}
.
If this is the case, then by (
8.26
),

(8.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{Q}_{ij}v-2\bar{q}_{(i}h_{j)k}=\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{R}_{pijk}=-\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pikj}=-Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}+2q_{(i}h_{k)j},\end{split}

so that

(8.31)

\displaystyle\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}+Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=(\bar{q}_{i}+q_{i})h_{jk}+\bar{q}_{j}h_{ki}+q_{k}h_{ji}.

Tracing (
8.31
) in
jk
gives
(n+1)(q_{i}+\bar{q}_{i})=0
where
n=\dim M
. Hence
\bar{q}_{i}=-q_{i}
and (
8.31
) becomes

(8.32)

\displaystyle\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}+Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=-q_{j}h_{ki}+q_{k}h_{ji}.

Tracing (
8.32
) in
ij
and in
ik
gives
h^{ij}\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}=(n-1)q_{k}
and
h^{ik}Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=(1-n)q_{j}
, so that
q_{i}=f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
where
-h^{pq}\bar{Q}_{pq}=h^{pq}Q_{pq}=(1-n)f
. Hence
0=\mathbb{E}^{p}q_{p}=fv
where
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
. In (
8.32
) this yields

(8.33)

\displaystyle\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}+Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=f(\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}h_{ji}-\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}h_{ki}).

Contracting (
8.33
) with
\mathbb{E}^{k}
and
\mathbb{E}^{j}
gives
v\bar{Q}_{ij}=fvh_{ij}-f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=-f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
and
vQ_{ik}=f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}-fvh_{ik}=f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}
, so that
v\bar{Q}_{ij}=-f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=-vQ_{ik}
. Wherever
v\neq 0
,
f
vanishes and so
Q_{ij}=0
and
\bar{Q}_{ij}=0
. Where
v=0
it follows that
f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=0
. Because
\mathbb{E}^{i}
is nonsingular, for
p\in M
there is
w^{i}\in T_{p}M_{\ast}
such that
\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}w^{i}=1
, and so
0=f\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}w^{i}w^{j}=f
at
p
. This shows
f
vanishes identically. Consequently
q_{i}
and
\bar{q}_{i}
also vanish identically. In (
8.33
) this yields

(8.34)

\displaystyle\bar{Q}_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}+Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=0.

Also
v\bar{Q}_{ij}=-vQ_{ik}=0
. From (
8.34
) it follows that
Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}=Q_{jk}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=Q_{ki}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
. The relation
Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}=Q_{ik}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
implies that there is a one-form
\alpha_{i}
such that
Q_{ij}=\alpha_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
. Since
Q_{[ij]}=0
, there is
z\in C^{\infty}(M)
such that
\alpha_{i}=z\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
on
M
. This shows
Q_{ij}=z\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
and from (
8.34
) it follows that
\bar{Q}_{ij}=-z\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}=-Q_{ij}
. It follows that
0=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}Q_{pq}=zv^{2}
, so that
z
vanishes wherever
v
does not. Since, by Lemma
8.7
,
dv=\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}
, and
\mathbb{E}^{i}
is by assumption nonvanishing, the zero set of
v
is contained in a union of codimension one submanifolds of
M
; because
z
is smooth and vanishes off this union,
z
vanishes identically. This proves that
Q_{ij}=0
and
\bar{Q}_{ij}=0
. In (
8.29
) there results
\mathbb{E}^{p}R_{pijk}=0
and
\mathbb{E}^{p}\bar{R}_{pijk}=0
, so that both
\nabla
and
\bar{\nabla}
are
\mathbb{E}
-invariant.

Now suppose
\mathbb{E}
is nonsingular and the conjugate radiant statistical structures
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E},h)
are conelike and have the same planelike surfaces. By Lemma
5.2
and (
8.2
) there is
Q_{ij}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M)
satisfying
\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}Q_{pq}=0
and such that
h^{kp}\nabla_{i}h_{jp}=\bar{\nabla}-\nabla=Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{k}-2q_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
where
q_{i}=\mathbb{E}^{p}Q_{ip}
. Hence
0=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}h)_{ij}-2h_{ij}=\mathbb{E}^{p}\nabla_{p}h_{ij}=\mathbb{E}^{p}(Q_{pi}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}-q_{p}h_{ij}-q_{i}h_{pj})=-q_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
. Since
\mathbb{E}^{i}
is nonsingular this implies
q_{i}=0
, so that
\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}
. Since
\nabla_{i}h_{jk}
is completely symmetric, this implies
Q_{ij}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{k}=Q_{jk}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}=Q_{ki}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
. Arguing as in the previous paragraph, this implies there is
z\in C^{\infty}(M)
such that
Q_{ij}=z\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{i}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}_{j}
and
zv=0
. Again as in the previous paragraph, this implies
Q_{ij}=0
, so that
\nabla_{i}h_{jk}=0
and
\bar{\nabla}=\nabla
.
∎

9.
AH structures and Einstein equations for statistical structures

The notion of AH structure introduced and studied by the author in
[
56
,
57
,
58
,
59
,
60
]
is recalled because it provides a convenient language for results described in Section
10
that generalize known constructions associating Einstein-Weyl structures with constant scalar curvature Kähler metrics. (For background on Einstein-Weyl structures, consult the survey
[
24
]
.) What is needed is a generalization of the notion of Einstein-Weyl structure. Informally, AH structures are to statistical structures as Weyl structures are to metric structures. The definition of AH structure given here is stated in a different way than in
[
56
,
57
,
58
,
59
,
60
]
but the formulation given here, although equivalent, is more economical and for it more readable.

An
AH structure
on an
n
-dimensional manifold,
M
, is a pair
(\nabla,[g])
comprising a torsion-free affine connection,
\nabla
, and a conformal class
[g]
of pseudo-Riemannian metrics, that satisfy two compatibility conditions:

(1)

For each
g\in[g]
there is a one-form
\chi_{i}\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
such that
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\chi_{[i}g_{j]k}
.

(2)

The pair is
aligned
, meaning that for each
g\in[g]
there holds
g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}=ng^{pq}\nabla_{p}g_{qi}
.

It is straightforward to check that each of the two conditions is well-posed. If
\tilde{g}=e^{f}g
with
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
, then
\nabla_{i}\tilde{g}_{jk}=e^{f}(\nabla_{i}g_{jk}+df_{i}g_{jk})
. Tracing this in two ways shows
\tilde{g}^{pq}(\tilde{\nabla}_{i}\tilde{g}_{pq}-n\tilde{\nabla}_{p}\tilde{g}_{qi})=g^{pq}(\nabla_{i}g_{pq}-n\nabla_{p}g_{qi})
, so that the alignment condition is well-defined, while antisymmetrizing it in
ij
shows that if
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\chi_{[i}g_{j]k}
, then
\tilde{\nabla}_{[i}\tilde{g}_{j]k}=\tilde{\chi}_{[i}\tilde{g}_{j]k}
with
\tilde{\chi}_{i}=\chi_{i}+df_{i}
. Note that it follows that
d\chi_{ij}
does not depend on the choice of
g\in[g]
.

Remark 17
.

The letters
AH
abbreviate
affine hypersurface
; see
[
56
,
57
,
59
,
60
]
for motivation.

Remark 18
.

The conformal structure
[g]
can be identified with the scale-invariant weighted tensor
G_{ij}=|\det g|^{-1/n}g_{ij}
. The alignment condition is equivalent to the vanishing of all traces of the covariant derivative of
G_{ij}
,
G^{ij}\nabla_{i}G_{jk}=0
(because
|\det G|=1
,
G^{jk}\nabla_{i}G_{jk}=0
is automatic). In
[
56
,
57
,
59
]
everything is written in terms of
G_{ij}
; while economical and conceptually clean, it appears that this impedes understandability.

The curvature of an AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
means the curvature of
\nabla
.
For an AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
, the one-form
\chi_{i}
associated with
g\in[g]
is given by
\chi_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}g_{qi}=\tfrac{1}{n}g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}=\tfrac{1}{n}(\det g)^{-1}\nabla_{i}\det g
.
It follows that the curvature of
(\nabla,[g])
satisfies

(9.1)

\displaystyle nd\chi_{ij}=2n\nabla_{[i}\chi_{j]}=2(\det g)^{-1}\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}\det g=-2R_{ijp}\,^{p}=4R_{[ij]}.

Following
[
23
]
in the context of Weyl structures, define the
Faraday curvature

F_{ij}\in\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!2}}T^{\ast}M)
of the AH structure to be the curvature of the connection induced by
\nabla
on the line bundle of
-(1/n)
-densities. Because
|\det g|^{-1/(2n)}
is
-(1/n)
-density it follows from (
9.1
) that
F_{ij}=-\tfrac{1}{2}d\chi_{ij}
.

An AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
is
exact
if the one-form
\chi_{i}
associated with any
g\in[g]
is exact. Equivalently, there is
g\in[g]
such that the associated one-form
\chi_{i}
is identically zero. In this case
g
is a
distinguished
representative of
[g]
. A distinguished representative is determined only up to positive homothety. Because
\nabla_{i}g^{ij}=-\chi^{j}
, an AH structure is exact if and only if there is
g\in[g]
so that the divergence
\nabla_{i}g^{ij}
vanishes. An AH structure is
closed
if its Faraday curvature is identically zero (equivalently,
\chi
is closed for any
g\in[g]
). A closed AH structure is locally exact, but it need not be globally so.

By definition, a distinguished representative
g
of an exact AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
determines with
\nabla
a special statistical structure. Lemma
9.1
implies that a statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
generates an AH structure
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
and the AH structure generated by a statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
is exact if and only if
(\nabla,g)
is a special statistical structure. Consequently, an exact AH structure can be viewed as a positive homothety class of special statistical structures.

Lemma 9.1
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, if
(\nabla,g)
satisfies
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\gamma_{[i}g_{j]k}
for some one-form
\gamma_{i}
, then the connection
\tilde{\nabla}
defined by
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\sigma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
where
\sigma_{i}=\tfrac{1}{n+2}\left(g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}-n\gamma_{i}\right)
generates with the conformal structure
[g]
an AH structure, the AH structure
generated
by
(\nabla,g)
. Moreover, the AH structure generated by a statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
is exact with distinguished representative
g
if and only if
(\nabla,g)
is a special statistical structure.

Proof.

Suppose
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\sigma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
for some one-form
\sigma_{i}
. Straightforward computation using
\tilde{\nabla}_{[i}g_{j]k}=\gamma_{[i}g_{j]k}-\sigma_{[i}g_{j]k}
shows

(9.2)

\displaystyle g^{pq}(\tilde{\nabla}_{i}g_{pq}-n\tilde{\nabla}_{p}g_{qi})=g^{pq}(\nabla_{i}g_{pq}-n\nabla_{p}g_{qi})+(n-1)(n+2)\sigma_{i}.

Because
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\gamma_{[i}g_{j]k}
,
g^{pq}\nabla_{p}g_{qi}=g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}+(n-1)\gamma_{i}
, so from (
9.2
) there results
g^{pq}(\tilde{\nabla}_{i}g_{pq}-n\tilde{\nabla}_{p}g_{qi})=(n-1)((n+2)\sigma_{i}-g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}+n\gamma_{i})
, so that
\tilde{\nabla}
is aligned with respect to
[g]
and
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
is an AH structure if and only if
\sigma_{i}=\tfrac{1}{n+2}\left(g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}-n\gamma_{i}\right)
.

If
(\nabla,g)
is a special statistical structure, then
\gamma_{i}=0
and
g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}=0
, so
\sigma_{i}=0
,
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla
is exact, and
g
is a distinguished representative of the AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
.

If
(\nabla,g)
is a statistical structure, then
\gamma_{i}=0
by hypothesis, and the AH structure
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
it generates is determined by
\sigma_{i}=\tfrac{1}{n+2}g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}
. On the other hand, the Faraday primitive of
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
associated with
g
is
\chi_{i}=\tfrac{1}{n}g^{pq}\tilde{\nabla}_{i}g_{pq}=\tfrac{n+2}{n}\sigma_{i}
. It follows that if
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
is exact with distinguished representative
g
, then
\sigma_{i}=0
and
(\nabla,g)
is special.
∎

It follows from (
9.2
) that given a conformal structure
[g]
any torsion-free affine connection is projectively equivalent to a unique such connection that is aligned with respect to
[g]
.

Lemma
9.2
shows that an AH structure is the same thing as a
locally statistical structure
in the sense defined in its statement.

Lemma 9.2
.

For a pair
([\nabla],[g])
comprising a projective structure
[\nabla]
and conformal structure
[g]
on a manifold
M
the following are equivalent.

(1)

([\nabla],[g])
is
locally statistical
in the sense that every
p\in M
is contained in an open neighborhood
U\subset M
on which there is a representative
\nabla\in[\nabla]
(not necessarily aligned with respect to
[g]
) and a representative metric
g\in[g]
such that
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
on
U
, that is such that
(\nabla,g)
is a statistical structure on
U
.

(2)

For the aligned representative
\nabla\in[\nabla]
,
(\nabla,[g])
is an AH structure.

(3)

For any
g\in[g]
there is
\tilde{\nabla}\in[\nabla]
such that
(\tilde{\nabla},g)
is a statistical structure.

Proof.

Suppose
([\nabla],[g])
is locally statistical. Let
\nabla\in[\nabla]
be the unique representative aligned with respect to
[g]
and fix a reference metric
g\in[g]
. By assumption there is an open cover
\{U_{a}\}
of
M
such that on
U_{a}
there are a connection
\nabla(a)=\nabla+2\sigma(a)_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
and a metric
g(a)_{ij}=e^{f(a)}g_{ij}
representing the restrictions to
U_{a}
of
[\nabla]
and
[g]
and satisfying
0=\nabla(a)_{[i}g(a)_{j]k}
. Antisymmetrizing
\nabla(a)_{i}g(a)_{jk}=e^{f(a)}(\nabla_{i}g_{jk}+df(a)_{i}h_{jk}-2\sigma(a)_{i}g_{jk}-2\sigma(a)_{(j}g_{k)i})
yields

(9.3)

\displaystyle 0=\nabla(a)_{[i}g(a)_{j]k}=e^{f(a)}\left(\nabla_{[i}g_{jk]}+df(a)_{[i}g_{j]k}-\sigma(a)_{i}g_{jk}\right),

so that
\nabla_{[i}g_{jk]}=\tau(a)_{[i}g_{j]k}
where
\tau(a)_{i}=\sigma(a)_{i}-df(a)_{i}
. On the overlap
U_{a}\cap U_{b}
there holds
\tau(b)_{[i}g_{j]k}=\nabla_{[i}g_{jk]}=\tau(a)_{[i}g_{j]k}
. Tracing this in
jk
yields
\tau(a)_{i}=\tau(b)_{i}
so the one-forms
\tau(a)
patch together to yield a globally defined one-form
\tau
such that
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\tau_{[i}g_{j]k}
. This shows that (
1
) implies (
2
). If there holds (
2
), then, for any
g\in[g]
,
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\sigma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
satisfies
\tilde{\nabla}_{[i}g_{j]k}=\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}-\sigma_{[i}g_{j]k}=\tau_{[i}g_{j]k}-\sigma_{[i}g_{j]k}
, so
\tilde{\nabla}_{[i}g_{j]k}=\tilde{\tau}_{[i}g_{j]k}
with
\tilde{\tau}_{i}=\tau_{i}-\sigma_{i}
. This shows that (
2
) implies (
1
).
If
([\nabla],[g])
is an AH structure, then by definition,
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\chi_{[i}g_{j]k}
. The connection
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla+2\chi_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}
then satisfies
\tilde{\nabla}_{[i}h_{j]k}=0
. This shows that (
2
) implies (
3
). It is immediate that (
3
) implies (
1
).
∎

Lemma 9.3
.

A radiant statistical structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
on
M
is special if and only if
(\nabla,[g])
is an AH structure on
\hat{M}=\{p\in M:\mathbb{E}_{p}\neq 0\}
, where
g_{ij}=v^{-1}h_{ij}
with
v=\mathbb{E}^{p}\mathbb{E}^{q}h_{pq}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.12
, if
(\nabla,\mathbb{E},h)
is a radiant statistical structure, then
g_{ij}=v^{-1}h_{ij}=\nabla_{i}\beta_{j}+2\beta_{i}\beta_{j}
, where
\beta_{i}=(1/2)v^{-1}dv_{i}
, satisfies
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}\beta_{k}-2\beta_{[i}\nabla_{j]}\beta_{k}=-\tfrac{1}{2}R_{ijk}\,^{p}\beta_{p}-2\beta_{[i}g_{j]k}=-2\beta_{[i}g_{j]k}
, so, by Lemma
9.1
,
\nabla
and
g_{ij}
generate an AH structure
(\tilde{\nabla},[g])
on
\hat{M}
. Moreover, by the proof of Lemma
9.1
,
\tilde{\nabla}=\nabla
if and only if
g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}=-2n\beta_{i}=-nv^{-1}dv_{i}
. This last condition is equivalent to
v^{n}\det g=\det h
being
\nabla
-parallel on
\hat{M}
, and because
\hat{M}
is open and dense in
M
, this holds if and only if
\det h
is
\nabla
-parallel on
M
.
∎

Remark 19
.

Note that given
(\nabla,[g])
as in Lemma
9.1
there is no way to reconstruct
h
without the additional data of a function
v
such that
v^{n}\det g
is
\nabla
-parallel. Constructing such a function from
\nabla
and
g
amounts to solving a Monge-Ampère equation.

Given an AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
and
g\in[g]
with associated one-form
\chi_{i}
, the tensor
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
defined by

(9.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{ijk}=\nabla_{i}g_{jk}-\chi_{i}g_{jk}

is completely symmetric and completely
g
-trace-free. It is the
cubic form
of
\nabla
with respect to the representative
g\in[g]
. The tensor
\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{k}=g^{kp}\mathcal{L}_{ijp}
does not depend on the choice of
g\in[g]
and is called the
cubic torsion
of
(\nabla,[g])
. It is straightforward to check that the connection
\wideparen{\nabla}=\nabla+\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{k}
is aligned with respect to
[g]
and generates with
[g]
an AH structure having cubic torsion
\wideparen{\mathcal{L}}_{ij}\,^{k}=-\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{k}
. The AH structure
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
is said to be
conjugate
to
(\nabla,[g])
. Conjugacy is an involution on the space of AH structures. The curvature of
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
and the tensors derived from it are indicated by decoration with
\wideparen{\,\cdot\,\,}
, as in
\wideparen{R}_{ijk}\,^{l}
.

Remark 20
.

The AH structure generated by the statistical structure conjugate to a given statistical structure is not equal to the AH structure conjugate to the AH structure generated by the given statistical structure unless the given statistical structure is special. In general the resulting conjugate connections differ by a tensor of the form
2\sigma_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}-g_{ij}\sigma^{k}
, where
\sigma_{i}
is a multiple of the covariant derivative of
\det g
.

Example 24
.

A
Weyl structure
is a pair
(\nabla,[g])
comprising a torsion-free affine connection,
\nabla
, and a conformal class
[g]
of pseudo-Riemannian metrics such that, for each
g\in[g]
, there is a one-form
\chi_{i}\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
such that
\nabla_{i}g_{jk}=\chi_{i}g_{jk}
. By definition a Weyl structure is an AH structure with vanishing cubic form and the Weyl structures are exactly those AH structures that are self-conjugate, equivalently, that have vanishing cubic form.

Example 25
.

Although the language used here is a bit different, the geometric content of this example is fully present in H. Matsuzoe’s
[
107
,
108
]
. For details of the claims that follow in the language used here see
[
56
,
57
,
59
]
. The Blaschke metric
h
of a cooriented nondegenerate hypersurface immersion in flat affine space constitutes with the connection
\nabla
induced via the affine normal a special statistical structure, so generates an exact AH structure for which
h
is a distinguished metric. The pullback via the conormal Gauss map of the flat projective structure on the projectivization of the vector space dual to the ambient flat affine space yields a projective structure that has a unique representative
\wideparen{\nabla}
aligned with respect to the equiaffine metric
h
, and the AH structure
(\wideparen{\nabla},[h])
it generates with
h
is that conjugate to
(\nabla,[h])
.

Remark 21
.

H. Matsuzoe
[
108
]
called
semi-Weyl
a pair
(\nabla,g)
satisfying the first condition (
1
) and observed the relation with geometric structures induced on hypersurfaces in flat affine space described in Example
25
. The alignment condition (
2
) seems to be important for properly formulating the Einstein equations defined later in Definition
9.7
. Matsuzoe also defined the cubic form of
(\nabla,g)
by (
9.4
). However, without the alignment condition,
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
need not be trace-free. On the other hand, in Matsuzoe’s setting, the vanishing of
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
implies the alignment condition, so this condition is somehow hidden from view.

The
scalar curvature
of the AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
associated with the representative
g\in[g]
is the function
\mathit{s}=g^{pq}R_{pq}
.

Lemma 9.4
.

For
g\in[g]
with
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=\chi_{[i}g_{j]k}
, the curvatures
R_{ijkl}=R_{ijk}\,^{p}g_{pl}
and
\wideparen{R}_{ijkl}=\wideparen{R}_{ijk}\,^{p}g_{pl}
of the AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
and the conjugate AH structure
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
are related by:

(9.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \wideparen{R}_{ijkl}&=R_{ijkl}+2\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-2\chi_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}=-R_{ijlk}-d\chi_{ij}g_{kl},\\
\wideparen{R}_{ij}&=R_{ij}+\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}-\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}=g^{pq}R_{ipqj}+d\chi_{ij}.\end{aligned}

In particular, for any
g\in[g]
,
(\nabla,[g])
and
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
have the same scalar curvature
\wideparen{\mathit{s}}=g^{pq}\wideparen{R}_{pq}=g^{pq}R_{pq}=\mathit{s}
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
,

(9.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\wideparen{R}_{ijkl}&=R_{ijkl}+2g_{lp}\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]k}\,^{p}+2\mathcal{L}_{pl[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]k}\,^{p}=R_{ijkl}+2\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-2\mathcal{L}_{k[j}\,^{p}\nabla_{i]}g_{lp}+2\mathcal{L}_{pl[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]k}\,^{p}\\
&=R_{ijkl}+2\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-2\chi_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}\\
&=R_{ijkl}+2\nabla_{[i}\nabla_{j]}g_{kl}-2\nabla_{[i}\chi_{j]}g_{kl}-2\chi_{[j}\nabla_{i]}g_{kl}-2\chi_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}=-R_{ijlk}-d\chi_{ij}g_{kl}.\end{split}

The remaining identities follow by taking traces and using
\nabla_{i}g^{ij}=-\chi^{j}=-g^{jp}\chi_{p}
.
∎

An AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
has
self-conjugate
curvature if the curvature tensor of the conjugate AH structure equals that of
(\nabla,[g])
. For example, by (
9.5
) a Weyl structure has self-conjugate curvature because its cubic torsion vanishes.

A tensor derived from the curvature tensor of an AH structure that is unchanged under conjugacy is said to be
self-conjugate
. For example, the scalar curvature of an AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
associated with
g\in[g]
is self-conjugate.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
(\nabla,[g])
be an AH structure. Let
\chi_{i}
and
\mathit{s}
be the one-form and scalar curvature associated with the representative
g\in[g]
. The one-forms defined by

d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i}
and
\tfrac{n}{2}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}
do not depend on the choice of
g
, so are associated with the AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{g}=fg
with
0<f\in C^{\infty}(M)
, then
\tilde{\mathit{s}}=\tilde{g}^{ij}R_{ij}=f^{-1}\mathit{s}
and
\nabla_{[i}\tilde{g}_{j]k}=\tilde{\chi}_{[i}\tilde{g}_{j]k}
with
\tilde{\chi}_{i}=\chi_{i}+d\log f_{i}
. It follows that
d\tilde{\mathit{s}}_{i}+\tilde{\mathit{s}}\tilde{\chi}_{i}=d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i}
and
d\tilde{\chi}_{ij}=d\chi_{ij}
.
∎

For an AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
and
g\in[g]
define a symmetric tensor by

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{ij}=\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}-\tfrac{1}{2}|\mathcal{L}|^{2}g_{ij},

where
|\mathcal{L}|^{2}=\mathcal{L}^{ijk}\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
. The expression (
9.7
) does not depend on the choice of
g\in[g]
, so
\mathcal{T}
is associated with the AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
.

Lemma 9.6
.

On an
n
-manifold, let
(\nabla,[g])
be an AH structure with conjugate AH structure
(\wideparen{\nabla},[h])
. For
g\in[g]
, with associated one-form
\chi_{i}
and scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
, there holds

(9.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}&=\tfrac{n-2}{n}\left(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i}+\tfrac{n}{2}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}\right)-g_{ia}g^{pq}\left(\wideparen{\nabla}_{p}\wideparen{S}_{q}\,^{a}+\nabla_{p}S_{q}\,^{a}\right)-\chi^{p}(S_{ip}+\wideparen{S}_{ip})\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}+\tfrac{2-n}{2n}\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{ab}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ab}-\tfrac{1}{n}\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{ab}(\wideparen{S}_{ab}-S_{ab}),\end{aligned}

where
S_{ij}=R_{(ij)}-\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}
is the trace-free symmetrized Ricci tensor of
(\nabla,[g])
,
S_{i}\,^{j}=g^{ja}S_{ia}
,
\chi^{i}=g^{ip}\chi_{p}
, and
\mathscr{T}_{ij}
is the tensor defined in (
9.7
).

Proof.

The proof is an adaptation of the usual argument showing that the Einstein tensor of a metric is divergence free.

Observe that
R_{ij}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}+\tfrac{n}{4}d\chi_{ij}+S_{ij}
, so that
S_{[ij]}=0
and
\wideparen{R}_{ij}-R_{ij}=\wideparen{S}_{ij}-S_{ij}
. From (
9.5
) it follows that
R_{ip}\,^{p}\,{}_{j}=\wideparen{R}_{ij}-d\chi_{ij}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}+\tfrac{n-4}{4}d\chi_{ij}+\wideparen{S}_{ij}
. Differentiating
R_{ij}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}+\tfrac{n}{4}d\chi_{ij}+S_{ij}
and antisymmetrizing the result yields

(9.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{i}R_{jk}&=\tfrac{1}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i})g_{jk}+\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}\mathcal{L}_{ijk}+\tfrac{n}{4}\nabla_{i}d\chi_{jk}+\nabla_{i}S_{jk},\\
2\nabla_{[i}R_{j]k}&=\tfrac{2}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{[i}g_{j]k}+\mathit{s}\chi_{[i}g_{j]k})-\tfrac{n}{4}\nabla_{k}d\chi_{ij}+2\nabla_{[i}S_{j]k}.\end{split}

Contracting (
9.9
) with
g^{jk}
yields

(9.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}2g^{jk}\nabla_{[i}R_{j]k}&=\tfrac{n-1}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i})+\tfrac{n}{4}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}-g^{pq}\nabla_{p}S_{iq}+\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{pq}S_{pq}\\
&=\tfrac{n-1}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i})+\tfrac{n}{4}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}-g_{ia}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}S_{q}\,^{a}-\chi^{p}S_{qi},\end{split}

in which the last inequality follows from
\nabla_{i}g^{jk}=-\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{jk}-\chi_{i}g^{jk}
and that
S_{ij}
is trace-free. On the other hand, by the differential Bianchi identity and
R_{ip}\,^{p}\,{}_{j}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}+\tfrac{n-4}{4}d\chi_{ij}+\wideparen{S}_{ij}
,

(9.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}2g^{jk}\nabla_{[i}R_{j]k}&=g^{jk}\nabla_{p}R_{ijk}\,^{p}=\nabla_{p}R_{ia}\,^{ap}-R_{ijk}\,^{p}\nabla_{p}g^{jk}\\
&=\nabla_{p}R_{ia}\,^{ap}+\chi^{p}R_{ia}\,^{a}\,{}_{p}+\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{ibca}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}R_{ia}\,^{a}\,{}_{q}+\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}\\
&=\tfrac{1}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i})+\tfrac{4-n}{4}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}+g^{pq}\nabla_{p}\wideparen{S}_{qi}+\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{pq}\wideparen{S}_{pq}+\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}\\
&=\tfrac{1}{n}(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i})+\tfrac{4-n}{4}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}+g_{ia}g^{pq}\wideparen{\nabla}_{p}\wideparen{S}_{q}\,^{a}+\chi^{p}\wideparen{S}_{pi}+\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)},\end{split}

in which the last equality follows from
\wideparen{\nabla}_{i}g^{jk}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{jk}-\chi_{i}g^{jk}

Combining (
9.10
) and (
9.11
) yields the first equality of (
9.8
).

The Levi-Civita connection
D
of
g
has the form

(9.12)

\displaystyle D=\nabla+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{k}+\chi_{i}\delta_{j}\,^{k}+\chi_{j}\delta_{i}\,^{k}-g_{ij}\chi^{k}\right).

From (
9.12
) there follow

(9.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} D_{i}\mathcal{L}_{jkl}&=\nabla_{i}\mathcal{L}_{jkl}-\tfrac{3}{2}\mathcal{L}_{i(j}\,^{p}\mathcal{L}_{kl)p}-\tfrac{3}{2}\chi_{i}\mathcal{L}_{jkl}-\tfrac{3}{2}\chi_{(j}\mathcal{L}_{kl)i}+\tfrac{3}{2}g_{i(j}\mathcal{L}_{kl)}\,^{p}\chi_{p},\\
D_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}&=\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-\chi_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}+\tfrac{1}{2}g_{k[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]l}\,^{p}\chi_{p}+\tfrac{1}{2}g_{l[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]k}\,^{p}\chi_{p},\\
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}&=\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}-\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}+\tfrac{n}{2}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}\chi_{p},\end{aligned}

where
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}=D_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}
. Combining (
9.13
) with (
9.5
) yields

(9.14)

\displaystyle R_{ij(kl)}

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-\tfrac{1}{2}d\chi_{ij}g_{kl}+\chi_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}=-D_{[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kl}-\tfrac{1}{2}d\chi_{ij}g_{kl}+\tfrac{1}{2}g_{k[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]lp}\chi^{p}+\tfrac{1}{2}g_{l[i}\mathcal{L}_{j]kp}\chi^{p},

(9.15)

\displaystyle\wideparen{R}_{ij}

\displaystyle=R_{ij}+\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}-\tfrac{n}{2}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}\chi_{p}.

The divergence
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}=D^{p}\mathcal{T}_{ip}
satisfies

(9.16)

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{ab}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ab}+\mathcal{L}^{abc}D_{a}\mathcal{L}_{bci}-\tfrac{1}{2}D_{i}|\mathcal{L}|^{2}.

Contracting
\mathcal{L}^{abc}
and
R_{iabc}
and simplifying the result using (
9.14
) and (
9.16
) yields directly

(9.17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{iabc}&=\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{abc}D_{i}\mathcal{L}_{abc}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{abc}D_{a}\mathcal{L}_{bci}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{abc}\left(g_{b[i}\mathcal{L}_{a]c}\,^{p}\chi_{i}+g_{c[i}\mathcal{L}_{a]b}\,^{p}\chi_{i}\right)\\
&=-\tfrac{1}{4}D_{i}|\mathcal{L}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}-\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{ab}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ab}+\tfrac{1}{2}D_{i}|\mathcal{L}|^{2}\right)+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{aq}\,^{p}\chi^{a}\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{ab}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ab}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{aq}\,^{p}\chi^{a}.\end{aligned}

Substituting (
9.15
) in (
9.17
) yields the second equality of (
9.8
).
∎

Lemma
9.5
shows that the condition (
9.18
) in Definition
9.7
is well posed.

Definition 9.7
.

•

An AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
is
half naive Einstein
if the symmetric part of its Ricci curvature is a multiple of any
g\in[g]
. That is, for any
g\in[g]
,
R_{(ij)}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}
.

•

An AH structure is
conjugate half naive Einstein
if the conjugate AH structure is half naive Einstein.

•

An AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
is
naive Einstein
if it is both half naive Einstein and conjugate half naive Einstein - the symmetric parts of its Ricci curvature and the Ricci curvature of the conjugate AH structure are multiples of any
g\in[g]
. That is, for any
g\in[g]
,
R_{(ij)}=\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{n}g_{ij}=\wideparen{R}_{(ij)}
.

•

An AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
is
Einstein
if it is naive Einstein and for every
g\in[g]
with associated one-form
\chi_{i}
and scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
, there holds the
conservation condition

(9.18)

\displaystyle 0=d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i}+\tfrac{n}{2}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}.

Lemma 9.8
.

On an
n
-manifold, for a naive Einstein AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
, for
g\in[g]
with associated one-form
\chi_{i}
, scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
, and tensor
\mathcal{T}_{ij}
, there hold

(9.19)

\displaystyle\tfrac{n-2}{n}\left(d\mathit{s}_{i}+\mathit{s}\chi_{i}+\tfrac{n}{2}g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}\right)=\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}+\tfrac{2-n}{4}\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{aq}\,^{p}\chi^{a}.

Consequently, for a naive Einstein AH structure on a manifold of dimension
n\geq 3
the following are equivalent:

(1)

It is Einstein.

(2)

The invariantly defined one-form
\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{iabc}
vanishes identically.

(3)

There holds
2\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}=(n-2)\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{aq}\,^{p}\chi^{a}
.

Proof.

By the naive Einstein condition there vanish
S_{ij}
and
\wideparen{S}_{ij}
. By (
9.15
) this implies
2\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}=n\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}\chi_{p}
and with (
9.8
) there results (
9.19
).
The claimed equivalences follows from (
9.19
).
∎

The condition (
9.18
) of Definition
9.7
and the terminology
conservation condition
need motivation. This is given below, following Corollary
9.10
. For an exact AH structure the condition (
9.18
) is simply the constancy of the scalar curvature, and the issue is that this needs to be imposed, as it does not follow from the naive Einstein condition (Example
28
gives an example showing that naive Einstein does not imply Einstein).

Lemma 9.9
.

An AH structure is Einstein if and only if the conjugate AH structure is Einstein.

Proof.

That conjugacy preserves the naive Einstein condition is immediate.
Let
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
be the conjugate AH structure of the naive Einstein AH structure
(\nabla,[g])
. The scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
and one-form
\chi_{i}
associated with
\nabla
and
g\in[g]
are the same as those associated with
\wideparen{\nabla}
and
g
, and
g^{pq}\wideparen{\nabla}_{p}d\chi_{qi}=g^{pq}(\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}-\mathcal{L}_{pq}\,^{a}d\chi_{ai}-\mathcal{L}_{pi}\,^{a}d\chi_{qa})=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}-\mathcal{L}_{i}\,^{pq}d\chi_{pq}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}d\chi_{qi}
, which suffices to show that (
9.18
) holds for
(\nabla,[g])
if and only if it holds for
(\wideparen{\nabla},[g])
. Alternatively, the right-hand side of (
9.19
) is self-conjugate, so the left-hand side is as well.
∎

Corollary
9.10
shows that, with the additional condition of self-conjugacy of the curvature, the naive Einstein condition implies the conservation condition when
n>2
.

Corollary 9.10
.

On a manifold of dimension
n>2
, a naive Einstein AH structure with self-conjugate curvature satisfies (
9.18
), so is Einstein.

Proof.

If the curvature of
(\nabla,[g])
is self-conjugate, then, by (
9.5
),
R_{ij(kl)}=\tfrac{1}{2}d\chi_{ij}g_{kl}
, so
\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{iabc}=0
and
(\nabla,[g])
is conservative. With (
9.8
) this implies the claim.
∎

The conservation condition (
9.18
) generalizes the constancy of the scalar curvature of a metric. It follows from the traced Bianchi identities that if the dimension
n
is greater than
2
, then the usual Einstein condition for a metric implies the scalar curvature is constant. In dimension
2
this fails and the constancy of the scalar curvature is the best substitute for the Einstein equations. In
[
22
]
, Calderbank made this observation the basis of the definition of Einstein equations for Weyl structures in
2
-dimensions. With the notations used here, he defined a
2
-dimensional Weyl structure to be Einstein if it satisfies (
9.18
). This definition is justified by the observation that for a Weyl structure on a manifold of dimension
n>2
, the naive Einstein equations imply (
9.18
), by essentially the same argument as for ordinary metrics. Since Einstein equations for AH structures should specialize to the usual Einstein equations for Weyl structures when the cubic torsion vanishes, this suggests that the definition of Einstein equations for AH structures should force or require (
9.18
).

Lemma
9.8
suggests and Example
28
confirms that for AH structures the naive Einstein equations do not imply (
9.18
) when
n>2
.

For a metric
g
with Ricci and scalar curvature
\mathsf{Ric}(g)_{ij}
and
\mathscr{R}(g)
, the
Einstein tensor

\mathscr{G}_{ij}=\mathsf{Ric}(g)_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathscr{R}(g)g_{ij}
is divergence free by the traced differential Bianchi identity. Calling (
9.18
) a
conservation
condition is motivated by thinking of the constancy of the scalar curvature of an Einstein metric (in the usual sense) as the vanishing of the divergence of the Einstein tensor, which can be viewed as a conservation law. Corollary
9.13
shows that for a naive Einstein special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
to be Einstein is equivalent to the vanishing of the divergence of the tensor
\mathcal{T}_{ij}
defined in (
9.7
) and this is in turn equivalent to
g
solving the Einstein field equations with stress-energy tensor
\mathcal{T}_{ij}
. These observations seem the most convincing arguments for the requiring the conservation condition as part of the definition of Einstein.

Now these statements are made precise for special statistical structures.

Definition 9.11
.

A special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
is
naive Einstein
or
Einstein
if the AH structure that it generates has the same property. In this case it is said to have scalar curvature equal to the scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
corresponding with
g
.

A special statistical structure or an exact AH structure for which there vanish both the Ricci curvature and the conjugate Ricci curvature satisfies (
9.18
) so is Einstein. Such a structure is said to be
scalar-flat Einstein
.

The
stress-energy
tensor of the special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
is the tensor
\mathcal{T}_{ij}
defined by (
9.7
). This terminology is justified by Corollary
9.13
. Some preliminary calculations are needed for its proof.

Lemma 9.12
.

On an
n
-manifold, let
(\nabla,[g])
be an AH structure and consider a representative metric
g\in[g]
with Levi-Civita connection
D
, scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
, associated one-form
\chi_{i}
, and associated tensor
\mathscr{T}_{ij}
as in (
9.7
).
The Einstein tensor,
\mathscr{G}_{ij}=\mathsf{Ric}(g)_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathit{s}(g)g_{ij}
, of
g
satisfies:

(9.20)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathscr{G}_{ij}&=R_{(ij)}-\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{2}g_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}-\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}\chi_{p})\\
&\quad+\tfrac{2-n}{2}\left(D_{(i}\chi_{j)}-\operatorname{div}(\chi)g_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}+\tfrac{n-3}{4}|\chi|^{2}g_{ij}\right),\end{aligned}

where
\operatorname{div}(\chi)=g^{pq}D_{p}\chi_{q}
.

Proof.

Straightforward computations using the expression (
9.12
) for the difference tensor
D-\nabla
show the Ricci and scalar curvatures,
\mathsf{Ric}(g)
and
\mathit{s}(g)
, satisfy

(9.21)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(g)_{ij}&=R_{(ij)}+\tfrac{1}{4}\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}-\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}\chi_{p})\\
&\quad+\tfrac{2-n}{2}\left(D_{(i}\chi_{j)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}-\tfrac{1}{2}|\chi|^{2}g_{ij}\right)-\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\chi)g_{ij},\\
\mathit{s}(g)&=\mathit{s}+\tfrac{1}{4}|\mathcal{L}|^{2}+(1-n)\operatorname{div}(\chi)+\tfrac{(n-1)(n-2)}{4}|\chi|^{2}.\end{aligned}

Combining these yields (
9.20
).
∎

Corollary 9.13
.

On an
n
-manifold, for a special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
with stress-energy tensor
\mathcal{T}_{ij}
and scalar curvature
\mathit{s}
there holds

(9.22)

\displaystyle 2(n-2)d\mathit{s}_{i}=2n\mathcal{L}^{abc}R_{i(abc)}=n\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})_{i}.

For a naive Einstein special statistical structure on a manifold of dimension
n\geq 3
the following are equivalent:

(1)

It is Einstein.

(2)

Its stress-energy tensor is divergence free.

(3)

The metric
g
satisfies the Einstein field equations
\mathscr{G}_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{4}\mathscr{T}_{ij}
.

Proof.

For any special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
, specializing (
9.20
) shows

(9.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} R_{(ij)}-\tfrac{\mathit{s}}{2}g_{ij}&=\mathscr{G}_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{L})_{ij}.\end{aligned}

By (
9.15
) the naive Einstein condition implies
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}
is divergence free. The claimed equivalences follow from Lemma
9.8
together with (
9.23
).
∎

Remark 22
.

Definition
9.11
makes sense for statistical structures that are not special, but it is not clear whether this is the most natural notion of Einstein equations for general statistical structures. It is possible that it should be augmented by some additional condition, for example that the vector field
\chi^{i}
be Killing.

Example 26
.

Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection with nondegenerate symmetric Ricci tensor
R_{ij}=R_{(ij)}
and define
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}=\tfrac{1}{1-n}R_{(ij)}
. If
C_{ijk}=0
,
\nabla\det P=0
, and
g^{jk}R_{ijk}\,^{l}=0
, then
(\nabla,g)
is an Einstein special statistical structure. The following observation motivates the general construction: the AH structures induced on a cooriented nondegenerate hypersurface in flat affine space as in Example
25
are Einstein if and only if the affine hypersurface is an affine sphere. See
[
56
,
57
,
59
]
for proofs.

Example 27
.

Coupled with the main results of
[
55
]
and
[
62
]
,
[
62
, Lemma
1.18
]
yields Einstein AH structures that are not locally equivalent to those induced on an affine sphere. If
D
is the Levi-Civita connection of a Euclidean metric
g_{ij}
and
(\nabla,[g])
is an exact AH structure with
D
-parallel cubic torsion, then the Einstein AH equations become purely algebraic conditions on the cubic torsion, which can be interpreted as the structure tensor of a commutative, not necessarily associative, nonunital algebra for which the Killing type trace-from is invariant. The purely algebraic problem of constructing solutions that are not locally equivalent to affine spheres is tractable and is resolved affirmatively in all dimensions greater than
3
in
[
55
,
62
]
.

Example 28
.

Here is an example of a special statistical structure that is naive Einstein but not Einstein.

Consider
\mathbb{R}^{3}
equipped with a Euclidean metric
g_{ij}
and its Levi-Civita connection
D
. Let
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
be coordinates such that
dx_{i}
,
dx_{2}
, and
dx_{3}
constitute an orthonormal coframe, and let
\{\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\partial_{3}\}
be the dual frame. Write
\partial_{ijk}=\partial_{i}\otimes\partial_{j}\otimes\partial_{k}
