. Define a trace-free symmetric cubic tensor
L
by

(9.24)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} L&=2(x_{1}+x_{3})\left(\partial_{112}+\partial_{121}+\partial_{211}-\partial_{113}-\partial_{131}-\partial_{311}\right)\\
&+2(x_{1}-x_{3})\left(\partial_{123}+\partial_{231}+\partial_{312}+\partial_{213}+\partial_{321}+\partial_{132}\right).\end{aligned}

Let
L_{ij}\,^{k}=g^{kp}L_{ijp}
.
As
L
is trace-free,
\nabla=D-\tfrac{1}{2}L_{ij}\,^{k}
determines with
g
a special statistical structure. Straightforward calculations show that
L
is divergence-free and satisfies

(9.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}=-8(x_{1}^{2}+x_{3}^{2})g,

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})=-16(x_{1}\partial_{1}+x_{3}\partial_{3}).

Because
L
is divergence-free
(\nabla,g)
has self-conjugate Ricci curvature by (
9.15
). Because
g
is flat, by (
9.23
),
R_{(ij)}=-\tfrac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}_{ij}=2(x_{1}^{2}+x_{3}^{2})g_{ij}
, so
(\nabla,g)
is naive Einstein. On the other hand, by Corollary
9.13
, because
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{T})
is not identically zero,
(\nabla,g)
is not Einstein.

10.
Einstein statistical and AH structures on principal bundles with one-dimensional fibers

This section treats a metric
G_{IJ}
and a cone connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on the total space of a principal bundle
\rho:N\to M
with one-dimensional structure group making use of the notions related to statistical structures introduced in Section
9
. Uppercase Latin letters are used for abstract indices on
N
, and indices are raised and lowered with
G_{IJ}
. For example
\hat{R}_{IJKL}=\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{P}G_{PL}
is the curvature of
\widehat{\nabla}
with the last index lowered by
G_{IJ}
.

The general setting considered is the following. Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
and let
\beta
be a principal connection on
\rho:N\to M
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection on
\rho:N\to M
of
(\nabla,\beta)
.
Let
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
be the symmetrized projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
, let
\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}=d\beta_{IJ}
be the curvature of
\beta
, and let
\eta_{ij}=2P_{[ij]}+\omega_{ij}
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, define a symmetric tensor
G_{IJ}
on
N
by

(10.1)

\displaystyle G_{IJ}=\widehat{\nabla}_{(I}\beta_{J)}+(2+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}-\tfrac{1}{2}d\beta_{IJ}+(2+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=(1+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}-\rho^{\ast}(P)_{(IJ)}.

By Lemma
10.1
,
G_{IJ}
is a metric if
t\neq-1
. The form of
G_{IJ}
is a generalization of the construction used to produce the Berger metrics on a three sphere viewed as the total space of the Hopf fibration over the two sphere, and is commonly used in the construction of Einstein-Weyl metrics on
S^{1}
-bundles
[
24
,
119
]
where it is sometimes called the
canonical variation
, and
1+t
is usually written
r^{2}
.

Lemma
10.1
records basic computations relating the cone connection
\widehat{\nabla}
and the metric
G_{IJ}
. Corollary
10.2
shows that when
N=M\times G
is trivial and
\beta
is flat,
\widehat{\nabla}
generates with
[G]
a closed AH structure of which it is the aligned representative, and this AH structure is scalar-flat Einstein. In the case that
\beta
is exact, the conformal metric
H=e^{2f}G
, where
f
is a primitive such that
df=\beta
, constitutes with
\widehat{\nabla}
a scalar-flat Einstein special statistical structure.

This motivates Theorem
10.3
which shows that given a metric
H
on the frame bundle
\mathcal{F}
of a pseudo-tautological line bundle the
H
-conjugate connection of the Thomas connection
\nabla
of a projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
is again the Thomas connection of a projective structure
[\bar{\nabla}]
on
M
if and only if
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is a scalar-flat Einstein special radiant statistical structure. Moreover, in this case,
v=H(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
is a potential for
2H
, meaning
\widehat{\nabla}dv=2H
, and solves a Monge-Ampère equation
\det\widehat{\nabla}dv=c|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|^{2}
where
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
is the canonical volume density on
\mathcal{F}
. With the minor additional technical hypothesis that the closed one-form
\beta=H(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\flat}
is a principal connection, the unique representatives
\nabla\in[\nabla]
and
\bar{\nabla}\in[\bar{\nabla}]
inducing
\beta
generate with their projective Schouten tensors conjugate special statistical structures that are Einstein with negative scalar curvature. As in explained in Example
29
, when
\nabla
is a properly convex flat projective structure this recovers the picture, generalizing Example
1
, relating such structures with hyperbolic affine spheres. It is more general in several senses. For one, it makes sense for non-Riemannian signatures. For another, it allows
[\nabla]
to be not projectively flat, or, equivalently,
\widehat{\nabla}
to be not flat.

Lemma
10.4
shows that in the general setting where
\rho:N\to M
is a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, the connection
\widehat{\nabla}
can be modified in a way that depends on
\beta
and
G
to obtain a connection
\hat{D}
that has some possibility, in the presence of further geometric conditions, of constituting with
[G]
an Einstein AH structure. Theorem
10.5
shows that when
\widehat{\nabla}
is the cone connection of an Einstein special statistical structure that admits an appropriately compatible almost complex structure, then
\hat{D}
is aligned with respect to
G
and
(\hat{D},[G])
is an Einstein AH structure. This generalizes Corollary
10.2
to the case of nontrivial bundles. For a properly convex flat real projective structure on an oriented compact surface of genus at least
2
, the compatible complex structure is that determined by the Cheng-Yau metric and the given orientation, and the desired
\hat{D}
exists provided the Euler number
e(N)
of
N
satisfies
|e(N)|\leq-\chi(M)
. This is explained in Example
31
and summarized in Theorem
10.6
. As is explained in Example
30
, when the geometric data on
M
comprises a Kähler-Einstein metric of nonzero scalar curvature, the resulting
(\hat{D},[G])
are Einstein-Weyl structures whose construction is well known.

There is a long tradition of constructing Einstein metrics on the total spaces of principal bundles in which the Kaluza-Klein construction is paradigmatic. See
[
17
]
for a mathematically oriented survey. The constructions described in this section are similar in flavor to these constructions, although hewing to the model of Example
1
and based on the cone connection of an extended projective structure, and finally yielding Einstein statistical structures and Einstein AH structures rather than Einstein metrics as such. The presentation of Example
1
was made to conform with the statement of Lemma
10.4
and is guided by seeking analogues of the Fefferman metric
[
53
]
,
[
71
, Section
5
]
, and
[
96
]
in the context of projective structures.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
and let
\beta
be a principal connection on
\rho:N\to M
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection on
\rho:N\to M
of
(\nabla,\beta)
.
Let
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
be the symmetrized projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
, let
\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}=d\beta_{IJ}
be the curvature of
\beta
, and let
\eta_{ij}=2P_{[ij]}+\omega_{ij}
.

(1)

For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the tensor
G_{IJ}
on
N
defined by (
10.1
) is invariant under the principal action (so, in particular,
\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}G=0
) and
G_{IJ}
is nondegenerate if and only if
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
is nondegenerate and
t\neq-1
.

(2)

With
\tau_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{i}g_{pq}
and
\chi_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}g_{qi}
, there hold

(10.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{I}G_{JK}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK}-2\beta_{I}G_{JK}+2tG_{I(J}\beta_{K)}+(1+t)d\beta_{I(J}\beta_{K)}-2t(t+1)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\beta_{K},

(10.3)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}G_{J]K}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1+t}{2}\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}d\beta_{J]K}=-(2+t)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}-\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ}+\tfrac{2+t}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{KIJ},

(10.4)

\displaystyle G^{JK}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}G_{JK}

\displaystyle=-2(n+1)\beta_{I}-G^{JK}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla G)_{IJK}=-2(n+1)\beta_{I}+\rho^{\ast}(\tau)_{I},

(10.5)

\displaystyle G^{JK}\widehat{\nabla}_{J}G_{KI}

\displaystyle=(nt-2)\beta_{I}-G^{JK}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla G)_{JKI}=(nt-2)\beta_{I}+\rho^{\ast}(\chi)_{I},

(10.6)

\displaystyle G^{JK}(\widehat{\nabla}_{I}G_{JK}

\displaystyle-(n+1)\widehat{\nabla}_{J}G_{KI})=-n(n+1)t\beta_{I}+\rho^{\ast}(\tau-(n+1)\chi)_{I}.

(3)

The curvature
\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{L}
of
\widehat{\nabla}
satisfies

(10.7)

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{IJK}\,^{Q}G_{QL}

\displaystyle=(1+t)(\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ})-\rho^{\ast}(S)_{IJKL},

(10.8)

\displaystyle G^{AB}\hat{R}_{IAB}\,^{Q}G_{QJ}

\displaystyle=(1+t)\rho^{\ast}(\chi-\tau+\tfrac{1}{2}\nu)_{I}\beta_{J}+\rho^{\ast}(T)_{IJ},

where
\nu_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}\omega_{qi}
and

(10.9)

\displaystyle S_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=B_{ijkl}-g_{l[i}\eta_{j]k}+g_{kl}\eta_{ij}=R_{ijkl}+2g_{l[i}g_{j]k}-g_{l[i}\omega_{j]k}-g_{kl}\omega_{ij},

(10.10)

\displaystyle T_{ij}

\displaystyle=g^{pq}R_{ipqj}+(n-1)g_{ij}+\tfrac{3}{2}\omega_{ij},

with
B_{ijkl}=B_{ijk}\,^{p}g_{pl}
and
R_{ijkl}=R_{ijk}\,^{p}g_{pl}
.

Proof.

The last equality of (
10.1
) follows from (
6.4
) and implies the invariance of
G
under the principal action. That
G_{IJ}
is nondegenerate if and only if
g_{ij}
is nondegenerate and
t\neq-1
is apparent from (
10.1
).

For any
S_{ij}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{2}T^{\ast}M)
, a straightforward calculation using (
6.2
) shows

(10.11)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\rho^{\ast}(S)_{JK}

\displaystyle=\rho^{\ast}(\nabla S)_{IJK}-\rho^{\ast}(S)_{JI}\beta_{K}-\rho^{\ast}(S)_{IK}\beta_{J}-2\rho^{\ast}(S)_{JK}\beta_{I}.

Taking
S_{ij}=\omega_{ij}
in (
10.11
) yields

(10.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{I}d\beta_{JK}

\displaystyle=\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{IJK}-2\beta_{I}d\beta_{JK}+2\beta_{[J}d\beta_{K]I}.

Taking
S_{ij}=g_{ij}
in (
10.11
) and substituting (
10.1
) in the result yields

(10.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widehat{\nabla}_{I}\rho^{\ast}(g)_{JK}&=\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK}+2\beta_{(J}G_{K)I}+2\beta_{I}G_{JK}-4(1+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\beta_{K}.\end{aligned}

Combining (
10.13
) with

(10.14)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{I}(\beta_{J}\beta_{K})=2\beta_{(J}\widehat{\nabla}_{|I|}\beta_{K)}=2\beta_{(J}G_{K)I}+d\beta_{I(J}\beta_{K)}-2(2+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\beta_{K},

yields (
10.2
).
Antisymmetrizing (
10.2
) in
IJ
, noting that

(10.15)

\displaystyle 2\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=C_{ijk}+\nabla_{k}P_{[ij]}=C_{ijk}+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{k}\eta_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{k}\omega_{ij},

and using (
10.12
) yields

(10.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widehat{\nabla}_{[I}G_{J]K}&=-(2+t)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}+\tfrac{1+t}{2}\left(\beta_{K}d\beta_{IJ}-\beta_{[I}d\beta_{J]K}\right)\\
&\quad-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}-\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ}+\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{KIJ}\\
&=-(2+t)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}-\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ}+\tfrac{2+t}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{KIJ}-\tfrac{1+t}{4}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}d\beta_{IJ},\end{aligned}

which can be rewritten as (
10.3
).
Let
\{E(1),\dots,E(n)\}
be a
g
-unimodular orthogonal local frame on
M
. Then
\{|1+t|^{-1/2}\mathbb{E},\widehat{E(1)},\dots,\widehat{E(n)}\}
is a
G
-unimodular orthogonal local frame on
N
. The tensor corresponding with the inverse symmetric bivector
G^{IJ}
is

(10.17)

\displaystyle G^{IJ}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}G(\widehat{E(i)},\widehat{E(i)})\widehat{E(i)}^{I}\widehat{E(i)}^{J}+\tfrac{1}{1+t}\mathbb{E}^{I}\mathbb{E}^{J}=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(E(i),E(i))\widehat{E(i)}^{I}\widehat{E(i)}^{J}+\tfrac{1}{1+t}\mathbb{E}^{I}\mathbb{E}^{J}

It follows that, for
k\geq 2
and any
S_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}\in\Gamma(\otimes^{k}T^{\ast}M)
there holds

(10.18)

\displaystyle G^{AB}\rho^{\ast}(S)_{I_{1}\dots A\dots B\dots I_{k-2}}=-\rho^{\ast}(\sigma)_{I_{1}\dots I_{k-2}},

where
\sigma_{i_{1}\dots i_{k-2}}=g^{ab}S_{i_{1}\dots a\dots b\dots i_{k-2}}
. Using this remark and contracting (
10.13
), (
10.14
), and (
10.2
) with
G^{JK}
yields (
10.4
), (
10.5
), and (
10.6
).
The identities (
10.7
) and (
10.8
) follow straightforwardly from (
6.5
) and (
10.1
) together with (
10.18
).
∎

Remark 23
.

If the inertial indices of
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
are
(i_{+},i_{-},i_{0})
, then those of
G_{IJ}
are
(1+i_{-},i_{+},i_{0})
if
t>-1
and
(i_{-},1+i_{+},i_{0})
if
t<-1
. When applying Lemma
10.1
it should be kept in mind that
g_{ij}
can be negative definite.

Corollary 10.2
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N=M\times G\to M
be a trivial principal
G
-bundle where
G
is
S^{1}
or
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
and let
\beta
be a flat principal connection on
N
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection of the extended projective structure
[\nabla,\beta]
on
\rho:N\to M
generated by
(\nabla,\beta)
.

Suppose the symmetrized projective Schouten tensor
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
of
\nabla
is nondegenerate and there hold
\nabla\det g=0
,
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
, and
g^{pq}R_{(i|pq|j)}=(1-n)g_{ij}
, so that
(\nabla,g)
is an Einstein special statistical structure with negative scalar curvature. Let
G_{IJ}
be the metric on
N
defined by (
10.1
) with
t=0
.

(1)

\widehat{\nabla}
is aligned with respect to the metric
G_{IJ}
and
(\widehat{\nabla},[G])
is a scalar-flat Einstein closed AH structure on
N
.

(2)

If
\beta
is exact and
f\in C^{\infty}(N)
is a primitive of
\beta
, then
H_{IJ}=e^{2f}G_{IJ}
constitutes with
\widehat{\nabla}
a scalar-flat Einstein special statistical structure.

Proof.

The assumptions on
\nabla
imply the vanishing of
\tau
,
\chi
,
\nu
,
\omega_{ij}
, and
P_{[ij]}
, and, by (
10.15
), of
C_{ijk}+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{k}\eta_{ij}
. Because
t=0
, by (
10.3
),
\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}G_{J]k}=-2\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}
, while by (
10.6
),
G^{JK}(\widehat{\nabla}_{I}G_{JK}-(n+1)\widehat{\nabla}_{J}G_{KI})=0
, so
\widehat{\nabla}
is aligned with respect to
G
and
(\widehat{\nabla},[G])
is an AH structure that is closed because
\beta
is flat. From (
10.8
) and the hypothesis that
(\nabla,g)
is scalar-flat Einstein AH it follows that
G^{AB}\hat{R}_{IAB}\,^{Q}G_{QJ}
vanishes, so that
(\widehat{\nabla},[G])
is naive Einstein. A scalar-flat closed naive Einstein AH structure automatically satisfies the condition (
9.18
), so
(\widehat{\nabla},[G])
is Einstein. If
\beta=df
and
H_{IJ}=e^{2f}G_{IJ}
, then
\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}H_{J]K}=0
and, by (
10.4
),
H^{JK}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JK}=2(n+1)df_{I}-2(n+1)\beta_{I}=0
, so, by the preceding,
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
is a special statistical structure that is scalar-flat Einstein.
∎

Remark 24
.

The assumptions of Corollary
10.2
are satisfied by the connection induced on a hyperbolic affine sphere via its equiaffine normal, and so also by the statistical structure on a properly convex flat real projective manifold generated by the Cheng-Yau metric and the aligned representative of the flat projective structure.

Theorem
10.3
refines the special case of Corollary
10.2
where
N
is the total space
\mathcal{F}
of the frame bundle of a pseudo-tautological line bundle
\mathcal{E}^{-1}
over an
n
-manifold,
M
, and
\beta
is exact.
For a statistical structure
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
on
\mathcal{F}
for which
\widehat{\nabla}
is a Thomas connection, Theorem
10.3
shows that the connection conjugate to the
\widehat{\nabla}
is itself a Thomas connection if and only if
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
is a scalar-flat Einstein special statistical structure.

A metric
H_{IJ}
on the total space
\mathcal{F}
has
homogeneity
2
if
R^{\mathcal{F}}_{r}(H)=r^{2}H
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
. If
H
is assumed self-similar, this amounts to supposing additionally that
R_{-1}^{\ast}(H)=H
.

Theorem 10.3
.

On an
n
-manifold
M
, let
\mathcal{E}\to M
be a pseudo-hyperplane line bundle, let
\rho:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E}^{-1})\to M
be the frame bundle of
\mathcal{E}^{-1}
, and let
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{X}^{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the Thomas connection of a projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
. For a pseudo-Riemannian metric
H_{IJ}
on
\mathcal{F}
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

The
H
-conjugate connection,
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
, of
\widehat{\nabla}
is the Thomas connection of a projective structure
[\bar{\nabla}]
on
M
.

(2)

(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is a special radiant statistical structure such that
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
is scalar-flat Einstein as a special statistical structure.

In the case there hold (
1
) and (
2
):

(3)

The special radiant statistical structure
(\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}},\mathbb{E},H)
is also scalar-flat Einstein as a special statistical structure.

(4)

For
v=H(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
, the closed one-form
\beta_{I}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}dv_{I}
satisfies

(10.19)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dv_{J}=\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}+2\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=v^{-1}H_{IJ}=\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}_{I}\beta_{J}+2\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}_{I}dv_{J}

(5)

There is
c\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
such that
2^{-n-1}\det\widehat{\nabla}dv=\det H=c|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|^{2}
, where
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
is the volume density defined in Lemma
4.9
.

(6)

The
[\nabla]
-invariant lift of the trace-free tensor
[\bar{\nabla}]-[\nabla]=\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M\otimes TM)
is
\mathscr{L}(\Pi)_{IJ}\,^{K}=\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}-\tfrac{1}{n-1}\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\mathbb{E}^{K}
where
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}=\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}-\widehat{\nabla}=H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}
.

If there hold (
1
) and (
2
) and additionally
H_{IJ}
has homogeneity
2
, then:

(7)

The closed one-form
\beta_{I}=H(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{P}H_{PI}
is a principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-connection on
\mathcal{F}
.

(8)

The unique representatives
\nabla\in[\nabla]
and
\bar{\nabla}\in[\bar{\nabla}]
inducing
\beta
have vanishing projective Cotton tensors and nondegenerate symmetric projective Schouten tensors
P_{ij}
and
\bar{P}_{ij}
satisfying

(10.20)

\displaystyle-\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}-\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=\rho^{\ast}(P)_{IJ},

\displaystyle-\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}_{I}\beta_{J}-\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=\rho^{\ast}(\bar{P})_{IJ}

and
\nabla_{i}\det P=0
and
\bar{\nabla}_{i}\det\bar{P}=0
. In particular each of
(\nabla,P)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\bar{P})
is a special statistical structure.

(9)

The special statistical structures
(\nabla,P)
and
(\bar{\nabla},\bar{P})
are conjugate and Einstein with negative scalar curvature.

Proof.

The condition (
2
) can be restated as that
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is a special radiant statistical structure with curvature satisfying
H^{AB}\hat{R}_{IABJ}=0
. In this case, by Lemma
8.3
,
(\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}},\mathbb{E},H)
is a special radiant statistical structure with vanishing Ricci curvature and conjugate Ricci curvature. Since
\widehat{\nabla}
is a Thomas connection it is
\mathbb{E}
-invariant, so
\mathbb{E}^{A}\hat{R}_{PIJ}\,^{B}H_{BK}=0
. By (
8.25
) of Lemma
8.13
,
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is also
\mathbb{E}
-invariant and by Lemma
8.14
,
(\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}},\mathbb{E},H)
is conelike. By Theorem
7.3
,
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is the Thomas connection of some projective structure on
M
.

Now suppose
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is the Thomas connection of some projective structure on
M
. Since
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is torsion-free, there holds
\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}H_{J]K}=0
, and since
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is radiant, by (
2
) of Lemma
8.3
this implies
(g,\mathbb{E})
is self-similar, so
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is a radiant statistical structure. Since both
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
and
\widehat{\nabla}
preserve
|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|
,
H^{AB}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{AB}=0
, so
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is special. Because
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is Ricci-flat, by (
8.24
) of Lemma
8.13
there holds
H^{AB}\hat{R}_{IABJ}=0
, so
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
satisfies (
2
).

Suppose there hold (
1
)-(
2
).
In this case it is immediate from Lemma
8.13
that
(\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}},\mathbb{E},H)
is also Ricci-flat and conjugate Ricci-flat special radiant statistical structure, and claim (
4
) is immediate from Corollary
8.12
. By (
5
) of Lemma
8.13
, the statements that
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is special,
(\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}},\mathbb{E},H)
is special, and both
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
are equiaffine are all equivalent. Since
\widehat{\nabla}|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|=0
, these conditions are equivalent to the existence of
c\neq 0
such that
\det H=c|\Psi^{\mathcal{F}}|^{2}
.

Because
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
is statistical,
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}=\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}-\widehat{\nabla}=H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}
satisfies
\hat{\Pi}_{[IJ]}\,^{K}=0
. Because
g
is self-similar,
\mathbb{E}^{P}\widehat{\nabla}_{P}H_{IJ}=(\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}H)_{IJ}-2H_{IJ}=0
. Hence
\mathbb{E}^{P}\hat{\Pi}_{PI}\,^{J}=0
. Because
(\widehat{\nabla},H)
is special,
\hat{\Pi}_{IQ}\,^{Q}=H^{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{PQ}=0
and
\widehat{\nabla}_{Q}H^{IQ}=0
. These observations imply that
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}
is the horizontal lift of some
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M\otimes TM)
. Let
\bar{\nabla}\in[\bar{\nabla}]
and
\nabla\in[\nabla]
be the representatives inducing
\beta\in\mathsf{Prin}(\mathcal{F})
. By definition of the Thomas connections,
\rho^{\ast}(\theta)(\hat{\Pi}(\hat{X},\hat{Y}))=\rho^{\ast}(\theta(\Pi(X,Y)))
for all
X,Y\in\Gamma(TM)
and
\theta\in\Gamma(T^{\ast}M)
, so
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}=\bar{\nabla}-\nabla
. Because
\bar{\nabla}
and
\nabla
induce the same principal connection,
\Pi_{ip}\,^{p}=0
, so
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}
is trace-free and
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}=[\bar{\nabla}]-[\nabla]
.

Calculating using the preceding observations yields

(10.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}&=\widehat{\nabla}_{K}H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}+H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}\\
&=H^{KA}\left(\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}H_{JA}-\hat{R}_{KIJ}\,^{B}H_{BA}-\hat{R}_{KIA}\,^{B}H_{JB}\right)\\
&=H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\widehat{\nabla}_{J}H_{KA}-\hat{R}_{KIJ}\,^{K}+H^{KA}\hat{R}_{IKA}\,^{B}H_{JB}\\
&=\widehat{\nabla}_{I}(H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{J}H_{KA})-\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{J}H_{KA}=H^{KP}H^{AQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{J}H_{KA}=\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}.\end{split}

Thus
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}
is not the
[\nabla]
-invariant lift of
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}
.
Because
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
and
\widehat{\nabla}
are invariant under the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-action so is
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}
. Hence

(10.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}&\left(\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\mathbb{E}^{K}\right)=\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}\mathbb{E}^{K}+\mathbb{E}^{K}\widehat{\nabla}_{K}\left(\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\right)\\
&=(n+1)\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}+\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbb{E}}\left(\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\right)-2\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}=(n-1)\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A},\end{split}

where the penultimate equality follows from (
3.12
).
It follows that
\hat{\Pi}_{IJ}\,^{K}-\tfrac{1}{n-1}\hat{\Pi}_{IA}\,^{B}\hat{\Pi}_{JB}\,^{A}\mathbb{E}^{K}
is a horizontal lift of
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}
and
\widehat{\nabla}
-divergence free, so, by Lemma
7.4
, it is the
[\nabla]
-invariant lift of
\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}
.

Now suppose
H_{IJ}
has homogeneity
2
and let
v=H(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
.
Since
H_{IJ}
has homogeneity
2
,
\beta_{I}=v^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{P}H_{PI}
is invariant under the principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
action on
\mathcal{F}
, so, by (
3
) of Corollary
8.12
, is a principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-connection.
The vanishing of the projective Cotton tensors in claim (
8
) follows from (
7.6
) of Theorem
7.3
and (
8.25
) of Lemma
8.13
. That there holds (
10.20
) follows from the observation that
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}+\beta_{I}\beta_{J}
is invariant under the principal connection, so has the form
\rho^{\ast}(k)_{IJ}
for some
k_{ij}\in\Gamma(S^{2}T^{\ast}M)
and an explicit computation using the definition of the Thomas connection in the proof of Theorem
7.3
that shows
k_{ij}=-P_{ij}
. Because, by (
10.19
) and (
10.20
),
v^{-1}H_{IJ}=\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}+2\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=-\rho^{\ast}(P)_{IJ}+\beta_{I}\beta_{J}
,
P_{ij}
is nondegenerate.

Because
d\beta_{IJ}=0
,
P_{[ij]}=0
. Let
G_{IJ}=v^{-1}H_{IJ}=-\rho^{\ast}(P)_{IJ}+\beta_{I}\beta_{J}
and
\tau_{i}=P^{ab}\nabla_{i}P_{ab}
. By (
10.4
) of Lemma
10.1
and because
2\beta_{I}=v^{-1}dv_{I}
,

(10.23)

\displaystyle-G^{KA}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla P)_{IJA}

\displaystyle=G^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}G_{JA}+2\beta_{I}\delta_{J}\,^{K}=H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}.

Tracing (
10.23
) in
JK
and using (
10.18
) show that
\rho^{\ast}(\tau)_{I}=0
, so
\tau_{i}=0
and
(\nabla,P)
is a special statistical structure. Similarly, by (
10.17
) and (
10.23
),
-G^{KA}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla P)_{IJA}=H^{KA}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}H_{JA}
is the horizontal lift of
P^{ka}\nabla_{i}P_{ja}
, and by the proof of (
6
),
P^{ka}\nabla_{i}P_{ja}=\bar{\nabla}-\nabla
. This shows
(\bar{\nabla},P)
is the conjugate statistical structure of
(\nabla,P)
. It follows that
(\nabla,P)
and
(\bar{\nabla},P)
are conjugate special statistical structures that are naive Einstein, so Einstein. Because, by definition,
R_{ij}=(1-n)P_{ij}
, they have negative scalar curvature.
∎

Remark 25
.

The metric
H_{IJ}
of Theorem
10.3
must be self-similar in the sense of Definition
8.2
, but in the setting of the theorem it is not clear that this necessarily implies that it have homogeneity
2
. The issue is that although
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
and
\widehat{\nabla}
are both invariant under the principal action and
g
is positively homogeneous of degree
2
, these conditions do not seem to be sufficient to imply that
g
be homogeneous of degree
2
, although the author does not know an example demonstrating their insufficiency.

Remark 26
.

The tractor connection of the regular, normal Cartan connection canonically associated with a projective structure can be constructed by descending the Thomas connection to the rank
n+1
tractor bundle on
M
obtained by quotienting
T\mathcal{E}^{-1}
by an appropriate
\mathbb{R}^{+}
action, and the condition that
H
have homogeneity
2
characterizes those metrics which arise by lifting metrics from the tractor bundle.

Example 29
.

Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the flat affine connection determined by the affine structure on the
(n+1)
-dimensional real vector space
\mathbb{V}
and let
\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{V}
be a pointed convex cone. Let
\Psi
be the standard volume form on
\mathbb{V}
and let
\mathbb{E}
be the Euler field generating dilations by
e^{t}
. By a theorem of Cheng-Yau (see
[
60
,
99
,
101
]
for details and references), there is a unique smooth function
F
on
\mathbb{K}
solving
\det\widehat{\nabla}dF=e^{2F}\Psi^{\otimes 2}
, tending to
+\infty
on the boundary of
\mathbb{K}
, and such that
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dF_{J}
is a complete Riemannian metric on
\mathbb{K}
. Moreover, the function
e^{F}
has positive homogeneity
-n-1
. (The level sets of
F
are affine spheres asymptotic to
\mathbb{K}
and foliating its interior.) The positive homogeneity
2
function
v=-\tfrac{n+1}{2}e^{-\tfrac{2}{n+1}F}
, one-form
\beta_{I}=\tfrac{1}{2}v^{-1}dv_{I}=-\tfrac{1}{n+1}dF_{I}
, and tensor
H_{IJ}=\tfrac{1}{2}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dv_{J}
satisfy
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\beta_{J}=v^{-1}H_{IJ}-2\beta_{I}\beta_{J}=-\tfrac{1}{n+1}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dF_{J}
,
2\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}H_{J]K}=\widehat{\nabla}_{[I}\widehat{\nabla}_{J]}dv_{K}=0
, and
2^{n+1}\det H=\det\widehat{\nabla}dv=-\Psi^{\otimes 2}
. To justify the last claim, observe that
H_{IJ}
is nondegenerate and the tensor inverse to
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dF_{J}
is
-(n+1)^{-1}(vH^{IJ}-2\mathbb{E}^{I}\mathbb{E}^{J})
and use the formula for the determinant of a rank one perturbation to calculate
\det\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dv_{J}
in terms of
\det\widehat{\nabla}_{I}dF_{J}
(or see
[
60
, section
5
]
). In particular,
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},H)
is an Einstein special radiant statistical structure.

Let
M
be the set of rays in
\mathbb{K}
, viewed as a subset of the oriented projectivization of
\mathbb{V}
(the projective sphere). The canonical projection
\rho:\mathbb{K}\to M
has the structure of principal
\mathbb{R}^{+}
-bundle and can be viewed as the frame bundle of the tautological line bundle restricted to
M
. Because
(\widehat{\nabla},\mathbb{E},\Psi)
is a flat conelike equiaffine radiant structure on
\mathbb{K}
, it determines a flat projective structure
[\nabla]
on
M
. Because
\mathbb{K}
is pointed,
[\nabla]
is properly convex. The homothety class of the metric of the induced special statistical structure on
M
comprises the Blaschke metrics of the affine spheres foliating the interior of
\mathbb{K}
. The induced special statistical structure on
M
is a Einstein.
By Lemma
8.13
and Theorem
10.3
, the
H
-conjugate connection
\smash{\overline{\widehat{\nabla}}}
is a flat connection that with
\mathbb{E}
and
\Psi
constitutes a flat conelike equiaffine radiant structure and with
\mathbb{E}
and
H
constitutes a special radiant statistical structure inducing on
M
a flat projective structure
[\bar{\nabla}]
that is again properly convex. In fact,
[\bar{\nabla}]
is the flat projective structure dual to
[\nabla]
obtained via the preceding process applied to the dual cone
\mathbb{K}^{\ast}
, and the homothety class of the metric of the special statistical structure induced on
M
comprises the Blaschke metrics of the dual family of affine spheres foliating
\mathbb{K}^{\ast}
.

Because all of the preceding behaves well with respect to automorphisms of the structures involved, it can be applied to the cone over the universal cover of any properly flat convex projective structure. The point is to indicate that Theorem
10.3
extends this picture based on Cheng-Yau’s work on affine spheres and the related Monge-Ampere equations to a possibly nonflat setting.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
and let
\beta
be a principal connection on
\rho:N\to M
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection on
\rho:N\to M
generated by
(\nabla,\beta)
.

Suppose the symmetrized projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
,
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
, is nondegenerate and define the metric
G_{IJ}
by (
10.1
), let
\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}=d\beta_{IJ}
be the curvature of
\beta
, and let
\eta_{ij}=2P_{[ij]}+\omega_{ij}
.

Define
\tau_{i}=g^{ab}\nabla_{i}g_{ab}=(\det g)^{-1}\nabla_{i}\det g
,
\chi_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}g_{qi}
,
\nu_{i}=g^{pq}\nabla_{p}\omega_{qi}
, and
\mathcal{K}_{ijk}=2\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}
.
For
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
-1\neq t\in\mathbb{R}
let
G^{IJ}
be the symmetric bivector inverse to
G_{IJ}
and define
\hat{D}=\widehat{\nabla}+\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}
where

(10.24)

\displaystyle\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}

\displaystyle=(1+t)\beta_{(I}d\beta_{J)Q}G^{QK}-t\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\mathbb{E}^{K}+(s-1)\left(2\beta_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{K}-G_{IJ}\mathbb{E}^{K}\right)+2st\beta_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{K}.

The modified connection
\hat{D}
satisfies

(10.25)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{I}G_{JK}

\displaystyle=-2s(t+1)\beta_{I}G_{JK}-\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK},

(10.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{D}_{[I}G_{J]K}&=-2s(1+t)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(C)_{IJK}-\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\eta)_{KIJ}+\tfrac{1}{4}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{KIJ}\\
&=-2s(1+t)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{K})_{IJK},\end{split}

(10.27)

\displaystyle G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{I}G_{PQ}

\displaystyle-(n+1)G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}G_{QI}=\rho^{\ast}(\tau-(n+1)\chi)_{I},

(10.28)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{I}\mathbb{E}^{J}

\displaystyle=(1+t)(\tfrac{1}{2}d\beta_{I}\,^{J}+s\delta_{I}\,^{J}).

In particular,
\hat{D}_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}=s(1+t)\mathbb{E}
, so the fibers of
\rho:N\to M
are
\hat{D}
-totally geodesic.
The Ricci curvature
\mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}
of
\hat{D}
is

(10.29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}=&-(n+1)\rho^{\ast}(P)_{[IJ]}-\tfrac{s(n+1+(n+3)t)}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}+(t+1)\left(\rho^{\ast}(\nu)_{(I}\beta_{J)}+\beta_{(I}\rho^{\ast}(\chi\circ\omega)_{J)}\right)\\
&\quad+\left((n-1+nt)s^{2}-n+1-t\right)\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}-\tfrac{1+t}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\omega\circ\omega)_{IJ}\\
&\quad+(t+1)\left(t(1-s^{2})+\tfrac{(t+1)}{4}\rho^{\ast}(|\omega|^{2}_{g})\right)\beta_{I}\beta_{J},\end{aligned}

(10.30)

\displaystyle G^{IJ}\mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1+t}{4}\rho^{\ast}(|\omega|^{2}_{g})-\tfrac{(s^{2}-n-1)t^{2}+(n^{2}+1)(s^{2}-1)t+n(n-1)(s^{2}-1)}{t+1}+\tfrac{ns^{2}t(t-n+1)}{t+1},

where
(\omega\circ\omega)_{ij}=\omega_{ip}\omega_{qj}g^{pq}
,
|\omega|^{2}_{g}=\omega_{ab}\omega_{pq}g^{ap}g^{bq}
, and
(\chi\circ\omega)_{i}=\chi_{p}\omega_{iq}g^{pq}
.

Proof.

In this proof indices are raised and lowered using
G_{IJ}
and
G^{IJ}
. By (
10.5
),

(10.31)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{Q}G^{IQ}

\displaystyle=G^{IQ}\rho^{\ast}(\chi)_{Q}+\tfrac{2-nt}{1+t}\mathbb{E}^{I}.

By (
10.12
), (
10.5
), and (
10.31
),

(10.32)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widehat{\nabla}_{A}d\beta_{I}\,^{A}&=\widehat{\nabla}_{A}(G^{AP}d\beta_{IP})=G^{AP}\widehat{\nabla}_{A}d\beta_{IP}+d\beta_{IP}\widehat{\nabla}_{A}G^{AP}\\
&=G^{AB}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{AIB}-d\beta_{I}\,^{P}((nt-2)\beta_{P}+\rho^{\ast}(\chi)_{P})=\rho^{\ast}(\nu)_{I}-d\beta_{I}\,^{P}\rho^{\ast}(\chi)_{P}.\end{aligned}

In the rest of the proof suppose
G_{IJ}
is nondegenerate and let
G^{IJ}
be the symmetric bivector inverse to
G_{IJ}
.
For
s\in\mathbb{R}
, define the modified connection
\hat{D}=\widehat{\nabla}+\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}
, where
\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}
is as in (
10.24
).
From (
10.24
) and (
10.2
) there follows (
10.25
).

(10.33)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{I}G_{JK}

\displaystyle=-2s(1+t)\beta_{I}G_{JK}-\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK}.

Antisymmetrizing (
10.25
) and using (
10.12
) and (
10.16
) to simplify the result yields the first equality of (
10.26
). The second equality of (
10.26
) follows from the definitions of
C_{ijk}
and
\eta_{ij}
and the observation that
\nabla_{[i}P_{jk]}=0
.
Tracing (
10.33
) in two different ways shows (
10.27
). A straightforward computation using (
10.24
) shows (
10.28
).

Let
\tilde{D}=\widehat{\nabla}+\Pi_{IJ}\,^{K}
where

(10.34)

\displaystyle\Pi_{IJ}\,^{K}

\displaystyle=(1+t)\beta_{(I}d\beta_{J)Q}G^{QK}-t\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\mathbb{E}^{K}+(s-1)\left(2\beta_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{K}-G_{IJ}\mathbb{E}^{K}\right)=\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}-2st\beta_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{K}.

The Ricci curvature
\mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D})_{IJ}
of
\tilde{D}
is

(10.35)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D})_{IJ}&=\hat{R}_{IJ}+\widehat{\nabla}_{Q}\Pi_{IJ}\,^{Q}-\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\Pi_{QJ}\,^{Q}+\Pi_{PQ}\,^{Q}\Pi_{IJ}\,^{P}-\Pi_{IP}\,^{Q}\Pi_{JQ}\,^{P}.\end{aligned}

Straightforward computations using
\mathbb{E}^{I}d\beta_{IJ}=0
, and (
10.32
) in the final equality, yield

(10.36)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Pi_{IQ}\,^{Q}&=((s-1)(n+1)-st))\beta_{I},\\
\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\Pi_{JQ}\,^{Q}&=((s-1)(n+1)-st))(G_{IJ}+\tfrac{1}{2}d\beta_{IJ}-(t+2)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}),\\
\Pi_{IJ}\,^{P}\Pi_{PQ}\,^{Q}&=((s-1)(n+1)-st))\left((1-s)G_{IJ}+(2(s-1)-t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\right),\\
\Pi_{IP}\,^{Q}\Pi_{JQ}\,^{P}&=\tfrac{(t+1)^{2}}{4}\beta_{I}\beta_{J}d\beta_{P}\,^{Q}d\beta_{Q}\,^{P}-2(s-1)^{2}G_{IJ}\\
&\quad+\left(t^{2}+2t(t-1)(s-1)+(n+3+t^{2})(s-1)^{2}\right)\beta_{I}\beta_{J},\\
\widehat{\nabla}_{Q}\Pi_{IJ}\,^{Q}&=(t+1)\left(\rho^{\ast}(\nu)_{(I}\beta_{J)}-\beta_{(I}d\beta_{J)}\,^{P}\rho^{\ast}(\chi)_{P}\right)+\tfrac{t+1}{2}d\beta_{I}\,^{A}d\beta_{AJ}\\
&\quad+(3-n)(s-1)G_{IJ}-\left((2s+n-3)t+4(s-1)\right)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}.\end{aligned}

Combining (
10.35
), (
10.36
), and Theorem
6.2
yields

(10.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D})_{IJ}&=-\tfrac{n+1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\eta)_{IJ}+\tfrac{(1-s)(n+1)-st}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}+(t+1)\left(\rho^{\ast}(\nu)_{(I}\beta_{J)}+\beta_{(I}\rho^{\ast}(\chi\circ\omega)_{J)}\right)\\
&\quad-\tfrac{t+1}{2}\rho^{\ast}(\omega\circ\omega)_{IJ}+(t-(s^{2}-1)(n-1))G_{IJ}\\
&\quad+\left((s^{2}-1)(n-1)-t(n-1+2s^{2})-s^{2}t^{2}+\tfrac{(t+1)^{2}}{4}\rho^{\ast}(|\omega|^{2}_{g})\right)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}.\end{aligned}

The Ricci curvature of
\hat{D}=\tilde{D}+st\beta_{(I}\delta_{J)}\,^{K}
can be calculated using (
10.37
) and (
2.4
), and there results

(10.38)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D}+st\beta\otimes\delta+st\delta\otimes\beta)_{IJ}&=\mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D})_{IJ}+st\left(ns\left)(2+t)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}-G_{IJ}\right)-\tfrac{n+2}{2}d\beta_{IJ}\right)\\
&=\mathsf{Ric}(\tilde{D})_{IJ}+st\left(ns\left(\beta_{I}\beta_{J}+\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}\right)-\tfrac{n+2}{2}d\beta_{IJ}\right).\end{aligned}

Combined with (
10.37
) this shows (
10.29
). Tracing (
10.29
) yields (
10.30
).
∎

Theorem 10.5
.

Let
M
be an
n
-manifold, let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle or principal
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
-bundle, and let
\mathbb{E}
be the fundamental vertical vector field generated by the principal action. Let
\nabla
be a torsion-free affine connection on
M
and let
\beta
be a principal connection on
\rho:N\to M
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection on
\rho:N\to M
generated by
(\nabla,\beta)
.
Suppose the symmetrized projective Schouten tensor of
\nabla
,
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}
, is nondegenerate, let
\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}=d\beta_{IJ}
be the curvature of
\beta
, let
\eta_{ij}=2P_{[ij]}+\omega_{ij}
, and, for
t\neq-1
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, define the metric
G_{IJ}
by (
10.1
) and the connection
\hat{D}=\widehat{\nabla}+\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}
by (
10.24
).

(1)

If
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
and
\nabla_{i}|\det g|=0
, so that
(\nabla,g)
is a special statistical structure, and there is
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
such that
\omega_{ip}\omega_{qj}g^{pq}=\alpha g_{ij}
, then
\hat{D}_{[I}G_{J]K}=-2s(t+1)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}
and, if
\alpha>-\tfrac{4(n-1)}{(n+2)(t+1)}
, for

(10.39)

\displaystyle s=\pm\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{1+t}+\tfrac{(n+2)\alpha}{4(n-1)}},

then
(\hat{D},G_{IJ})
is a half naive Einstein AH structure.

(2)

If
(\nabla,g)
is an Einstein special statistical structure with scalar curvature
-n(n-1)
, and there is
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
such that
\omega_{ip}\omega_{qj}g^{pq}=\alpha g_{ij}
, then
(\hat{D},G_{IJ})
generates a naive Einstein AH structure for which
\hat{D}
is the aligned representative. If
t=0
, then
(\hat{D},G_{IJ})
is moreover an Einstein AH structure for which the scalar curvature associated with
G_{IJ}
is
-\tfrac{n(n+1)\alpha}{4}
.

If
g
has definite signature, the hypotheses in (
1
) and (
2
) imply
\alpha<0
and
n
is even.

Proof.

The setting is as in the proof of Lemma
10.4
. For tensors on
M
raise and lower indices using
g_{ij}
and
g^{ij}
. Suppose
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
and suppose
g^{pq}\omega_{ip}\omega_{jq}=-\alpha g_{ij}
for some
\alpha\neq 0
. If
g
has definite signature, this last assumption means that
\alpha=-(1/n)|\omega|^{2}_{g}<0
and a multiple of
\omega_{ip}g^{pj}
is an almost complex structure, so
M
must have even dimension.
Differentiating
g^{pq}\omega_{ip}\omega_{jq}=-\alpha g_{ij}
yields

(10.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\alpha\nabla_{k}g_{ij}&=-\omega_{i}\,^{a}\omega_{j}\,^{b}\nabla_{k}g_{ab}-2\omega^{q}\,_{(i}\nabla_{|k|}\omega_{j)q}.\end{split}

Contracting (
10.40
) with
g^{ij}
yields
-\alpha\tau_{i}=\omega^{pq}\nabla_{i}\omega_{pq}
.
Contracting (
10.40
) with
g^{jk}
and using
-\alpha\tau_{i}=\omega^{pq}\nabla_{i}\omega_{pq}
and
\nabla_{[i}\omega_{jk]}=0
yields

(10.41)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\alpha\chi_{i}&=\omega_{i}\,^{a}\omega^{bk}\nabla_{k}g_{ab}-\omega^{pq}\nabla_{p}\omega_{qi}+\omega_{i}\,^{a}\nu_{a}=\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{pq}\nabla_{i}\omega_{pq}+\omega_{i}\,^{a}\nu_{a}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha\tau_{i}+\omega_{i}\,^{a}\nu_{a}.\end{split}

Contracting
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
with
g^{jk}
shows
\tau_{i}=\chi_{i}
, so (
10.41
) implies
0=\alpha(2\omega_{i}\,^{p}\chi_{p}-\omega_{i}\,^{p}\tau_{p}+2\nu_{i})=\alpha(\omega_{i}\,^{p}\chi_{p}+2\nu_{i})
. If there is assumed
\tau_{i}=0
and
\alpha\neq 0
this implies
\nu_{i}=0
.
Consequently, with the stated assumptions, by (
10.26
),
(\hat{D},G)
satisfies
\hat{D}_{[I}G_{J]K}=-2s(t+1)\beta_{[I}G_{J]K}
and, by (
10.27
), there holds
G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{I}G_{PQ}-(n+1)G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}G_{QI}=0
, so that in this case
\hat{D}
is the aligned representative of the AH structure that it generates with
G_{IJ}
. Specializing (
10.29
) yields

(10.42)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{(IJ)}&=\left((n-1+nt)s^{2}-n+1-t-\tfrac{\alpha(t+1)}{2}\right)\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}+(t+1)\left(t(1-s^{2})-\tfrac{n\alpha(t+1)}{4}\right)\beta_{I}\beta_{J}\\
&=-\left(t(s^{2}-1)+\tfrac{n\alpha(t+1)}{4}\right)G_{IJ}+\left((n-1)(t+1)s^{2}-(n-1)-\tfrac{(n+2)(t+1)\alpha}{4}\right)\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}.\end{aligned}

where the second equality follows from (
10.1
). By (
10.42
),
\mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}
is a multiple of
G_{IJ}
if and only if
s
solves (
10.39
). This proves (
1
).

The hypotheses of (
2
) are the same as those of (
2
) except there is assumed additionally that the conjugate Ricci curvature
\wideparen{R}_{ij}
equals
R_{ij}=(1-n)g_{ij}
. By (
9.5
) this means
\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}=\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}
where
\mathcal{L}_{ijk}=\nabla_{i}g_{jk}
is the cubic tensor of
(\nabla,g)
. Let
(\wideparen{\hat{D}},[G])
be the conjugate AH structure of
(\hat{D},[G])
. By definition and (
10.25
) the cubic tensor of
(\hat{D},[G])
associated with
G_{IJ}
is
\hat{D}_{I}G_{JK}+2s(t+1)\beta_{I}G_{JK}=-\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK}=-\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJK}
. By (
9.5
), the difference of the Ricci curvatures of
\wideparen{\hat{D}}
and
\hat{D}
is
-\hat{D}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJ}\,^{P}-\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IP}\,^{Q}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{JQ}\,^{P}
. It will now be shown that this is zero. On the one hand
\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IP}\,^{Q}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{JQ}\,^{P}=G^{AB}G^{CB}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IAC}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{JCD}
, which is the pullback via
\rho
of
\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}
. On the other hand, because
\hat{D}_{P}G^{PI}
is a multiple of
\mathbb{E}^{I}
,

(10.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{D}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJ}\,^{P}&=G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJQ}\\
&=G^{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJQ}-2\Omega_{(I}\,^{PQ}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{J)PQ}-G^{AB}\Omega_{AB}\,^{Q}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJQ}\\
&=G^{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJQ},\end{split}

in which considerable simplification occurs because of the form (
10.24
) of
\Omega_{IJ}\,^{K}
(for example,
G^{AB}\Omega_{AB}\,^{K}
is a multiple of
\mathbb{E}^{K}
so its contraction with
\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJK}
vanishes). Computing as in (
10.12
) shows

(10.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\nabla}_{I}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{JKL}=\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\mathcal{L})_{IJKL}-3\beta_{I}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{JKL}-3\beta_{(J}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{KL)I},\end{split}

from which it follows that
G^{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{P}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{L})_{IJQ}
is the pullback via
\rho
of
-g^{pq}\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ijq}=-\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}
, the last equality because
(\nabla,g)
is special. It follows that the difference of the Ricci curvatures of
\wideparen{\hat{D}}
and
\hat{D}
is the pullback via
\rho
of
\nabla_{p}\mathcal{L}_{ij}\,^{p}-\mathcal{L}_{ip}\,^{q}\mathcal{L}_{jq}\,^{p}
, which vanishes, as commented above. This shows that
(\hat{D},[G])
is a naive Einstein AH structure. To show that it is Einstein there remains to verify the condition (
9.18
).

The one-form associated with
G
qua representative of the AH structure
(\hat{D},[G])
is
-2s(t+1)\beta_{I}
. Let
\hat{\mathit{s}}
be its scalar curvature. By (
10.12
) and (
10.24
),

(10.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}d\beta_{QI}&=G^{PQ}\widehat{\nabla}_{P}d\beta_{QI}-\Omega_{I}\,^{PQ}d\beta_{PQ}=G^{PQ}\rho^{\ast}(\nabla\omega)_{PQI}-\tfrac{(1+t)}{2}d\beta_{PQ}d\beta^{PQ}\beta_{I}\\
&=-\rho(\chi)_{I}-\tfrac{1+t}{2}\rho^{\ast}(|\omega|^{2})=-\tfrac{(1+t)n\alpha}{2}.\end{split}

Hence,

(10.46)

\displaystyle d\hat{\mathit{s}}_{I}-2s(t+1)\hat{\mathit{s}}\beta_{I}-2s(t+1)\tfrac{n+1}{2}G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}d\beta_{QI}=-2s(t+1)\left(\hat{\mathit{s}}+\tfrac{(1+t)(n+1)n\alpha}{4}\right)\beta_{I}.

When
t=0
,
\hat{\mathit{s}}=-n(n+1)\alpha/4
, and this yields
0
, completing the proof.
∎

Example 30
.

Theorem
10.5
recovers special cases of results of Calderbank-Pedersen-Swann constructing an Einstein-Weyl manifold on a circle bundle over a positive scalar curvature Kähler-Einstein manifold. On a
2n
-dimensional manifold
M
, let
\nabla
be the Levi-Civita connection of a Kähler-Einstein metric
h_{ij}
having nonzero scalar curvature
\mathscr{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
. Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle with principal connection
\beta
whose curvature
\omega_{ij}
is a constant multiple of the Kähler form. The assumptions imply
\nabla_{i}\omega_{jk}=0
,
\eta_{ij}=-\omega_{ij}
, and
C_{ijk}=0
, so, by Theorem
10.5
, the connections
\hat{D}
and the metric
G_{IJ}
on
N
defined by (
10.24
) and (
10.1
) for
t=0
and the two values of
s
as in (
10.39
) constitute a pair of Einstein-Weyl structures. The tensor
g_{ij}
of Theorem
10.5
equals
-\tfrac{1}{2n-1}\mathscr{R}h_{ij}
, so if
\mathscr{R}>0
then
G_{IJ}
is Riemannian, while if
\mathscr{R}<0
, then
G_{IJ}
has Lorentzian signature. In the case
\mathscr{R}>0
, this recovers the special case of
[
119
, Theorems
3.2
,
4.2
]
stated as
[
24
, Theorem
5.8
]
. Moreover, these references give examples of
S^{1}
-bundles to which the construction can be applied.

Example 31
.

Let
M
be an oriented, compact surface of genus
g\geq 2
and fix an integer
k
satisfying
|k|\leq 2(g-1)=|\chi(M)|
. Let
[\nabla]
be a convex flat real projective structure on
M
. It follows from a theorem of Cheng-Yau that
[\nabla]
is properly convex and there are a Riemannian metric
h
on
M
and a representative
\nabla\in[\nabla]
such that
\nabla_{[i}h_{j]k}=0
,
\nabla_{i}|\det h|=0
, and the Ricci curvature
R_{ij}
of
\nabla
satisfies
R_{ij}=\tfrac{c}{2}h_{ij}
for a negative constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
; see
[
57
, Theorem
7.3
]
and
[
99
,
101
]
. The Riemannian metric
g_{ij}=P_{(ij)}=-R_{(ij)}=-(c/2)h_{ij}
is homothetic to
h_{ij}
so satisfies
\nabla_{[i}g_{j]k}=0
and
\nabla_{i}|\det g|=0
. Let
J_{i}\,^{j}
be the complex structure determined by rotation by
\pi/2
with respect to the metric
g_{ij}
in the sense of the given orientation of
M
. The two-form defined by
\omega_{ij}=\tfrac{2\pi k}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)}J_{i}\,^{p}g_{pj}
is a constant multiple of the volume form determined by
g
and the given orientation of
M
and satisfies
\omega_{ip}\omega_{pj}g^{pq}=\alpha g_{ij}
where
\alpha=-\tfrac{4\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}
. Because
\nabla
preserves the volume form of
g
,
\nabla_{i}\omega_{jk}=0
. Because
\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\int_{M}\omega=k
is an integer, there is a principal
S^{1}
-bundle
\rho:N\to M
and a principal
S^{1}
-connection
\beta
on
N
such that
d\beta=\rho^{\ast}(\omega)

[
86
]
. Moreover
k
is the Euler number
e(N)
of
\rho:N\to M
. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the cone connection of the extended projective structure generated by
(\nabla,\beta)
and, as in (
10.1
) of Lemma
10.1
, define on
N
the Lorentz signature metric
G_{IJ}=\beta_{I}\beta_{J}-\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}=\beta_{I}\beta_{J}+\tfrac{c}{2}\rho^{\ast}(h)_{IJ}
and the connection
\hat{D}
as in (
10.24
) (with
t=0
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
). By Lemma
10.4
and Theorem
10.5
,

(10.47)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}&=-\tfrac{3}{2}s\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}+\tfrac{2\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}\beta_{I}\beta_{J}+\left(s^{2}-1+\tfrac{2\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}\right)\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}\\
&=-\tfrac{3}{2}s\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}+\tfrac{2\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}G_{IJ}+\left(s^{2}-1+\tfrac{4\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}\right)\rho^{\ast}(g)_{IJ}.\end{aligned}

By
[
57
, Theorem
7.3
]
,
\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)=-(c/2)\mathsf{vol}_{h}(M)\geq 4\pi(g-1)
, with equality if and only if
\nabla
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
. Hence, if
|k|\leq 2(g-1)
, then
\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}\geq 4\pi^{2}k^{2}
, so there is
s
solving
s^{2}=1-\tfrac{4\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}
, and
s
equals
0
if and only if
\nabla
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
. For each of the resulting roots
s=s^{\pm}
, by Theorem
10.5
, the resulting connection
\hat{D}
satisfies
\hat{D}_{I}G_{JK}=-2s\beta_{I}G_{JK}-\rho^{\ast}(\nabla g)_{IJK}
,
G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{I}G_{PQ}-3G^{PQ}\hat{D}_{P}G_{QI}=0
, and
\mathsf{Ric}(\hat{D})_{IJ}=-\tfrac{3}{2}s\rho^{\ast}(\omega)_{IJ}-\tfrac{2\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}G_{IJ}
and
(\hat{D},[G])
is an Einstein AH structure that is not closed and has scalar curvature
-\tfrac{3\pi^{2}k^{2}}{2\mathsf{vol}_{g}(M)^{2}}
.
The preceding proves:

Theorem 10.6
.

Let
M
be an oriented, compact surface of genus
g\geq 2
. Let
\rho:N\to M
be a principal
S^{1}
-bundle with Euler number
e(N)
satisfying
|e(N)|\leq 2(g-1)=-\chi(M)
. On
N
there are a Lorentzian signature metric
G
and a torsion-free affine connection
\hat{D}
which is the aligned representative of the AH structure
(\hat{D},[G])
it generates with
G
and having the following properties:

(1)

(\hat{D},[G])
is an Einstein AH structure.

(2)

The fibers of
\rho:N\to M
are timelike and
\hat{D}
-totally geodesic.

(3)

There is a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
such that
\rho:(N,G)\to(M,-g)
is a metric submersion.

(4)

The connection
\nabla
on
M
defined by
\nabla_{X}Y=T\rho(\hat{D}_{\hat{X}}\hat{Y})
, where
X\in\Gamma(TM)\to\hat{X}\in\Gamma(TN)
is the horizontal lift with respect to a principal connection on
\rho:N\to M
, generates a properly convex flat projective structure on
M
for which
g
is homothetic to the Cheng-Yau metric. The symmetric part of the Ricci curvature of
\nabla
is
-g
and
(\nabla,g)
is an Einstein special statistical structure with negative scalar curvature.

(5)

All properly convex flat projective structures on
M
arise in this way, and the resulting Einstein special statistical structure
(\nabla,g)
comprises a Riemannian metric of constant curvature and its Levi-Civita connection if and only if
e(N)=\pm\chi(M)
and
(\hat{D},[G])
is closed.

The condition on the Euler number is needed to construct
G
. It would be interesting to show that it is necessary.

11.
Left-invariant conelike radiant structures on Lie groups

A radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
on
M
is
homogeneous
if there is a Lie subgroup
G\subset\Aut(\nabla)
acting transitively on
M
and preserving
\mathbb{E}
. The simplest nontrivial examples of homogeneous radiant structures are left-invariant radiant structures on Lie groups. The main result of this section, Theorem
11.2
, shows that an invariant symmetric bilinear form
k
and an a
k
-anisotropic element
t
in the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of a Lie group
G
determine on
G
in a canonical way a left-invariant conelike radiant connection having antisymmetric Ricci tensor. In the particular case that
k
is the Killing form, Theorem
11.4
shows that the construction is equivariant with respect to the natural actions of
\Aut(G)
on all the structures involved.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be the Lie algebra of a real Lie group
G
and, for
r\in\mathfrak{g}
, let
E^{r}_{g}=\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}g\exp(tr)
be the left-invariant vector field generated on
G
by
r
.
A radiant structure
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
on
G
is
left-invariant
if
\nabla
and
\mathbb{E}
are left-invariant. In this case
\mathbb{E}=E^{t}
for some
t\in\mathfrak{g}
and so a left-invariant radiant structure is always nonsingular. For this reason, when discussing left-invariant radiant structures the qualifier
nonsingular
is omitted.

That
\nabla
be left-invariant implies there is
A\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
such that
\nabla_{E^{r}}E^{s}=E^{A(r,s)}
for all
r,s\in\mathfrak{g}
, and
\nabla
is torsion-free if and only if
A(r,s)-A(s,r)=[r,s]
for all
r,s\in\mathfrak{g}
. This means
A(r,s)=\Pi(r,s)+\tfrac{1}{2}[r,s]
for some
\Pi\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
. That
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
be a left-invariant radiant structure on
G
means that there is
t\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
\mathbb{E}=E^{t}
and
A(r,t)=r
for all
r\in\mathfrak{g}
. This implies that, for all
r\in\mathfrak{g}
,
A(t,r)=r+[t,r]
, so that
\Pi(t,r)=r+\tfrac{1}{2}[t,r]=\Pi(r,t)
. In abstract index notation,
A_{ij}\,^{k}=\Pi_{ij}\,^{k}+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{ij}\,^{k}
where
c_{ij}\,^{k}
is the structure tensor of
\mathfrak{g}
defined by
a^{i}c_{ij}\,^{k}=\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(a)_{j}\,^{k}
.

Let
\ell(a)=\mathsf{tr}\,\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(a)
be the one-form on
\mathfrak{g}
measuring the failure of unimodularity (so
\ell_{i}=c_{ip}\,^{p}
). Let
B_{\mathfrak{g}}(a,b)=\mathsf{tr}\,\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(a)\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(b)
be the Killing form of
\mathfrak{g}
(so
B_{ij}=c_{ip}\,^{q}c_{jq}\,^{p}
).

The adjoint action induces an action of
\mathfrak{g}
on any space of tensors on
\mathfrak{g}
. For example, for
a\in\mathfrak{g}
,
a\cdot B_{\mathfrak{g}}=0
, where, for
\Omega\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
, the action of
a
on
\Omega
is given by

(11.1)

\displaystyle(a\cdot\Omega)(b,c)=[a,\Omega(b,c)]-\Omega([a,b],c)-\Omega(b,[a,c]).

Since the curvature of a left-invariant radiant structure is left-invariant, there is
\mathsf{r}\in\otimes^{3}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
defined by
R(E^{a},E^{b})E^{c}=E^{\mathsf{r}(a,b)c}
. By definition,

(11.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathsf{r}_{ijk}\,^{l}&=2A_{[i|p|}\,^{l}A_{j]k}\,^{p}-c_{ij}\,^{p}A_{pk}\,^{l}=2\Pi_{p[i}\,^{l}\Pi_{j]k}\,^{p}-c_{p[i}\,^{l}\Pi_{j]k}\,^{p}+\Pi_{p[i}\,^{l}c_{j]k}\,^{p}-c_{ij}\,^{p}\Pi_{pk}\,^{l}-\tfrac{1}{4}c_{ij}\,^{p}c_{pk}\,^{l}.\end{split}

Equivalently,

(11.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(t,r)s=[t,\Pi(r,s)]-\Pi([t,r],s)-\Pi(r,[t,s])=(t\cdot\Pi)(r,s)

for all
r,s\in\mathfrak{g}
. Write
\mathsf{Ric}(E^{a},E^{b})=\mathsf{ric}(a,b)
for
a,b\in\mathfrak{g}
and
\rho(a)=\mathsf{ric}(t,a)
. Contracting (
11.2
) in
il
yields

(11.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{(jk)}=\Pi_{pq}\,^{q}\Pi_{jk}\,^{p}-\Pi_{jp}\,^{q}\Pi_{kq}\,^{p}-\tfrac{1}{2}\ell_{p}\Pi_{jk}\,^{p}-c_{p(j}\,^{q}\Pi_{k)q}\,^{p}-\tfrac{1}{4}B_{jk},

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{[jk]}=\tfrac{1}{2}c_{jk}\,^{p}\Pi_{pq}\,^{q}.

Suppose
n=\dim\mathfrak{g}>2
. By Lemma
5.2
, a left-invariant radiant structure is conelike if and only if there is
Q\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
such that
nQ(t,r)=\rho(r)
,
Q(t,t)=0
, and

(11.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(t\cdot\Pi)(r,s)&=\mathsf{r}(t,r)s=Q(r,s)t-Q(t,r)s-Q(t,s)r=Q(r,s)t-\tfrac{1}{n}\rho(r)s-\tfrac{1}{n}\rho(s)r,\end{split}

for all
r,s\in\mathfrak{g}
, and, in this case, there holds

(11.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(n-2)Q(r,s)&-Q([t,r],s)-Q(r,[t,s])\\
&=\tfrac{n-2}{n}\rho(\Pi(r,s))+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathsf{ric}([t,r],s)+\mathsf{ric}(r,[t,s])+\mathsf{ric}([t,s],r)+\mathsf{ric}(s,[t,r])\right),\end{split}

for all
r,s\in\mathfrak{g}
, where there has been used
\mathsf{ric}(\,\cdot\,\,,t)=0
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Suppose
n>2
and
G
is a
n
-dimensional Lie group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. If the radiant vector field of a left-invariant conelike radiant structure on
G
is generated by
t\in\mathfrak{g}
, then
\rho([t,r])=0
for all
r\in\mathfrak{g}
.

Proof.

Taking
s=t
in (
11.6
) yields

(11.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tfrac{n-2}{n}\rho(t)-\tfrac{1}{n}\rho([t,r])&=(n-2)Q(r,t)-Q([t,r],t)-Q(r,[t,t])\\
&=\tfrac{n-2}{n}\rho(\Pi(r,t))+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathsf{ric}([t,r],t)+\mathsf{ric}(r,[t,t])+\mathsf{ric}([t,t],r)+\mathsf{ric}(t,[t,r])\right)\\
&=\tfrac{n-2}{n}\rho(r)+\tfrac{n-2}{2n}\rho([t,r])+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho([t,r])=\tfrac{n-2}{n}\rho(r)+\tfrac{n-1}{n}\rho([t,r]),\end{split}

which, because
n>2
, implies that
\rho([t,r])=0
for all
r\in\mathfrak{g}
.
∎

Theorem 11.2
.

Suppose
n>2
and let
G
be an
n
-dimensional Lie group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and Killing form
B_{\mathfrak{g}}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
. Suppose
k\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
is invariant, meaning that
a\cdot k=0
for
a\in\mathfrak{g}
and suppose there is
t\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
k(t,t)\neq 0
. Define
h\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
by
h(r,s)=k(t,t)^{-1}k(r,s)
. Define
\Pi\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
by

(11.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi(r,s)&=-h(t,r)h(t,s)t+h(t,r)s+h(t,s)r+\tfrac{1}{2}h(t,r)[t,s]+\tfrac{1}{2}h(t,s)[t,r]\\
&+\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)h(t,r)h(t,s)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,r)h(t,s)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,s)h(t,r)-2h([t,r],[t,s])\right)t.\end{split}

The left-invariant connection
\nabla
determined by
A(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)=\Pi(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)+\tfrac{1}{2}[\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,]\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
constitutes with the left-invariant vector field
E^{t}
a left-invariant conelike radiant structure with antisymmetric Ricci tensor

(11.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathsf{ric}(r,s)=\tfrac{2n+\ell(t)}{4}h(t,[r,s]),\end{split}

and satisfying
\rho=0
.

(1)

For
g\in G
, the left-invariant conelike radiant connection
\nabla
associated with
\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g^{-1})t
is
\operatorname{Ad}(g)^{\ast}(\nabla)
. In particular the stabilizer of
t
in
\operatorname{Ad}(G)
acts as automorphisms of
(\nabla,E^{t})
.

(2)

(\nabla,E^{t})
admits a complete set of planes.

(3)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is semisimple, then
\nabla
is not Ricci-flat.

(4)

A subgroup
H\subset G
tangent to
t
is
\nabla
totally geodesic, and the connection induced on
H
by
\nabla
constitutes with the left-invariant vector field
E^{t}
a left-invariant conelike radiant structure on
H
.

(5)

Suppose
H\subset G
is an abelian subgroup tangent to
t
, having Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
, and such that
\dim H\geq 3
. The left-invariant connection
\tilde{\nabla}
on
H
associated with the tensor
\tilde{\Pi}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{h}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{h}
defined by

(11.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}(a,b)=h(t,a)b+h(t,b)a-h(t,a)h(t,b)t,

constitutes with
E^{t}
a Ricci-flat left-invariant conelike radiant structure on
H
having the same planelike surfaces as the connection induced on
H
by
\nabla
.

Proof.

Define
\bar{\Pi}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
by

(11.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\Pi}(r,s)&=-h(t,r)h(t,s)t+h(t,r)s+h(t,s)r+\tfrac{1}{2}h(t,r)[t,s]+\tfrac{1}{2}h(t,s)[t,r].\end{split}

By definition
\bar{\Pi}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\bar{\nabla}
be the associated left-invariant connection on
G
. Taking
s=t
yields
\bar{\Pi}(r,t)=r+\tfrac{1}{2}[t,r]
, so
\bar{\nabla}
forms with
E^{t}
a left-invariant radiant structure. As
\bar{\Pi}
is constructed from tensors invariant under the action induced by
\operatorname{ad}(t)
, it is also invariant under the action induced by
\operatorname{ad}(t)
. That is
t\cdot\Pi=0
. By (
11.3
) this shows the curvature
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)
of
\bar{\nabla}
satisfies
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(t,r)s=0
, and, by Lemma
5.2
, this shows that
(\bar{\nabla},E^{t})
is a conelike left-invariant radiant structure.

In calculating the Ricci curvature of
\bar{\nabla}
it is convenient to use abstract indices and rewrite the definition (
11.11
) as

(11.12)

\displaystyle\bar{\Pi}_{ij}\,^{k}

\displaystyle=-t_{i}t_{j}t^{k}+2t_{(i}\delta_{j)}\,^{k}+t_{(i}A_{j)}\,^{k},

where
t_{i}=t^{p}h_{ip}
and
A_{i}\,^{j}=\operatorname{ad}(t)_{i}\,^{j}
. By definition
t_{p}t^{p}=1
,
t^{p}A_{p}\,^{i}=0
,
A_{i}\,^{p}\ell_{p}=0
, and
A_{p}\,^{p}=\ell_{p}t^{p}
. From the invariance of
h
there follow
A_{i}\,^{p}t_{p}=0
. In what follows these observations are used without further comment.
From (
11.12
) there follow

(11.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\Pi}_{ij}\,^{p}t_{p}&=t_{i}t_{j},\qquad\bar{\Pi}_{ip}\,^{p}=(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(t))t_{i},\qquad\bar{\Pi}_{ij}\,^{p}\ell_{p}=-\ell(t)t_{i}t_{j}+2t_{(i}\ell_{j)},\\
\bar{\Pi}_{ip}\,^{q}\bar{\Pi}_{jq}\,^{p}&=\left(n+\ell(t)+\tfrac{1}{4}B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)\right)t_{i}t_{j},\qquad c_{p(i}\,^{q}\bar{\Pi}_{j)q}\,^{p}=-\ell_{(i}t_{j)}-\tfrac{1}{2}t^{p}B_{p(i}t_{j)}-\tfrac{1}{2}A_{i}\,^{p}A_{j}\,^{q}h_{pq}.\end{split}

Substituting (
11.13
) in (
11.4
) yields

(11.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\mathsf{r}}_{(ij)}&=-\tfrac{1}{4}B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)t_{i}t_{j}-\tfrac{1}{4}B_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{2}t^{p}B_{p(i}t_{j)}+\tfrac{1}{2}A_{i}\,^{p}A_{j}\,^{q}h_{pq},\\
\bar{\mathsf{r}}_{[ij]}&=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(t)\right)c_{ij}\,^{p}t_{p}=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(t)\right)A_{i}\,^{p}h_{pj}.\end{split}

This shows that the left-invariant connection
\bar{\nabla}
determined by
\bar{A}(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)=\bar{\Pi}(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)+\tfrac{1}{2}[\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,]\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
constitutes with
E^{t}
a left-invariant conelike radiant structure with Ricci curvature

(11.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\mathsf{ric}}(r,s)&=\tfrac{1}{4}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,r)h(t,s)+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,s)h(t,r)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)h(t,r)h(t,s)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)+2h([t,r],[t,s])\right)\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{2n+\ell(t)}{2}h(t,[r,s]).\end{split}

By the proof of Theorem
5.6
,
\Pi(r,s)+\tfrac{1}{2}[r,s]=A(r,s)=\bar{A}(r,s)-\tfrac{1}{2(n-2)}(\bar{\mathsf{ric}}(r,s)+\bar{\mathsf{ric}}(s,t))t
yields a left-invariant connection
\nabla
that constitutes with
E^{t}
a conelike radiant structure having antisymmetric Ricci tensor equal to the antisymmetric part of the Ricci tensor of
\bar{\nabla}
(Because
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(t,r)s=0
, the tensor
T_{ij}
in the proof of Theorem
5.6
is identically
0
). The resulting tensor
\Pi
is as in (
11.11
) and the Ricci curvature of
\nabla
is given by (
11.9
). Because
\mathsf{ric}(t,\,\cdot\,\,)=0
,
\rho=0
.

For
g\in G
, let
\bar{\nabla}
be the connection associated with
\bar{\Pi}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
determined by
\bar{t}=\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g^{-1})t
as in (
11.8
). Because
\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g)
is an automorphism of
\mathfrak{g}
,
\bar{\Pi}(r,s)=\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g^{-1})\Pi(\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g)r,\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(g)s)
, which is the tensor determined by the left-invariant connection
\operatorname{Ad}(g)^{\ast}(\nabla)
. This shows (
1
). By (
1
),
\nabla
is
E^{t}
-invariant, so, by Corollary
5.3
,
(\nabla,E^{t})
admits a complete set of planes.

If
G
is an
n
-dimensional semisimple real Lie group, then, by the Jacobson-Morozov theorem, its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
contains an
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})
-triple
\{t,r,s\}
such that
[t,r]=2r
,
[t,s]=-2s
, and
[r,s]=t
. By (
11.25
),
\mathsf{ric}(r,s)=\tfrac{2n+\ell(t)}{4}h(t,[r,s])=\tfrac{n}{2}h(t,t)=\tfrac{n}{2}
. In particular,
\nabla
is not flat. This shows (
3
).

Suppose
H\subset G
is a subgroup with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
containing
t
. For
r,s\in\mathfrak{h}
it follows from (
11.8
) that
A(r,s)\in\mathfrak{h}
, so
H
is totally geodesic. Consequently,
A
determines
\bar{A}\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{h}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{h}
defined by
\bar{A}(a,b)=A(a,b)
, and the left-invariant connection
\bar{\nabla}
on
H
determined by
\bar{A}
equals the connection induced on
H
by
\nabla
. Because
\bar{A}(a,t)=a
,
\bar{\nabla}
is radiant. By (
11.2
), its curvature
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(a,b)c
equals
\mathsf{r}(a,b)c
for
a,b,c\in\mathfrak{h}
, and so
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(t,a)b=\mathsf{r}(t,a)b=0
for
a,b\in\mathfrak{h}
, which by Lemma
5.2
suffices to show that the left-invariant radiant structure
(\bar{\nabla},\mathbb{E})
on
H
is conelike. This proves (
4
).

Suppose
t\in\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{h}
normalizes
t
. This means there is
\sigma\in\mathfrak{h}^{\ast}
such that
[a,t]=\sigma(a)t
for
a\in\mathfrak{g}
. By the invariance of
g
,
0=g(a,[t,t])=g([a,t],t)=-\sigma(a)g(t,t)
, so
\sigma=0
and
\mathfrak{h}
centralizes
t
.
By (
11.24
), for
a,b\in\mathfrak{h}
,

(11.16)

\displaystyle\Pi(a,b)=Q(a,b)t+S(a)b+S(b)a\in\mathfrak{h},

where
S(a)=h(t,a)
,
S(t)=1
,
Q(a,t)=-S(a)
, and

(11.17)

\displaystyle Q(a,b)

\displaystyle=-h(t,a)h(t,b)+\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(a,b)+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)h(t,a)h(t,b)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,a)h(t,b)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,b)h(t,a)\right).

The connection
\bar{\nabla}
induced on
H
by
\nabla
is is a left-invariant connection on
H
, and, because
H
is totally geodesic, its curvature
\bar{\mathsf{r}}(b,c)a
equals
\mathsf{r}(b,c)a
.

Next, suppose that
\mathfrak{h}
is moreover abelian, so that
A(a,b)=\Pi(a,b)
for
a,b\in\mathfrak{h}
.
From (
11.2
) and (
11.16
) it follows that

(11.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\mathsf{r}}(b,c)a&=\mathsf{r}(b,c)a=\Pi(b,\Pi(c,a))-\Pi(c,\Pi(b,a))\\
&=(Q(a,c)+S(a)S(c))b-(Q(a,b)+S(a)S(b))c+(Q(a,b)S(c)-Q(a,c)S(b))t\in\mathfrak{h},\end{split}

for all
a,b,c\in\mathfrak{h}
. Temporarily using abstract indices to indicate tensors on
\mathfrak{h}
, rewrite (
11.18
) as

(11.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{\mathsf{r}}_{ijk}\,^{l}&=(Q_{jk}+S_{j}S_{k})\delta_{i}\,^{l}-(Q_{ik}+S_{i}S_{k})\delta_{j}\,^{l}+(S_{j}Q_{ik}-S_{i}Q_{jk})t^{l}\\
&=(Q_{jk}+S_{j}S_{k})(\delta_{i}\,^{l}-S_{i}t^{l})-(Q_{ik}+S_{i}S_{k})(\delta_{j}\,^{l}-S_{j}t^{l}).\end{split}

Let
m=\dim\mathfrak{h}
. Tracing (
11.19
) in
il
yields

(11.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{\mathsf{ric}}_{jk}&=\bar{\mathsf{r}}_{pjk}\,^{p}=(m-2)(Q_{jk}+S_{j}S_{k}),\end{split}

so that

(11.21)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{ric}}(a,b)=\tfrac{m-2}{4(n-2)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(a,b)+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)h(t,a)h(t,b)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,a)h(t,b)-B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,b)h(t,a)\right),

for
a,b\in\mathfrak{h}
. In particular
\overline{\mathsf{ric}}(t,a)=0
, so
\bar{\rho}=0
for
\bar{\nabla}
. If
m\geq 3
, by the proof of Theorem
5.6
, the left-invariant connection
\tilde{\nabla}
on
H
associated with the tensor

(11.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\Pi}(a,b)&=\Pi(a,b)-\tfrac{1}{m-2}\overline{\mathsf{ric}}(a,b)t=S(a)b+S(b)a-S(a)S(b)t=h(t,a)b+h(t,b)a-h(t,a)h(t,b)t,\end{split}

(which is as in (
11.10
)) constitutes with
E^{t}
a left-invariant conelike radiant structure on
H
with Ricci curvature equal to the antisymmetric part of
\overline{\mathsf{ric}}
, so with vanishing Ricci curvature, and having the same planelike surfaces as the connection
\bar{\nabla}
induced on
H
by
\nabla
. This proves (
5
).
∎

Remark 27
.

The formula (
11.8
) is simpler than it appears. For
r,s\in\ker k(t,\,\cdot\,\,)
it simplifies to

(11.23)

\displaystyle\Pi(t,t)=t,

\displaystyle\Pi(r,t)=\Pi(t,r)=r+\tfrac{1}{2}[t,r],

\displaystyle\Pi(r,s)=\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)-\tfrac{2}{k(t,t)}k([t,r],[t,s])\right)t.

The utility of the formulation of Theorem
11.2
is that it can be applied even to a Lie group
G
for which the Killing form is identically zero (such as a nilpotent Lie group), but with some other invariant tensor
k
. A Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
equipped with an invariant nondegenerate bilinear form
k
is said to be a
quadratic Lie algebra
. There are many examples of nilpotent quadratic Lie algebras, and for such the Killing form is identically zero, but Theorem
11.2
still applies. In particular there holds the following.

Corollary 11.3
.

A Lie group of dimension
n>2
whose Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is equipped with a structure of a quadratic Lie algebra admits a left-invariant conelike radiant structure.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
11.2
. If
k
is an invariant metric on
\mathfrak{g}
, its nondegeneracy implies there is
t\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
k(t,t)\neq 0
.
∎

On the other hand, for
G
for which the Killing form is not identically zero, the invariant tensor
k
in Theorem
11.2
can be taken to be a multiple of the Killing form and the statement of the theorem can be simplified and strengthened in certain respects.

Theorem 11.4
.

Suppose
n>2
and let
G
be an
n
-dimensional Lie group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and Killing form
B_{\mathfrak{g}}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
. Suppose there is
t\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)\neq 0
. Define
\Pi\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
by

(11.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi(r,s)&=\left(\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)-\tfrac{1}{B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}\left(\left(\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}+\tfrac{1}{B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}\right)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,r)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,s)+\tfrac{1}{2(n-2)}B_{\mathfrak{g}}([t,r],[t,s])\right)\right)t\\
&+\tfrac{1}{B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,r)s+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,s)r+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,r)[t,s]+B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,s)[t,r]\right)\right).\end{split}

The left-invariant connection
\nabla
determined by
A(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)=\Pi(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)+\tfrac{1}{2}[\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,]\in\otimes^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
constitutes with the left-invariant vector field
E^{t}
a left-invariant conelike radiant structure with antisymmetric Ricci tensor

(11.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathsf{ric}(r,s)=\tfrac{2n+\ell(t)}{4B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,[r,s]),\end{split}

and satisfying
\rho=0
.

(1)

If
\Phi\in\Aut(G)
and
\phi=T\Phi(e)\in\text{End}(\mathfrak{g})
is the corresponding Lie algebra automorphism, then the left-invariant conelike radiant connection
\nabla
associated with
\phi^{-1}(t)
is
\Phi^{\ast}(\nabla)
. In particular the stabilizer of
t
in
\Aut(G)
acts as automorphisms of
(\nabla,\mathbb{E})
.

(2)

Claims (
2
), (
3
), (
4
), and (
5
) of Theorem
11.2
hold as stated.

(3)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is solvable, then
\nabla
is Ricci-flat.

Proof.

Taking
k=B_{\mathfrak{g}}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}
(so that
h=B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)^{-1}B
) in Theorem
11.2
yields (
11.24
) and (
11.25
).
Let
\Phi\in\Aut(G)
be an automorphism of
G
and let
\phi=T\Phi(e)\in\text{End}(\mathfrak{g})
be the corresponding Lie algebra automorphism. Let
\bar{\nabla}
be the connection associated with
\bar{t}=\phi^{-1}(t)
by
\bar{\Pi}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
as in (
11.24
). Because
\phi
is an automorphism,
\bar{\Pi}(r,s)=\phi^{-1}(\Pi(\phi(r),\phi(s)))
, which is the tensor determined by the left-invariant connection
\Phi^{\ast}(\nabla)
.

By the Cartan criterion, over a field of characteristic zero a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is solvable if and only if
B_{\mathfrak{g}}([\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}],\mathfrak{g})=\{0\}
, so when
G
is solvable it follows from (
11.25
) that
\nabla
has vanishing Ricci tensor.
∎

Remark 28
.

Theorem
11.4
is essentially the special case of Theorem
11.2
where
k=B_{\mathfrak{g}}
, with the difference that claim (
1
) of Theorem
11.2
is improved by replacing the group of inner automorphisms
\operatorname{Ad}(G)
by the full automorphism group
\Aut(G)
. This is possible because any automorphism of
G
preserves the Killing form, while an arbitrary invariant bilinear form need only be preserved by inner automorphisms. In the context of nilpotent Lie groups, this improvement is decidedly nontrivial because, by
[
80
, Theorem
4
]
, a nilpotent Lie algebra admits a derivation which is not inner, so the group of outer automorphisms of a connected nilpotent Lie group has positive dimension. See
[
43
]
for a survey of automorphism groups of Lie groups.

Although claims (
4
) and (
5
) of Theorem
11.2
continue to hold without change, with respect to claim (
4
) note that the hypothesis
B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)\neq 0
need not imply that
B_{\mathfrak{h}}(t,t)\neq 0
, and even if this be the case, in general
\bar{\nabla}
need not be the left-invariant conelike radiant connection associated with
(H,t)
by (
11.24
).

Remark 29
.

The connection
\nabla
associated with
t\in\mathfrak{g}
by Theorem
11.4
depends only on the adjoint orbit of
t
, in the sense that the connection
\bar{\nabla}
associated with
\bar{t}=\operatorname{Ad}(g)t
equals the pullback of
\nabla
via
\operatorname{Ad}(g^{-1})
, for if
t
is used in place of
t
in (
11.24
), the resulting tensor
\bar{\Pi}
satisfies
\bar{\Pi}(a,b)=\operatorname{Ad}(g)\Pi(\operatorname{Ad}(g^{-1})a,\operatorname{Ad}(g^{-1})b)
. That is the left-invariant conelike radiant structures on
G
associated by Theorem
11.4
with elements of a Killing nondegenerate adjoint orbit are isomorphic via inner automorphisms of
G
.

Example 32
.

The formula (
11.24
) is simpler than it appears. For
r,s\in\ker B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,\,\cdot\,\,)
it simplifies to

(11.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi(t,t)&=t,\qquad\Pi(r,t)=\Pi(t,r)=r+\tfrac{1}{2}[t,r],\\
\Pi(r,s)&=\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)-\tfrac{2}{B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}B_{\mathfrak{g}}([t,r],[t,s])\right)t=\tfrac{1}{4(n-2)}B_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(r,\left(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\tfrac{2}{B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)^{2}\right)s\right).\end{split}

The expressions (
11.26
) give rise to the following example. Let
G
be an
n
-dimensional Lie group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
that is
|k|
-graded, meaning there is a direct sum of linear subspaces,
\mathfrak{g}=\oplus_{i=-k}^{k}\mathfrak{g}_{k}
, satisfying
[\mathfrak{g}_{i},\mathfrak{g}_{j}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{i+j}
, where
\mathfrak{g}_{p}=\{0\}
if
|p|>k
and
\mathfrak{g}_{k}\neq\{0\}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{-k}\neq\{0\}
. If
\mathfrak{g}
is semisimple as a Lie algebra there exists a unique
grading element

t\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)(r)=ir
for
r\in\mathfrak{g}_{i}
and
t
is contained in the center of
\mathfrak{g}_{0}

[
27
, Proposition
3.1.2
]
. If
r\in\mathfrak{g}_{i}
and
s\in\mathfrak{g}_{j}
, then
B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)=0
if
i+j\neq 0
, while the restriction of
B_{\mathfrak{g}}
to
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
yields a linear isomorphism of
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
with
\mathfrak{g}_{-i}^{\ast}
is nondegenerate. There holds
B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)=\sum_{i}i^{2}\dim\mathfrak{g}_{i}>0
. Let
\nabla
be the left-invariant connection on
G
associated with
t
by Theorem
11.2
. Specializing (
11.26
) yields, for
r\in\mathfrak{g}_{i}
and
s\in\mathfrak{g}_{j}
,

(11.27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Pi(t,t)=t,&&A(t,t)=t,\\
&\Pi(t,r)=(1+i/2)r,&&A(t,r)=(1+i)r,&\\
&&&A(r,t)=r\\
&\Pi(r,s)=\begin{cases}0&i+j\neq 0,\\
\tfrac{\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)+2i^{2}\right)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)}{4(n-2)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}t&i+j=0,\end{cases}&&A(r,s)=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{2}[r,s]&i+j\neq 0,\\
\tfrac{1}{2}[r,s]+\tfrac{\left(B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)+2i^{2}\right)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(r,s)}{4(n-2)B_{\mathfrak{g}}(t,t)}t&i+j=0.\end{cases}\end{aligned}

Over a field
\mathbb{k}
of characteristic not equal to
2
, let
(\mathbb{V},\omega)
be a
2n
-dimensional
\mathbb{k}
-vector space equipped with a structure of a symplectic vector space. The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{heis}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{k})
is
\mathbb{V}\oplus\mathbb{k}
equipped with the bracket
[(x,r),(y,s)]=(0,\omega(x,y))
. By Lemma
11.5
any
2
-step nilpotent Lie algebra with one-dimensional center is isomorphic to the Heisenberg Lie algebra.

Lemma 11.5
.

Over a field of characteristic not equal to
2
, there exists a
2
-step nilpotent Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}
with one-dimensional center
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
if and only if
\dim\mathfrak{n}
is odd, and such a Lie algebra is unique up to isomorphism.

Proof.

Choose a linear subspace
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathfrak{n}
such that
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
. Let
z
span
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
and define an antisymmetric two-form
\omega
on
\mathfrak{m}
by
[a,b]=\omega(a,b)z
. If
x\in\mathfrak{n}
and
x\notin\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
, then there is
y\in\mathfrak{n}
such that
[x,y]\neq 0
, and evidently
y\notin\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
. this shows that the restriction to
\mathfrak{m}
of
\omega
is nondegenerate. Since
(\mathfrak{m},\omega)
is a symplectic vector space, it has even dimension. On
\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{Z}(\mathfrak{n})
the bracket
[\,,\,]
has the form
[(a,rt),(b,st)]=(0,\omega(a,b)t)
. Since any two symplectic vector spaces are linearly isomorphic, the claim follows.
∎

The
(2n+1)
-dimensional Heisenberg group
\operatorname{Heis}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{k})
is the vector space
\mathbb{V}\oplus\mathbb{k}
equipped with the product
(x,r)\cdot(y,s)=(x+y,r+s+\tfrac{1}{2}\omega(x,y))
.
Example
33
shows that are many different structures of a left-invariant conelike radiant structure with antisymmetric Ricci tensor that can be constructed on the Heisenberg group. The simplest such is obtained from Theorem
11.2
with
g
being the tensor square of the contact one-form on the Heisenberg group.

Example 33
.

Let
(\mathbb{V},\omega)
be a
2n
-dimensional symplectic real vector space. Let
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{2n}\}
be a basis of
\mathbb{V}
and write
E_{i}=(e_{i},0)\in\mathfrak{heis}_{2n+1}
. The center of
\mathfrak{heis}_{2n+1}
is the one-dimensional subspace spanned by
Z=(0,1)
and it follows that subgroup generated by exponentiating
z
is normal and so
\operatorname{Heis}_{2n+1}
fibers over its quotient by this subgroup, which is
\mathbb{V}
viewed as an additive abelian group. The action of
\mathbb{R}^{\ast}
generating the fibers is free, so the fibration
\rho:\operatorname{Heis}_{2n+1}\to\mathbb{V}
given by
\rho(x,r)=x
is principal. Let
\theta
be the left invariant one-form such that
\theta(Z)=1
and
\{E_{1},\dots,E_{2n}\}
span
\ker\theta
. Since
Z
is central,
R^{\ast}_{\exp(tZ)}d\theta=d\theta
, so
\theta
is a principal connection on
\rho:\operatorname{Heis}_{2n+1}\to\mathbb{V}
, and since
d\theta=-\rho^{\ast}(\omega)
,
\theta
is a contact one-form and is not flat as a principal connection.

The most general left-invariant torsion-free connection,
\nabla
, on
\operatorname{Heis}_{2n+1}
such that
Z
is a radiant vector field satisfies

(11.28)

\displaystyle\nabla_{Z}Z=Z,

\displaystyle\nabla_{E_{i}}Z=E_{i},

\displaystyle\nabla_{Z}E_{i}=E_{i},

\displaystyle\nabla_{E_{i}}E_{j}=A_{ij}\,^{k}E_{k}+B_{ij}Z+\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{ij}Z,

for
A_{ij}\,^{k}\in S^{2}\mathbb{V}^{\ast}\otimes\mathbb{V}
and
B_{ij}\in S^{2}\mathbb{V}^{\ast}
. The curvature of
\nabla
satisfies

(11.29)

\displaystyle\begin{split}R(\,\cdot\,\,,\,\cdot\,\,)Z&=0,\quad R(Z,\,\cdot\,\,)\,\cdot\,\,=0,\\
R(E_{i},E_{j})E_{k}&=(2A_{p[i}\,^{l}A_{j]k}\,^{p}+2\delta_{[i}\,^{l}B_{j]k})E_{l}+\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{jk}E_{i}+\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{ki}E_{j}-\omega_{ij}E_{k}.\end{split}

Since
R(Z,\,\cdot\,\,)\,\cdot\,\,=0
, by Lemma
5.2
,
(\nabla,Z)
is conelike. Its Ricci curvature is

(11.30)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ric}(Z,\,\cdot\,\,)=0=\mathsf{Ric}(\,\cdot\,\,,Z),

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ric}(E_{i},E_{j})=A_{ij}\,^{p}A_{qp}\,^{q}-A_{qj}\,^{p}A_{ip}\,^{q}+(2n-1)B_{ij},

so that the Ricci tensor of the conelike radiant structure
(\nabla,Z)
is antisymmetric if and only if
B_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{2n-1}\left(A_{ip}\,^{q}A_{jq}\,^{p}-A_{ij}\,^{p}A_{qp}\,^{q}\right)
, in which case the Ricci tensor is identically zero.

Let
\bar{\nabla}
be the connection on
\mathbb{V}
such that
\bar{\nabla}_{e_{i}}e_{j}=A_{ij}\,^{k}e_{k}
. Then the Ricci tensor
\bar{\mathsf{Ric}}
of
\bar{\nabla}
satisfies
\bar{\mathsf{Ric}}(e_{i},e_{j})=A_{ij}\,^{p}A_{qp}\,^{q}-A_{ip}\,^{q}A_{jq}\,^{p}
, so that the condition that
\mathsf{Ric}
be antisymmetric is equivalent to
B_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{1-2n}\bar{\mathsf{Ric}}_{ij}
. From Lemma
6.6
it follows that
\nabla
